# Journal

Newest entry first. One entry per working session: what was done, what merged, what is open.

## 2026-10-23, bug sweep 1 (D105): the text layer that sometimes had no window

- **The fault:** CI's Debian 13 job failed once on TextLayerTest.test_show_text_is_drawn_and_goes ("no text on the picture", 2026-10-21). The text core of pvj-render (the second libmpv that plays nothing and draws overlays and show-text) needs a window to have an on-screen layer. mpv makes that window as the core first goes idle, a moment after `mpv_initialize`. mpv 0.41 also acts on a `force-window` turned on later; mpv 0.40 (Debian 13, the Pi) and 0.37 (Ubuntu 24.04) never do. #148 turned `force-window` on after the render context, so on those versions the text layer worked only when the core went idle after that; otherwise that process drew no text and no overlay for its whole life.
- **How it was found:** the local mpv is 0.41, where the fault cannot happen (0 of 140 runs under load). mpv's sources (0.40 against 0.41) showed the difference: 0.41 added `handle_force_window` to the option-change callback. A libmpv 0.41 built here with that one line taken out failed the test the CI way 1 of 30 runs and 1 of 120 under load (two cores, three CPU hogs); a probe in the failing run showed the text core's `vo-configured` still "no" 300 ms after `force-window` was turned on.
- **The fix (render/src/main.c):** the text core's render context is made before `mpv_initialize`, and its `force-window` is on from the start, so its window is made as it first goes idle, on every mpv. The video cores are unchanged (they get their window when a clip loads).
- **The test:** `test_the_text_layer_works_after_a_slow_start` starts pvj-render with `PVJ_RENDER_TEST_SLOW_START=500` (a test-only wait after the text core's `mpv_initialize`, so the core is surely idle first). Break-check on the 0.40-like libmpv: without the fix it fails 3 of 3, with it passes 3 of 3; on stock 0.41 both pass (so CI's Debian and Ubuntu jobs are the ones that guard it). Stress with the fix: 0 failures in 120 runs of the show-text test on two cores under three CPU hogs with the 0.40-like libmpv (master: 1 in 120 the same way).
- **Not tested:** the real mpv 0.40 and 0.37 (CI runs them), and the Pi. On the Pi the same start race can leave a New-renderer start with no masks and no PIN text until pvj-render restarts; not seen there, and not claimed.

## 2026-10-21, CI pinned to Ubuntu 24.04

- **What broke:** GitHub moved `ubuntu-latest` to Ubuntu 26.04 (runner image 20261012). On it, actions/setup-python has no Python 3.9 ("The version '3.9' with architecture 'x64' was not found for Ubuntu 26.04") and Playwright refuses chromium ("Playwright does not support chromium on ubuntu26.04-x64"). Every PR failed, a journal-only one (#159) included. (A "No space left on device" line in the logs is a test simulating a full disk on purpose, not a real one.)
- **The fix:** every GitHub-hosted job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` instead of `ubuntu-latest` (supported by GitHub until 2029). The self-hosted device job is unchanged. No check name changes, so the "Protect master" ruleset is untouched.
- **A flaky test fixed on the way:** tests/test_boxcare.py DiagnosticsTest.test_no_secret_is_in_it looked for the test PIN 4821 as plain text in each stream's diagnostics, and the runner's free disk (90882482176 bytes) held those four digits by chance. The first loop of that test already matched a PIN only as a number of its own; the second loop now does the same.
- **The NDI PipeReuseTest flake, found and fixed (test only, no product change):** it failed in 3 of the last 4 runs here (CI's 3.12 part 2 on 4ae974c: line 2282, `old.gone` not set after the open of B). The cause was the test's player, not the helper: it closed its end of the pipe and set `gone` one step later, and on a busy machine the helper's look could fall between the two, see the pipe without a reader (true), answer, and the test then read `gone` as not set. Evidence: with a check inside the helper's look, a run on two cores under 16 CPU hogs showed the look seeing the pipe free while `gone` was unset, in the run that failed; the unchanged class failed 1 of 40 runs that way (and 2 of 70 instrumented), and failed 5 of 5 with a 50 ms pause put between the close and the set. Fix: the close and the set are one step under `OldPlayer.LETTING_GO`, and PipeReuseTest's service takes the same lock around the helper's look (`_pipe_free`), so `gone` says exactly what the pipe said; the late-reader test also waits (`sent_all`) for A's last frame to be taken before it opens B, so a frame of A in the shared fake queue cannot be B's first (a hole found by reading, not seen to fail). After: 40 of 40 on two cores under 16 hogs, 5 of 5 with the 50 ms pause (now inside the lock), the whole `tests.test_ndi` (202 tests) passes. Break-check: with `_clear_pipe` made to answer at once, the next-source test and the late-reader test fail on their `gone` checks; the mirror test still passes then, because its old player leaves (0.3 s) before B's first frame is through, so its `gone` check does not guard the wait (as before this change).
- **Later:** move to 26.04 on purpose, once Playwright supports it and the Python matrix is reviewed (3.9 is not available there; the Pi's Debian 13 has 3.13). A dated follow-up, not urgent while 24.04 is supported.

## 2026-10-20, CI's unit tests in three parts (`ci-test-split`, D104)

- **The task:** the unit-test jobs used 1,535 to 1,731 of their 1,800 seconds; split or speed up, raise the limit only as a last resort. Started after #153 merged (it changed the same workflow).
- **Measured:** per module from master's run 39384981398 (its log lines are stamped): no single cause, the ten slowest modules about half. The GPU shader steps after the unit tests took another 14 minutes of the same 45-minute job.
- **Built:** `tools/ci-shard.py`, the `unit` job in three parts per Python, the `shaders-gpu` job, and `test (3.9)` and `test (3.12)` as checks over them, so the ruleset's required names stay. `tests/test_ci_shard.py` (new, 10 tests).
- **The review:** no High or Medium; three Lows. Fixed: the two checks over the parts cancelled each other on a failure (now `fail-fast: false`), and `tests/test_ndi_review.py` looked at the whole file from the unit job on, so the Debian job's fetch of master passed it; it now reads the unit job alone (break-checked: it fails with the unit job's fetch removed). Not taken: "test_000_tmp tests only discover"; its `test_named_modules_leave_nothing` runs named modules.
- **CI on 2f8647f:** each part took 8.5 to 11 minutes (the one step had taken 25 to 29). Part 3 failed on both Pythons: the new `tests/test_ci_shard.py` did not `import tests` at its top, which `test_lockrank` asks of every module; now it does. Part 2 failed once on 3.12 on the known NDI PipeReuseTest flake (passed in the other three runs).
- **Not done:** no test was made faster. The Debian job's whole suite for information already times out (2,104 s, exit 124); a follow-up.

## 2026-10-20, sources and surfaces, step 1 (`sources-surfaces`, D101)

- **The owner's words:** "start sources and surfaces, step 1: more clip layers and a source per surface on the new renderer."
- **Found first:** the mapping's two-layer show table (D58) had two spare channels in its second texture, and pvj-render's own reader of the mapping file (D95) could take a new BIND without anything of mpv's. So the source per surface rides in the table that exists, and pvj-render supplies the other pictures by name.
- **Built:** pvj-render `--sources=N` (1 to 8): sources 2 to N are one libmpv core each, muted, idle until loaded, with `pvj-source` (loadfile, stop, a short set and get list) and `get_property pvj-sources`; every older command still means source 1. `//!BIND SOURCEk` in the mapping file. A surface's `"source"` (no key for 1), the table's source per layer read at the nearest cell, both shaders, classic byte for byte as before. The renderer's number of sources per board (a Pi 4 has 4). `POST /api/play` with `"source"`, `GET`/`POST /api/sources`, the warning about 1080p clips. The panel: Shows on the chosen surface, the Sources strip on the mapping page, Play to above the pads.
- **Chosen:** a shader, NDI or a render cue sent to Source 2 and up is refused in plain words rather than played on Source 1; the level (opacity, Blackout) reaches every source; Stop stays Source 1's.
- **The owner's note, the same day:** a mixing stage for later A/B decks, a manual crossfader and blend modes. Each source now has an opacity and a blend (normal only; the others refused), set and read over the socket and read once a frame; the crossfade of D98 is that stage with the opacity moved by the clock, its tests unchanged. D101 says how decks build on it.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_pvj_render.py` 64 tests, OK with `PVJ_RENDER_TEST=1` and no skips (sources, the capture per source, opacity, idle cost, stop, refusals, sound, 270 loads with descriptors 9, threads 110 and GL objects 27 flat, a crossfade on source 1 while source 2 plays); `tests/test_sources.py` (new, 21); the related suites (mapper, mapping mode, player, layers, transitions, server, roles, masks, renderer, settings, boxcare: 565 tests) OK.
- **Break-checks (21), each caught:** a load on source 2 going to source 1; the final pass reading source 1 for SOURCEk; sources keeping their sound; sources converted at every vblank; the level not reaching sources; any name settable on a source; source numbers not bounded; a descriptor leaked per load; BIND SOURCEk not taken; source 1 stored as a key; a source the player lacks not drawn from source 1; the layer under taking the top's source; the editing shader reading source 1 everywhere; the engine never asking the player's sources; a shader pad going to a source; a guest playing to a source; the warning needing three others; `play_to` not looping; `play_to` taking any number (first not caught, see LESSONS; caught after the test asked for the player's own words); the opacity not reaching the final pass.
- **Not run here:** `tests/ui/panel.test.js` (its Chromium needs libnss3 and libnspr4, which this WSL has not; CI runs it on the push).
- **Not done:** anything on the Pi, a real GPU or the X27 (rows SO1 to SO8); a crossfade, effects and sound on sources 2 to N; shaders as sources (step 2); the full sources panel (step 3).

## 2026-10-20, the review of #152 (masks, D100 amendment)

- **The review:** one Medium and one Low, both fixed on `mask-list`. Medium: a 995 KB PNG that says 16000 x 16000 made mpv use about 1.2 GB per copy. Now the PNG header is read first (8192 a side, 40 megapixels at most, in the single-picture path too), the masks combined at once are bounded (70 megapixels of their own, and 4 x 3840 x 2160 of the screen's: 8 masks at 1080, 4 at 4K), and mpv runs under a 2 GB address-space cap with 1 MB thread stacks and 2 malloc arenas. The 1.5 GB cap asked for did not let mpv start even one mask on this 16-core laptop; 2 GB with the smaller stacks fits every allowed case (the heaviest 2.7 s, about 1.05 GB resident). Low: `set_overlay` now holds the settings' lock from the read to the write, so two Adds at once both land.
- **Tests:** 7 new (`SizeTest`: the header, both paths checked before mpv starts, the bounds, the cap seen by the child, the real mpv under the cap at 4K; `BudgetWorkerTest`; `ConcurrentAddTest`). 9 new breaks, each caught. Two older tests now write real PNGs (their 8-byte stand-ins have no header).
- **Not done:** the cap on the Pi's own mpv (row MK10).

## 2026-10-18, the review of #148 (pvj-render phase 2, D98 addendum)

- **The review:** one Medium and four Lows, all fixed on `render-layers`. The Medium: a crossfade out of a generator or a live input became a cut on the new renderer, where classic and phase 1 fade from D71's still. Now the renderer's own crossfade is taken only from a plain clip (`Player.fades_from_clip`), and everything else goes D71's way as before; the status shows the still's reason in that case. The Lows: the outgoing clip keeps its last frame if it ends during a fade (`keep-open=yes` on it at the tap); a crossfade with nothing on the screen is a cut into the front layer; the render thread reads the fade's layer, phase and clock in one look; the leak test counts descriptors, threads and GL objects at rest and allows 2, and runs 30 fades each cut short by the next. The sound is handed over in order: incoming loaded silent, outgoing sound off and its output closed (bounded wait of 1 s, logged and counted as `sound-held` if not), then the incoming sound on.
- **Tests:** `FadeEdgesTest` (3), the stricter `ManyFadesTest` (8 descriptors, 81 threads, 38 GL objects at the start, after 200 fades and after 30 cut short), and the changed rows of `LayeredCrossfade` (out of a generator and out of a live input: the still, run to its end; the status). Breaks caught: the plain-clip check (three rows), keep-open on the outgoing layer, the cut with nothing on the screen, the incoming sound, a descriptor leaked in `end_fade`, a libmpv core leaked per fade. The snapshot race is fixed but not break-checked (no test can hit a window of microseconds on purpose).
- **Found, not fixed (open):** in a fresh headless pvj-render a clip is often drawn black for its whole run (five starts of six in one trial; a picture is fine, and a clip is fine once a picture has been shown in that process). Master's phase 1 build does the same, and no phase 1 test judged a clip by its pixels. To be looked at on its own, and on the Pi (R4 should show it if it happens there).
- **CI's PaceTest** (63 to 64 flips a second on 60, all four pvj-render jobs on 31fc88a): the `pvj-render` property asked the idle libmpv core for `idle-active` after taking the counters, so one late answer stretched the window. It now asks no core (the idleness is observed by each layer's event thread). Not reproduced on this machine's mpv 0.41 (0.3 ms an answer; PaceTest 9 times OK, 3 on 2 pinned cores): CI's next run is the check.
- **The sound test** uses ALSA's null device, so a real output is open during the handover; both sound breaks caught. WSLg's own sound server once held a core for over 10 seconds ("no reply"), which is why it is not used.
- **The text layer drew nothing in some starts** (an overlay never shown; master 1 start in 30, here 4 in 30): the cores were started with `force-window=yes` before their render context existed. It is now turned on after the render context is made: 0 in 60. The clip drawn black in a fresh process (above) is something else: unchanged by this fix, still open.
- **CI's Debian 13 leak test** (da0808a: GL objects 39 then 42 after 30 fades cut short, over a slack of 2): over 600 fades cut short here the count moved between 37 and 41, down as well as up, with descriptors and threads flat: a pool, not a leak. The test now compares later checkpoints with the highest of three warm-up readings (band 4); a leak of one object a fade, one in ten, and one in ten only when cut short all fail it.
- **CI's Debian 13 leak test again** (6cadfe7: 41 GL objects after the 200 whole fades, over a slack of 2 from 38): the same pool band, seen on whole fades too. The 200-fade check now uses the band (4 over the start reading) as the cut-short checkpoints do; a leak of one object every ten fades still ends 20 over. Descriptors and threads keep their slack of 2.
- **Lost and redone:** a cleanup line in a test script ran `git checkout` on a test file with uncommitted edits; the edits were made again. Commit before any script that may restore files.
- **Not done:** the optional `playlist_entry_id` match for the start of a fade (not needed for the panel, which never sends loads faster than their events).

## 2026-10-18, pvj-render phase 2: two layers and real crossfades (`render-layers`, D98)

- **The owner's words:** "yes, start phase 2 of the renderer. let's give it real crossfades across two layers."
- **Built:** a second libmpv core and render context in pvj-render, idle (no file, never drawn) until a crossfade; `["pvj-crossfade", URL, SECONDS, OPTIONS]` loads the clip into the idle layer, which the socket speaks to from that moment, and the render thread mixes the two pictures by real opacity at the internal size once a vblank, from the clip's first frame, in a straight line over the seconds, then stops the outgoing layer. The final pass (mapping or scale) is unchanged and takes the mix. How the picture looks (brightness, zoom, pan, rotation, crop, flips, effects) is set on both layers, so a Blackout during a fade darkens both. A load, a stop, a step in the list or a new crossfade ends a fade at once. On the panel's side `Player.crossfades()` asks once per player process, and `Api.play` hands the Mix's crossfade to the renderer instead of taking D71's still; out of a generator or a live input, into a list, on a refusal, and on classic it is the cut or the still as before.
- **Tests:** 15 new rows in `tests/test_pvj_render.py` against the real program (the mix by pixel, the swap, the answers after it, the straight line, the refusals, the idle layer, 60 flips a second during a fade, 220 fades with no file descriptor, fence or GL object left behind: 9 descriptors before and after) and 8 in `tests/test_transitions.py` (`LayeredCrossfade`). 16 breaks on purpose, each caught. `tests.test_pvj_render`, `tests.test_renderer`, `tests.test_player`, `tests.test_transitions` and `tests.test_server`: OK here (WSL, mpv 0.41, llvmpipe).
- **Open:** everything on the Pi (DEVICE-TESTING CF1 to CF8): flips and missed vblanks during a fade at each detail, the GPU's share, the sound device handed from one core to the other, the sync files after 200 fades. Wipes and slides on the new renderer, a fade out of a generator or a live input, and a crossfade of the sound are not built.

## 2026-10-18, pvj-render: the faults of its first Pi run (`render-fixes`, D95)

- **The run** (test Pi 4, Debian 13, mpv 0.40, master 9ca9cbf, New with detail Automatic): a clip played, then the socket jammed (a probe's connect: `EAGAIN`, the listen queue full; the panel's plays 503), the journal had 18,046 lines of `MESA: error: Export failed` in about 6 minutes, the screen flipped only on new clip frames (10,035 flips, 10,032 frames drawn in 5.7 minutes: 30 a second on 60 Hz), and Classic again did not take until `systemctl restart pvj-player`.
- **The cause of the first, second and fourth: one leak.** Read on the Pi under gdb with Debian's debug symbols: two sync-file exports a frame, one from Mesa's own swap (freed), one from `ra_gl_ctx_submit_frame` in libmpv. mpv 0.40 and 0.41 make a GL fence at the end of every render-API frame and delete it only in a swap that the render API never calls (mpv's own fix, f74adc4 "require swap_buffers param for FenceSync", is not in any release). On V3D each GL fence holds a sync file (`v3d_pipe_flush`, src/gallium/drivers/v3d/v3d_context.c, Mesa 26.2.2: `drmSyncobjExportSyncFile`, then `mesa_loge("Export failed")` when it fails). The helper had 549 sync files open after 18 s of master's build; at the limit of 1024 every export failed, and so did `accept()` (`EMFILE`): the one socket thread turned on the readable listen socket without taking anyone, the queue filled, and the panel's quit for the switch never arrived.
- **Fixed, each with a test seen to fail without it:** the GL functions libmpv asks this program for are wrapped, and the fence it made last in a render (the frame's) is deleted (`FenceTest`: 61 fences left after 61 frames without the deletion, at most 4 with it). The socket: a thread per client (at most 32), its own buffer, a client that takes no answer for 2 s or sends nothing for 30 s dropped, a pause on `EMFILE`; every bound of the parser as before (`SocketTest`: a client that never reads, 12 silent ones, 16 at once; master's helper fails those three; the fourth, the bound of 32, it met with its bound of 8). A picture at every vblank with the last clip frame reused, one clip conversion a vblank at most, the next picture drawn while the flip is pending (`PaceTest`: master's helper flips 0.5 a second idle and 30 with a 30 fps clip; now 60 and 60 with 30 clip frames). The supervisor reads the wish every half second while pvj-render draws and ends it itself on a change: asked on the socket, SIGTERM after 2 s, SIGKILL after 3 s more; systemd's stop kills it after 5 s; how it ended goes to the report and the Renderer card (`SwitchTest` with stand-ins that never answer, take no quit, or answer; four fail with the watch taken out).
- **Found on the way:** a Stop could leave the last picture on the screen (libmpv asks for no frame when its video ends; `test_stop_gives_black_and_idle` had passed on timing). The video layer is drawn again at the end of a file and a change of its output.
- **By hand on the Pi with this branch** (stopped player, detail 540, Lumia 1 looped, 60 s, a client flooding without reading and 12 silent ones for 20 s of it): 60.00 flips a second, 29.87 clip frames, 0 missed vblanks, 0 frames dropped, 0 GL fences left (1945 deleted), file descriptors 15 to 28 with one sync file, 0 `Export failed`, the slowest of 393 other requests 43 ms, the flooding client dropped after 2 s.
- **"pw.conf: can't load config client.conf":** libpipewire's, from mpv's sound output trying PipeWire first (the box runs no PipeWire) before ALSA. Classic mpv prints the same two lines at each start. Harmless; the sound output is chosen the same way for both, the panel's `audio-device` after each start.

## 2026-10-18, sound-reactivity of the box's own (`sound`, #145, D96)

- **The owner's words (2026-10-16):** "Yes, it's worthwhile to add sound-reactivity to the Pi and Mastercontrol overall." RNBO stays an optional later source; this is the box's own. Design first (6f1a5e6), then the code.
- **Built:** `pvj-sound`, its own unit and account (group `audio` only, no network at all, `CPUQuota=25%`), running `arecord` on a capture device chosen from `/proc/asound` (always `plughw:CARD=<id>,DEV=<n>`, never a free ALSA string) and analysing 20 ms blocks in pure Python at 16 kHz: RMS dB, an envelope, onsets, beats and a rough tempo, six bands from a half-size real FFT. A 128-byte levels file in `/run/pvj-sound` at 50 Hz with a fixed layout (for the panel now and pvj-render's phase 4). The panel's side: the setting (the box's own, in no settings file), telling the service again after a restart, the meter, OSC under `/nxlx/sound/...` to the D82 targets and the D94 nodes (two switches, off, capped), and a slow envelope onto one shader input on the classic renderer. Setup > Sound reactivity. D80 rows; remote support may switch capture off and never on.

The benchmark found a division by zero in the tempo that no test had reached (LESSONS).
- **CI, first run:** `test (3.12)` failed once in `tests.test_boxcert` (a serial of 30 digits where 32 are asserted): `tools/boxcert.py` masked the serial's first digit with 7 alone, so one serial in 128 began with 00 and openssl printed it shorter. Fixed at its cause (the first digit is 1 to 7), with a test of the edge values; the assertion is unchanged. `pvj-render (Ubuntu)` failed once on a superseded commit in `MappingTest.test_the_same_with_a_smaller_inside` (a pixel black) and passed on the next; that is the renderer's and was not touched here.
- **The security review of 7ffce1c:** two Mediums (the fast bands reached devices off the box and the support tunnel; no core limit) and three Lows (the cap below the schedule, targets worked out every 10 ms, late values looking fresh), all fixed with tests; the bands now leave the box at 5 a second at most, averaged over 200 ms, and never through the tunnel (D96's addendum).
- **Open:** everything on the Pi with a USB audio input (DEVICE-TESTING SR1 to SR8); whether arecord runs in this sandbox; the browser test runs in CI only (no Playwright here). It reads a microphone and sends network output: an independent security review before merge.

## 2026-10-16, the screen's card: Automatic did nothing on the Pi, and why (`screen-card`, D91)

- **On the test Pi** (master 3ad61a7, #139 deployed by the coordinator): the player was started with `--drm-connector=0.HDMI-A-1`, had no screen, and the panel started it again with the monitor's own 2560 x 1440. No black screen: D90's net held. Automatic itself did nothing.
- **Cause, read in mpv 0.40's drm_common.c:** `--drm-connector` takes a bare name and compares it whole; the "0." that help prints is the card's place in libdrm's list, which nothing parses. The card is picked by `--drm-device` or as the first with an output. On a Pi 4 the outputs are card1's.
- **Fixed:** the card from /sys/class/drm (cardN-NAME), and `--drm-device=/dev/dri/cardN --drm-connector=NAME --drm-mode=...`; the mode alone where two cards share a name. `CardTest` holds the Pi 4, a one-card PC and a two-card board to exact argv; each case fails with the old text. Lesson in LESSONS.md.
- **Not run on the Pi.** S1 says what to check after the next deploy.

## 2026-10-15, the screen's mode: Automatic up to 1920 x 1200 at 60 Hz, a mode by hand tried and kept or back (`screen-modes`, #139, D90)

The owner, after the research on outputs and resolutions: "I want you to proceed with whatever is going to provide the most economic output for performance and clarity and detail in the output." Read as the research's recommendation; built on a branch of its own while #138 (transitions-fast, D89) changes the crossfade.

- **Automatic on every box** (`pvj/screenmode.py`): the screen's own mode up to 1920 x 1200 at about 60 Hz, else the largest up to that size of its own shape (else 1080p) at 60, 50 only without 60. The owner's 1440p monitor goes from 2560 x 1440 at 75 to 1080p60 at the update.
- **Found on the way:** sysfs lists the sizes but never the rates, so the player's service asks `mpv --drm-mode=help` (the rates as mpv compares them, to 0.01 Hz) and passes one exact `--drm-mode` from that list. And a bad mode does not make the service loop, as expected: mpv with `--idle` stays up with no screen. So the panel asks a new player for `current-vo` and starts it once more with the screen's own mode if there is none. Both in LESSONS.md.
- **By hand:** Setup > Screen output (Owner), the screen's modes, the question in place, then "Keep this mode? Going back in 15 s" from the moment the new player is up; back by itself, also across a restart of the panel or the box. The keep is refused through the support tunnel, like the network's confirm (D84). Not in an export, not imported (`boxcare.BOX_OWN`).
- **The test pattern** at the screen's size, with a grid, a 1 pixel red border and the mode in words. `GET /api/status` has `screen` (in use, asked, why). The mapping page says when a change of shape squeezed a mapping; its points were already moved per axis.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_screenmode.py` (each kind of screen, the exact text and mpv's own comparison, the fallbacks, the try with a stand-in clock and timer, the report read as untrusted, the mapping through 1080p and 1024 x 768, rollback on master's own code at 33e7eb4, and the texts against a real mpv in CI and in the 0.40 job). Each part was broken on purpose and seen to fail. `tests/test_transport.py` and `tests/test_shaders.py` now expect the sized pattern; `tests/test_boxcare.py` knows `BOX_OWN`.
- **Not run:** nothing on the Pi, no screen, no projector; no mode has been set by this code on any display. The device rows are S1 to S14. It reads devices and takes a file from another account: it needs the independent review before it is merged.
- **CI, first run:** all green but `test (3.9)`, on the known NDI PipeReuseTest flake (passed five times out of five here, and on the rerun).
- **The independent review (2026-10-16, default model; Fable out of credits): one Medium, five Low, all fixed** (D90's last paragraph): the report is read without following a link or waiting on a pipe (M1); Keep and the going back are one step (L1); one thread claims the look at a new player (L2), which also showed two threads' writes of the wanted file sharing a temp name; a failed word with the player is "not known yet" (L3); a setting of the wrong kind is Automatic (L4); the HDMI screen and its card are passed with `--drm-connector` (L5). Each has a test seen to fail without the fix; the regular-file check in `read_json` is a second guard behind the look at the name and has no test of its own that fails without it.

## 2026-10-15, a smaller still for the crossfade where mpv can scale an overlay (D89, #138)

The owner called the second of standing still before a crossfade "not acceptable" and suggested working at a lower size and scaling up. The coordinator measured on the test Pi 4 the same day (2560 x 1440, a 1920 x 1080 clip, mpv 0.40.0): a still of the window 0.63 to 0.68 s, of the clip alone 0.38 to 0.44 s, a full-size step 57 to 71 ms and a half-size step drawn at full size with `dw`, `dh` 26 to 38 ms.

- **Built on the branch `transitions-fast`, draft pull request #138:** the player is asked once per process whether it scales an overlay; where it does, a still of the clip at its own size when that is the screen (else of the window), a large still read at half its size, every step and every wipe's part drawn at full size by the player; where it does not (CI's 0.37), the commands of D71 unchanged. `mix.last` in `GET /api/status` says which still and what size. D89 has the rules and what a still of the clip lacks (the logo, the bars beside a clip of another shape).
- **The stress test found one fault in its first run** against a stand-in that scales: a generator chosen during the screenshot made the still of the clip the carrier's size, and the box gave up. A still of the clip that misses is now a cut, and twice in a row that player gets stills of the window. A lesson.
- **Tests:** hand tests of each piece, the real Player over the stand-in for both kinds of mpv, the stress test on both, and `ScaledCase` on a real mpv: 0.37 in the effects-gpu jobs, the Pi's 0.40 in the job ndi-mpv (new step, with a virtual display). Each piece was broken on purpose; the table is in D89 and in the stress test's docstring.
- **The independent review of #138** (nothing high, two medium, five low), all fixed the same day with tests: wipes and slides take the window's still over a clip with bars, a refused still of the clip is a cut and never a give-up, the clip's still is held to its size, an unwritable question's pixel is "cannot tell"; the Pi's check list gained the effect and opacity runs and the 1080p screen. D89 has the list.
- **Not run:** nothing on the Pi. X24 to X27 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` are for the coordinator's measurement there.

It does not touch what changed here. Left as a timing flake to watch.

## 2026-10-15, the first session on the HP: the move checked, and a test that failed by chance

The owner moved development from the Mac to his HP (Windows 11) and asked for the WSL route.

- **Checked before trusting the notes:** master is 67da348 (#135 merged), all 16 of its checks green; open are only #116 and #65. The box answers on the network, and its secure connection checks out against the owner's `root.pem` (the certificate names nxlx-mastercontrol.local and 192.168.0.169, good until 2027-11-14). Curl on Windows itself refuses that connection unless given `--ssl-no-revoke`, because Windows asks for a revocation list that a private root does not publish; curl inside WSL has no such step.
- **WSL:** Ubuntu installed with git, gh, Python, Node and curl. Mirrored networking failed to start on this computer (WSL error 0x8007054f, falling back to no network at all), so WSL runs with its normal networking for now, which reaches the Pi; mirrored is needed only for TouchOSC's helper and is to be tried again after a Windows restart.
- **The test that failed by chance:** `test_never_the_pin` in `tests/test_join.py` looked for the four-digit PIN anywhere in an answer that also holds a random six-digit guest code (seen: PIN 6283, code 628324). It now looks for the PIN as a word of its own, through `tests.pin_stands_in` (a digit or a letter beside it means it is part of something else), and so do four more checks that a review found or I did: the recovery test whose answer holds a new device's token, two in `test_https` whose error holds the test server's random port, and one in `test_auth` that looks through the settings, where the PIN's hash and salt are random hex (about one run in 730). The other checks of the same kind look at short fixed texts. The same rule written twice before (`test_boxcare`, `test_pin_and_logout`) is left as it is.
- **Not run:** nothing on the Pi; SSH from the HP is not yet allowed by the Pi.

## 2026-10-12, one sound source for the whole box (`ndi-sound-from`, D87; not pushed, never on a box)

The owner sends his picture with NDI Scan Converter and VLC's sound through a separate NDI sender that has no picture, and asked that the NDI page account for networked sound. Step 1 of the design: one source named as where the sound comes from, played under every NDI picture.

- **Part 1, the helper, the settings and the actions.** `SoundFeed` in `pvj/ndi.py`: a connection of the service's own, opened for sound alone, with one thread that hands each block to the receiver on the screen (`Receiver.take_audio`, which is what the receiver's own sound loop now calls too). It stays while a picture is closed and opened again. Settings `ndi.sound_from` (an allowed source's id) and `ndi.sound_delay_ms`, no new schema; `POST /api/ndi` with `sound_from` and `sound_delay`; the source that is the sound source cannot be forgotten. Every writer of the section keeps both keys.
- **The audio-only bandwidth is 10.** Read from the owner's copy of the SDK (6.3.2; the header was unpacked into a temporary folder outside the repository, the one number read, the folder removed). Tried on the test Pi with the real library: Test Patterns opened that way gave 51 blocks of sound and no picture in five seconds, and a picture and 50 blocks when opened as before; Scan Converter gave no picture that way and 108 as before.
- **On the Pi's network, by hand in a lab folder that was removed afterwards (nothing of the box was changed or restarted).** The helper's real `Receiver` and `SoundFeed`: the picture of Scan Converter (1540 x 964) with the sound of Test Patterns into mpv 0.40 with the player's options and null outputs: two tracks, `audio-pts` at real time, `avsync` under 0.0001, the tone at -20 dBFS; the picture opened again with the sound connection opened once. The owner's own sound-only sender, "macOS AV Output", connected and sent no block of sound in 22 seconds (nothing was playing into it, as far as can be told from here): the picture played as bare frames. So his case is built and not yet seen with sound.
- **Tasks, for the unit's bound of 64.** One process, by hand: 6 with the finder, 35 at most with one picture connection, 38 to 46 with a sound-only connection beside it, the same after the picture was opened again six times. The helper has about five threads of its own. The running helper stood at 28 with one connection. No unit change was made; whether to raise the bound is the owner's.
- **Found while measuring:** a sound sender that sends nothing while nothing plays would have made every new clip begin as bare frames and be opened a second time when its sound came. The feed now remembers the form of its last sound, and a picture opened while it is quiet gets its sound track from the start.
- **What the stand-in library and CI need (another builder's):** a source with sound and no picture; a receiver opened with bandwidth 10 must be given no picture (and the bandwidth it was opened with should be readable by a test); and for the ndi-mpv job a case with the picture from one stand-in source and the sound from another, the sound offset by a known amount, judged by `audio-pts` at real time and by the delay setting moving it.
- **Parts 2 to 4, the same day.** The page's card "Where sound comes from" (three choices, a picker, the line that says what arrives, the slider for the delay); a sender with sound and no picture answers "X sends sound and no picture; choose it under 'Where sound comes from' on the NDI page", is labelled "sound only" and has "Use for sound" in place of Play; the delay is applied as mpv's `audio-delay` for the NDI stream with sound, set before the load and taken back on every road the narrowing is. The browser test's steps were rewritten for the card and checked for syntax only (`node --check`): CI's panel-ui job is their first run. The manual has the card.
- **The helper's unit allows 96 tasks, not 64** (the owner's go, after the numbers above). A box that is set up for NDI gets the new unit at its next install or update.
- **The owner's own rig, by hand, while he played a film in VLC through NDI's sound device:** Scan Converter's picture with the sound of "macOS AV Output": 48 kHz, six channels (two played), loudest at -27 to -47 dBFS after the headroom, two tracks, `audio-pts` at 1.000 of real time over 150 seconds, `avsync` under 0.001 (0.24 at ten seconds in the first run), and through five changes of size in two seconds the sound connection was opened once. The Pi was loaded by the box's own player at the time. Nobody listened; how far the two senders are apart is for the ear and the slider.
- **Two more, asked for by the coordinator.** A press on the sound source (or on a sender known to be sound only) is refused before the player or the helper is touched; before, it took the picture off the screen and closed the sound's connection first. And a source that changes size while it is being opened is opened again by the helper, three tries in all inside the press's own time, in place of answering the press with "the source changed to ...".
- **CI's first run of the whole (#132), three failures, mended, not yet run again.** The judge of "Play on the sound-only source" met the newer refusal for the box's chosen sound source: it now asks both states by name (not chosen: "sends sound and no picture", then refused at once on a second press; chosen: "is where this box's NDI sound comes from ... what plays was not touched"), both sentences say what to do, and the press under a playing picture is judged (the picture plays on). The three descriptions of the sound choices were 12 px, under the Signal rule's 13: they are the page's own hints under the buttons now. One stand-in test made a folder with `dir=` and did not remove it itself.
- **The independent review of #132: "merge after the Highs". Answered on the branch, not pushed.** H1: the "sound only" label locked a slow picture sender out until the helper started again; it is advice now (Play stays, nothing refuses for it, it expires in ten minutes). M1: the sound source is compared by name and machine as a pad's is, by the helper (told the allowed row) and by the panel. M2: twins are looked at again at every status and end the sound's connection. M3: the delay only with one sound source chosen. M4: a sound source that keeps changing its sound is held back (3 s to a minute) and cannot blink the picture. Lows: the feed's thread that cannot start, the open's budget, the tunnel test through the route, the delay given with the load, the page saying what was measured (the coordinator's run on the box's own player: 2010 of 2011 frames, `audio-pts` within 40 ms, 48096 samples a second at the sound device, 33 tasks) and what nobody has heard.
- **The re-check found the M2 fix undoing M4:** the back-off was the connection's, and closing the connection for twins wiped it. It is the source's now, kept by the service across every connection, and it counts every ending of a picture's stream by that source's sound (a change of form, and sound starting under a picture opened without it). Two limits are written down as accepted: a change every 30 seconds or so is always a first one, and a host that announces the sound source's name at its address can silence the sound.
- **The final re-check, two things.** The pace let a greedy sender have three endings in 39 seconds for ever (it went back to 3 seconds after half a minute of calm): the pause now doubles with every ending and goes back only after ten minutes without one, so the bound is 0, 3, 9, 21, 45, 93 seconds and then one a minute, and both documents say that. And panel-ui's one failure was the test's own: it asked the box, from the page, for a sound source that is not on the network and counted the 404 it had asked for as a console error; that one question is now asked beside the page. The page itself asked for nothing that was not there (one 404 in the whole log).
- **Open.** A deploy and a listen. The delay against a real mpv. Scan Converter passes through several sizes when a clip changes, and each is a re-open of the picture (four in two seconds in that run): the picture side's own matter, not changed here.
- **Later, on `shader-thread-leak` (2026-10-13; not pushed, no box): the thread that disturbed other tests.** The stray folder scans in the full run came from the effects' worker (`shader-changes`), which looks at the picture once a second for as long as an effect is on; `tests.test_osc_reach.ShadersOverOscTest.test_the_effect_said_outright` ends with an effect on, and three tests of `test_controllers` and `test_pads_and_effects` left a worker too. `Changer.stop()` in `pvj/shaderlive.py` ends the thread and waits for it (the panel never calls it); `ServerBase` stops both engines' workers in a cleanup; `tests/__init__.py` looks for the name after every test and fails the test that left it (`WATCHERS`, tried in `tests/test_000_tmp.py`). Run on the Mac only.

## 2026-10-12, the NDI pipe on the Pi, and the two discovery areas (`ndi-pipe-cleanup`)

The owner tried NDI on the test Pi for the first time (a group, the box's own discovery server, Test Patterns from his Mac) and got "FIRESPRAY-31.LOCAL (Test Patterns) stopped: The input pipe was replaced; not writing to it". He asked whether he had made an error in one of the "two areas to control the box's discovery server".

- **The deploy that was not journalled:** the Pi has run master e03e274 (everything through #125) since 05:06 UTC on 2026-10-12; the entry below names 87c4f6a.
- **Read on the Pi (nothing changed there):** his settings were consistent (the server module on and listening on 192.168.0.169:5959, the input set to ask the box's own server, the group NXLX.Leyline, the source found five times at 1920 x 1080). The pipe is root's (uid 0) and the helper is uid 996.
- **The cause:** the helper's look at the pipe before writing took only a pipe it owned; since M3 (2026-10-11) the pipe is root's. No source could reach the screen on any set-up box. Fixed (`pipe_to_write`: a pipe of root's or the helper's own), with a test that fails on master's code and a step in the real-install test as the helper's account. A lesson is in LESSONS.md.
- **An independent review (Fable):** the fix is right and weakens nothing; nothing else found that stops a picture. Open and only a deploy can tell: whether the player draws Test Patterns' one still picture when sound is on (Matroska). Noted, not changed: a helper restart under a playing source is a black screen of up to about three seconds; since M3 the pipe keeps one inode, so a new writer could in a small window meet the old reader (a guess, Low).
- **The two areas:** running a server (Setup, NDI discovery server) and asking one (NDI input) are two halves. Each page now names its half, shows the state of the other and whether the two fit, and carries the switch; the list is headed "Discovery servers this box asks". Not seen in a browser on the Mac (no Playwright there); CI's browser test draws it.
- **The reviewer's two open points, settled with tests (`ndi-pipe-reuse`, not pushed, never on a box).** (1) The reused pipe: real, and worse than rated. With a real pipe and a reader that holds its end after the end of the stream, the next source's frames went into the old stream, the source ended "the player stopped reading the input", the watch took that for "something else was played" and forgot the source: black, the pad dark, nothing said. The coordinator then measured it on the Pi (Scan Converter changing size with each clip: the re-open lost the player twice out of twice, "playing" and "ended" both empty afterwards), which also showed that mpv does not let go of the pipe by itself. Fixed in three places: the panel tells the player to let go of the pipe before it asks for a source (`Player.release_pipe`), the helper holds the writer until the pipe was seen without a reader, before its answer to the panel (2 s at most), and the watch shows a source again that lost its reader while the pipe is what the panel loaded. After the stop the panel asks mpv until it says it is idle (1.5 s at most; mpv's own `path` decides whether it holds the pipe, so a restarted panel still releases it), an open over a pipe that is still held is refused with the reason and never written, and the page never shows nothing playing with no reason: an ended source always keeps why, and a source being brought back says "...; trying again". Still open from the coordinator's second look at the Pi (not started here): the panel calling a source "playing" only when mpv itself has tracks and moving time, and mpv stuck opening the Matroska stream of a still source with sound, whose cause was read in mpv 0.40's source (its still-image probe reads on for a second picture block, up to 10000 blocks; 0.35 and 0.37 have no such probe, so CI could not see it) and is written in NDI.md; the first cure (the first picture twice) did nothing on the box, because both copies carried the time 0. An experiment on the test Pi (the helper's writer feeding mpv 0.40 run by hand, asked over its socket) then showed the second fault behind it: mpv pauses everything "for buffering" while a picture track is dry, so a still picture silenced its own sound, and a source that went still came back as late as it had stood. Now the stream is loaded with cache-pause=no and a picture that stands is written once more; measured there for over two minutes each way, not yet on the box's own player. From the independent review of the branch: a player that will not let go is no longer left wedged (the helper's source is closed, then the player is asked again), a source that is gone from the network is refused before the screen is touched, the page's "playing" also needs mpv to have a track (a load that never opened is shown again once, then ended with the reason), only ENXIO means "no reader", and a stop that mpv does not answer is not a release. On the box's own player that was better and not right: two tracks and a sound device, and ALSA's own file said not a sample had been played. Found by hand: mpv waits with the sound until the picture reaches the first sound's time ("delaying audio start"), which a still never does; the load now has initial-audio-sync=no, and the end-to-end checker wants the sound's clock to move. ALSA's pointer on the box is still to be seen. Then, on the branch ndi-avsync, sound against picture on a moving source: measured through the real helper into mpv 0.40 (in step to a millisecond once the stream begins whole; 0.2 s out for two seconds when the first sound was stamped after the first picture), and the stream changed so that it begins whole and its sound never jumps (which was also CI's last failing case, a still whose sound comes back after six seconds). What mpv's own number cannot show, and the one-minute clap test for the owner, are in NDI.md. Follow-ups of the re-review (branch ndi-followups): the pause between tries stays grown until a source has played for 30 s, mpv idle clears the record that the pipe plays, a restarted mpv's source is shown again, a first press refused at the pipe keeps its reason, the flaky no-jump test follows its own clock, and two race tests (two presses at once; a load after the writer's deadline). Connecting to the new source before the old one is let go was looked at and not built (NDI.md says why). Earlier, not done from that review: bringing a source back after mpv itself restarted (low 5), keeping the reason when a first play's look at the pipe fails (low 8), and the two tests of races it lists. The first play's "one frame, then still, received 0" is by its counters a sender that sent nothing (no sound came either); NDI.md says how that was read. D62 has the paragraph. (2) A helper restart under a source: it comes back by itself every time in the tests, also when the helper goes again during the watch's try; by the timers in the code the gap is 3 to 7 s plus whatever the library needs to find the sender again, not "about 3". A first press that was still connecting when the helper went is not brought back (answered 409). Proposed, not built: a first retry after 1 s when the helper has just started. (3) Stale texts from before M3 put right (NDI.md's "only the helper makes and removes the pipe", the path in the drawing, two comments); the writer's wait for the stream's form spun on an event that was already set (up to half a second of one core with sound on), fixed. Unproven without mpv: listed under "Not verified" in NDI.md.
- **Not done:** no deploy (the standing word ended at the wind-down), so nobody has yet seen an NDI picture from the Pi since M3. With local announcements still on, finding a sender does not show that the discovery server was used.
- **A test that would have caught it (`ndi-box-e2e`, not yet run in CI):** a stand-in for NDI's library, our own C written from what `pvj/ndi.py` calls (`tests/ndi_standin/`), a unit test that drives it through the helper's real code (`tests/test_ndi_standin.py`), and new steps in `tests/real_install_test.sh` that take the whole road on the box layout: the real `pvj-ndi-runtime install`, the real helper in its sandbox, root's pipe, the panel's API, the real mpv (with its null outputs, the runner has no screen). Sound off, sound on, a helper restart under a playing source. What mpv does with one still picture and then only sound is printed and reported, not judged yet. With the old check simulated on the Mac the new question ends: `'PVJ-STANDIN (moving)' did not play: it stopped with "the input pipe was replaced; not writing to it"`. It never shows that NDI's own library works; NDI.md, "What the tests prove".
- **Its first run in CI (#128) was green**, and its report-only step showed the fault the Pi showed the same day: after one still picture and then only sound, mpv sat with `core-idle` true and its clock at 0.0 while the helper wrote sound. The helper's counts are not proof that the player plays. So the steps now ask mpv itself (path, tracks, a picture track of the right size, a moving clock), and six hand-overs are each a step: still then sound, the same source twice, A then B at once, sound on and off under a source, a change of size, a helper restart. Each ends good (mpv plays, or `ended` gives a reason) or prints `FINDING` with `SILENT LOSS` or `STUCK`; one switch (`NDI_FINDINGS_FATAL`) makes findings fatal once the hand-over is mended on `ndi-pipe-reuse`. The six steps then ran: the first is a finding (STUCK), the other five passed on the runner while the Pi lost its re-opens. So the steps were made more like the Pi: sources at 1280 x 720 (a picture larger than the pipe), sound on both sides, a start from the stuck state with nothing cleaned, and a chain of eight hand-overs with nothing cleaned between them. Those have not run in CI yet; the job's limit went from 15 to 25 minutes.
- **CI on the Pi's mpv (`ndi-mpv-as-pi`, not yet run in CI):** the second run found four hand-overs lost on the runner; and a mended hand-over that was then green there was still stuck on the Pi, whose mpv is 0.40 where the runner's is 0.37. A new job, `ndi-mpv (Debian 13, the Pi's mpv)`, runs in a `debian:trixie` container, fails if its mpv is not 0.40.x, and runs `tests/test_ndi_mpv.py`: the stand-in, the helper's real code, the real Api and a real mpv started by the real Player, judged by the code the real-install test uses (now one module, `tests/ndi_standin/mpvword.py`). The whole suite also runs there as a step that does not decide the job until its first result was read. The owner would add that job's name to the ruleset's required checks; nothing in the ruleset was changed.
- **One branch for the mending and its judges (`ndi-pipe-reuse`, #129):** `ndi-mpv-as-pi` was merged in. The ndi-mpv job's first run (mpv 0.40.0, master's pvj) matched the Pi: the still source stuck with no track, a source whose picture stopped froze with its sound, A then B with sound was lost. The whole suite did not finish there in 35 minutes (it stood in a test server's shutdown; it runs with `-v` now to name what failed). The Pi then showed a fault no test saw (the sound never started, because the stand-in's sound began at the picture's own time): the stand-in got sources with late sound, sound is judged by `audio-pts` at real time, and the job runs the still cases on the code from before the mending, where they must fail. The helper-restart step of the real-install test was the test's fault and is mended. The switches are set to fatal; no run has judged the mended code with them yet.
- **That run, and sync (`ndi-pipe-reuse`):** real-install passed with every switch fatal; the ndi-mpv job passed 14 of 15 on mpv 0.40 and failed the still with sound after a long gap, which the next change of the helper (silence written as time passes, the sound track beginning at 0) is said to mend. The owner asked that sound against picture be proven on a moving source: the job now judges mpv's own `avsync`, `total-avsync-change`, `audio-pts` against `time-pos` and the dropped frames, for a moving source with sound, with late first sound, after a re-open and across a pause, and prints the numbers for every case. NDI.md says what that cannot show: the helper stamps at arrival, so a sender that was out of step reads 0.000. Not run in CI yet.
- **A flaky unit test made deterministic (`usb-cache-test`, not pushed):** `test_a_huge_directory_is_scanned_only_up_to_a_limit_and_cached` gave `2 != 0` on a slow runner, whose five GETs outlasted the cache's two seconds. The test now holds `time.monotonic` as `pvj/api.py` sees it (a stand-in for the module's `time`, nothing else touched), and a second test shows the opposite: after the lifetime the two drives are scanned again, once. Both fail on a cache check that is broken either way. No product change.
- **That test again, the real cause (`usb-cache-test-2`, not pushed):** #131 held the clock and the test still gave `2 != 0` in CI. The cache was never at fault. The test counted every `os.scandir` in the process, and the shaders' `shader-changes` thread, left running by an earlier test, globs `/sys/devices/platform/v3dbus` now and then (glob scans); found by logging each scan's path, thread and stack over the whole suite on the Mac (34 scans, all that one). The test now counts only scans of its own drives and makes a foreign scan itself inside the window. With a stray scanning thread master's test fails 4 of 4 and the new one passed 20 of 20 under 16 busy loops; the plain module 20 of 20 too. No product change. Open: which test leaves that thread behind was not looked for.

## 2026-10-11, what a remote support session may do (`support-reach`, #125, D84; a draft, security review before any merge)

The owner asked what an Owner may do from a remote support session, suggested "everything since it is a 'phone for help' solution", was advised "nearly everything", and said "proceed with ... your recommendations for the remote sessions". The principle is D84's first line: anything that ends when the session ends, nothing that outlives it or locks the owner out.

- **Opened to a session of the Owner's role:** settings import (the recovery stick switch is left as the box has it), theme add and remove, the OSC key, the OSC state targets (and with them his view of the targets and his export), the guest lock, removing a Guest's or an Operator's device.
- **Kept refused for every session, each with its reason in `support.REMOTE_DENY`:** pairing, sessions, invitations, `/api/access`, the PIN and the QR, `/api/recovery`, `/api/https`, the session's own settings, start and extend, power-off, factory reset. Sessions below the Owner's role are as they were.
- **The log.** What a session changes is written into its entry of the support log (`did`) and shown under Recent sessions; a session that saw the OSC key makes the page tell the owner to make a new one.

## 2026-10-11, the review of #94 at 8cfde96, and master merged in (`ndi-input`, #94; a draft, not merged)

The critical review found the branch safe for the owner's test box and asked for these before the merge; each has a test in `tests/test_ndi_review.py` that was committed first and seen to fail.

- **Medium 1, an update broke NDI until a reboot.** The installer wrote the NDI tmpfiles lines again and never ran them; the helper's unit named root's pipe folder without a dash. Now the installer runs `systemd-tmpfiles --create` on that file right after the refresh, on a real install, before it starts anything; the unit has the dash. CI's real-install job has a step that removes the folders and the tmpfiles file, installs over it, and looks for the folders, the pipe, the helper and the panel's way to it. Device row N31 is the owner's Pi itself.
- **Medium 2, a crowd of names silenced every pad.** The panel now tells the helper the ids of the allowed sources (ids only) and the helper keeps them in its list of 64 while they are there. Tested with 500 announced names.
- **Low:** a Guest's status carries no reason for a pad's refusal (it can name an address); CI fetches master so the rollback tests run there; the discovery server's program is started in a session of its own and its whole group is ended; nothing but the program may lie in `/opt/pvj-ndi-server` (NDI's program reads a configuration file beside itself), else no install and no start; NDI.md says a pad follows a name at an IP address, and that a press does not fall back to "the same port as last time" (decided, with the reasons).
- **Master merged** (#124, D83): conflicts only in the logs and the workflow, both sides kept; D83 stands before D85. `support-reach` (D84) had not merged: when it does, its table of what a support session may reach needs `GET` and `POST /api/ndi/discovery` and `POST /api/modules/ndi-discovery` as never reachable, as `support.REMOTE_DENY` has them here.

## 2026-10-11, pads hold NDI sources; groups and discovery servers (`ndi-input`, #94, D62 amended; a draft, not merged)

Built on the branch after the hand-over below, by one session. Nothing of it has run on a Pi, with a real sender, with mpv or in a browser on the dev Mac: the Python tests ran here, the browser steps were written and syntax-checked and run in CI only.

- **Pads.** A third kind in the pad editor. The pad stores the id; the allowed list is `ndi.sources`; an Operator allows a source by picking one that is on the network now, the Owner removes. The press checks the live list first and then calls `Api.play_ndi`. The rules, who does what and the trap are in D62 and in pvj/NDI.md ("Pads that hold an NDI source").
- **What the hand-over had wrong.** It said the id follows a name. `ndi.source_id` binds it to the announcing machine too, so the rule built is "a name on a machine", and a same-named sender elsewhere is refused by name. Two senders under one id could not be seen (the helper folded them); `clean_sources` now marks the row (`twins`).
- **Groups and discovery** (asked by the owner during the session). Groups through the finder's `p_groups`. Discovery servers through NDI's own configuration file, written by the panel into a new root-made folder, `/run/pvj-ndi-config`; the helper's unit, the tmpfiles lines and the panel's drop-in changed, so the real-install job in CI is the first real look at them. No mDNS switch was built: NDI documents none (LESSONS).
- **The SDK was read** (the owner's copy, 6.3.2, read only, nothing copied into the repository): every structure, signature and constant the helper uses matches. Two notes are in NDI.md.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_ndi_pads.py` (22) and `tests/test_ndi_groups.py` (17); each was committed before its code and seen to fail without it. The rollback tests run master's own `pvj` from git.
- **Open for the owner.** Should a pad follow a sender to a new address when exactly one sender carries the name (now: no)? Should the box run a discovery server itself (now: no; its licence forbids bundling)? Is an Operator allowing sources right, or should it be the Owner's alone?
- **Slips.** The first test commit held one test that did not pass yet (the schedule step; fixed in the next commit). The full suite was started while files were still being edited, so its first run proves nothing and it was run again.

## 2026-10-11, the NDI input brought up to master (`ndi-input`, #94, D62 amended)

Master had moved 335 commits since the branch was last merged (the area desks of D72, crossfades, shader pads, effects over shaders, controller layouts, the owner PIN, the OSC locks, HTTPS, the roles of D80, owner recovery). Merged with a merge commit; fourteen files conflicted.

- **The schema bump is out.** The branch had settings schema 15 for one key; the key is now read with defaults and the schema stays 14 (D62, the amendment). Proved with master's own settings module: a file this branch wrote with an address and the sound switch loads on master and keeps the key; a file master wrote loads here. `boxcare.KNOWN_SCHEMA` back to 14; the two migration tests became one test that no migration runs.
- **The play of a source under the player's lock.** The branch's play held the helper's "screen" lock and then took the player's; master's `_as_newest` takes the player's lock first and then cancels the NDI ticket under it, which would have been an inversion of D71's order. The NDI play now hands `_as_newest` a `ready` check (the ticket, the pipe's owner, the note of what plays) that runs under the player's lock, and the screen lock is a leaf for the ticket alone. `ndi` and `speed.hold` have places in `pvj/locks.py`.
- **Two pipes.** Master decides whether to stop the capture helper by `Player.pipe_playing`, which says only that a pipe is what plays; with the NDI pipe loaded the capture helper would have been left alone. The player now remembers which pipe (`pipe_is`), and each helper is stopped by its own.
- **Roles.** `GET /api/ndi` Operator, `POST /api/ndi` Owner (and a full support session, as Streams), `{"ndi": id}` refused to a Guest; rows in `tests/test_roles.py`, nothing added to `policy.LEGACY_LIVE`.
- **The panel.** The Mix picture screen on master has no Speed slider (it is on the strip), so the "held at 1.00x" hint is on the strip's Speed now; the NDI page is a Setup row beside Streams with role `op`; `tests/ui/shell.js` lists its screen for the owner and the operator and its controls in ADDED; `signal-pages.js` and `panel.test.js` carry the row.
- **Docs.** HANDOFF, `pvj/NDI.md`, `docs/UI.md`, `docs/MANUAL.md`, `pvj/README.md`, N0 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`.
- **CI's first run of the merge** (`0d36ef1`): `panel-ui`, `effects-gpu`, `real-install` and `shellcheck` green; the unit test job was cut at its own twenty-minute budget (`timeout -s ABRT 1200`), with no deadlock in the dump: master alone took 1027 s that morning, and the NDI modules add a few minutes of real pipes and measured waits. The budget is thirty minutes now (the job's limit of 45 stands). The slowest test in the run is master's own (`test_roles`, 94 s).

**The independent review of `b46410c`** (nothing High; three Medium, two Low; "safe for the owner's test box, merge after these"), each fixed with a test seen to fail first, one commit each:
- M1: the decoder narrowing for the stream with sound outlived a panel restart (an in-memory list). `Player._widen` sends the `MKV_ONLY` defaults at a Player's first word with mpv and whenever mpv is another process, not in the middle of a pipe load.
- M2: the module switched off under a source on the screen skipped the clear (because the pipe played), kept the note and the speed hold, and the helper went anyway. Now: clear, let go with a reason (`ndi.SWITCHED_OFF`, shown on the page as "RESOLUME stopped: the NDI input was switched off"), the hold goes, the panel's switch says it.
- M3: the pipe is root's, made once by tmpfiles lines the setup writes, in a folder of root's; the helper writes it and never unlinks it; the panel checks the owner and the folder. The sentence "cannot reach localhost" was true of 127.0.0.1 only: corrected in D62, NDI.md and the unit, and the helper's trust boundary is a section of SECURITY.md. Denying the box's own address: not static, so written down and a device row (N21); `SystemCallFilter` a device row (N20), not set blind.
- L4: the flaky second-open test waits for the second open instead of a tenth of a second. L5: `pvj-ndi-runtime install` prints the sha256 of the placed library.
- The coordinator's default: NDI sound is OFF until it has been heard on a box; the page says why. Guests see no NDI page.

**The owner's answers of 2026-10-11** (his words are in D62): the licence question is set aside as personal and proof-of-concept use, he contacts NDI before any mass production; pads should hold NDI sources; sound off by default with an easy switch; no NDI page for guests. The last two are so (the switch on the NDI page; `GET /api/ndi` is `KINDS["op"]` in `tests/test_roles.py`, so a Guest gets 403, and `tests/ui/shell.js` MENUS gives a guest no `setup/ndi`). The pads were taken out of this branch's scope by the coordinator.

**Hand-over for NDI pads** (not built). A source's identity is the helper's id, `ndi.source_id(name, host)` (the name the runtime reports plus the announcing host; `_ID` pins its form); the page's list is `Input.status()["sources"]` (`{"id", "name", "from"}`), fresh every 2 s, and only ids on it can be opened (`Input.open` asks the helper, which refuses one not on the network: "that source is not on the network now"). "Allowed" has no store yet: the natural one is a key beside `ndi.addresses` in the settings (`"ndi": {"sources": [{"id", "name", "from"}]}`, written by `set_ndi`, read with defaults like the rest, no schema bump; `ndi.validate_saved` must learn it for boxcare's export). A pad would store that record (D73 added `shader` and `preset` to a pad with no schema change; do the same with an `ndi` key; `pads.validate` and the pad editor's kind list in `padKinds`). The play path is `Api.play_ndi` (`{"ndi": id}`): it claims a D71 wish at the tap, opens (seconds, under `ndi.lock`, place 6a in `pvj/locks.py`), and loads under the player's lock through `_as_newest(..., ready=)`; a pad path should call it, not copy it. Guests: `policy._play` allows only `pad`/`file` bodies, and `test_roles` has a table of pad bodies that must not reach a stream (`test_a_guest_may_play_a_pad_so_a_pad_must_never_be_a_way_to...`): an NDI pad must be refused there and in a Room scene exactly as a stream pad is, and `GUEST_BODIES` already refuses `{"ndi"}`. Controllers: a MIDI or OSC pad press becomes `{"pad": [bank, index]}` through `LEGACY_LIVE`; how a stream pad answers them today is the rule to copy. Traps: the id follows the NAME and host, and anyone on the LAN can announce a sender with that name (say it in the docs; prefer an owner's address); `Input.open` takes up to `FIRST_FRAME_SECONDS + 8`, so a pad press blocks that long on a missing source unless the list is consulted first (`status()["sources"]`); the speed hold (`_hold_speed`) and `pipe_is` are already right for any caller of `play_ndi`; sound off means the pipe is bare frames whatever the helper answers.

Nothing of this ran on hardware. What the Pi has seen is what the entries of 2026-10-08 below say: the opt-in, discovery, a moving source for a minute and a still source over SSH, nobody at the monitor; the sound never heard anywhere. The helper service `pvj-ndi.service` from that trial is still enabled on the test Pi.

## 2026-10-11, the box sends its state back over OSC (`osc-replies`, #123, D82; a draft, not merged)

The owner's answer to "Should the box send its state back to TouchOSC, so the layout shows what is really playing?" was "Yes, build it" (2026-10-10). Built as D82 says, with the threat model written and pushed before the code: off by default; at most 4 targets that the Owner adds at the studio (a private IPv4 address on a subnet the box is connected to, a port from 1024 up), checked again at every send; state under `/pvj/state/...` as floats and strings; on change 5 times a second, a heartbeat every 2 seconds, everything every 30 seconds and on `/pvj/state` from a listed target; at most 20 datagrams and 12,000 bytes a second to one target and 60 and 36,000 to all; the key never in a packet; one thread, a socket that never waits, time to live 1. The state is the controller lights' own (`pvj/boxstate.py`). The panel has the card "Send state back" on Setup > OSC for the Owner. `pvj/OSC.md` has the true rule in place of "no replies", the state table and "Setting up TouchOSC to listen".

Tests: `tests/test_osc_feedback.py` (35, one with the real shader engine and a fake graphics chip; the effect's amount and a running Vibes were added after the review; 39 in all), a row in `tests/test_roles.py`, a browser step and the inventory. Each guard was broken on purpose after its commit and a test seen to fail (25 breakages; one, the plain spelling of an address, fails nothing on this Python and D82 says why; two showed tests that passed for the wrong reason, which were fixed). The browser test was not run on this Mac (no Playwright here): CI is its first run.

Not done and not known: nothing met a real TouchOSC, a real tablet or the box. Whether TouchOSC takes bundles, and whether a fader jumps under a finger when its own value comes back, are open (D82, rows F1 to F9 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`). An independent security review on another model comes before any merge.

**After the review (the same day).** The independent security review of 5dd0e64 found nothing High. Fixed, each with a test seen to fail first: the targets were readable by a support session in the settings export and by anyone in a diagnostics file (taken out of both); a file name with a broken byte or a number beyond a float32 silenced the sender (encoded safely, and one bad value no longer stops the rest); container, bridge and VPN ports counted as the box's own networks (they do not now); a stale target blocked adding a new one (only the new one is checked, the stale one is named on the page). D82 lists what else a support session can still read in an export, left as it is. The reviewer's answers to the four questions are adopted (D82).

## 2026-10-10, owner recovery (`owner-recovery`, #122, D81)

The owner asked for "a reliable and user friendly way in for an owner in the event codes are forgotten and devices are lost" and said "Build both" to printed recovery codes and a USB stick at the box. The threat model went into D81 before the code: who guesses over the network with nobody present, who floods the throttle, who holds a stolen operator session, who stands in the room when a PIN is on the projector, who finds the paper. Server: codes of 12 symbols from a 31 symbol alphabet (about 59 bits), scrypt hashed with one salt per set, redeemed through `/api/pair` under the PIN's lock and throttle with one scrypt per attempt whether a set exists or not, burnt and the device added in one save; `/api/recovery` for owners only, never through remote support, TLS-only while the owner-only switch is on, and a code is redeemed over TLS only then too. The stick: `pvj/recovery.py`, polled by the PIN screen's tick, a fresh PIN for two minutes that dies at first use, at two minutes, when the stick comes out or the switch goes off. The export carries the switch only (the first draft would have exported the hashes: a lesson), an import never brings codes, a factory reset removes the section, and master's own code was run against a settings file written here (it loads, keeps the section, answers "wrong PIN" to a code). Panel: the Recovery card on People and codes, "I have a recovery code" and "Locked out?" on the pairing screen, a browser-test step. Docs: the manual's "Locked out?" for a venue manager, SECURITY, HANDOFF, four device-testing rows. Tests: 21 in `tests/test_recovery.py`, seen to fail without the code (the module does not import without `pvj/recovery.py`, and the routes are 404 on master). ## 2026-10-10, the trust check's policy (`https-own-ca`, #119, D79 amended)

A short session after the previous builder was cut off by a usage limit. The browser test in CI (head `18d8621`, job `panel-ui`) failed at its CSP check: the Secure connection page's trust check fetched `https://127.0.0.1/api/https/probe` from the `http://` page and `connect-src 'self'` refused it. On a real box the same fetch rested on a CSP level 3 rule for the default ports, unverified for Safari on iOS, so the check could have failed where it matters most. Weighed as an attacker first (D79, amended): the box now names its own `https://` origin in the policy of the HTML page over plain http, only while a certificate is in use and only when the Host header is a clean name or address that the certificate carries; the origin is the matched name plus the TLS listener's port, never a byte of the header; the header builder refuses any other shape a second time; the panel asks only in that case and otherwise answers by itself. The probe route was read and left as it was: a tiny answer, no CORS header. Tests: the pure function against thirty hostile and odd hosts, the header as sent over http and https for good, foreign, injected, ported and IPv6 hosts and on the document only, the probe's headers, and the browser test's step tightened to the no-certificate branch; each seen to fail without its code. ## 2026-10-10 (OSC: three locks on who may send, D78; branch `osc-lockdown`)

The owner asked to make the connection from TouchOSC to the box secure. Built as three locks on System > OSC, each off by default: a list of single devices, a paired device, a key in the address; plus the senders of the last ten minutes with "Allow this one" and the last 50 messages. The decision, the choices where the brief left one and the limits are in D78; the user's text is in `pvj/OSC.md` ("Who may send: three locks", "Setting up TouchOSC").

- **Run on the development Mac only** (no mpv there, so the tests that need it skip): the whole unit suite, with `tests.test_osc_lockdown` (40 tests) in it. After the commit, 28 pieces of the new code were broken on purpose, one at a time (the three checks, the key comparison, the limits, what is kept of a refused packet, the bounds, roles, expiry, removal, the tunnel, what each role is sent, export, import, reset). A test failed for 27 of them. The 28th (an import that takes the key from the file) is stopped in two places, `check_osc` and `_osc_layers`; with both broken a test fails.
- **Not run here**: the browser test (`tests/ui/panel.test.js`, the OSC part with real UDP on loopback and the page at 320 px with IPv6 addresses). This Mac has no Playwright and no mpv, and nothing was installed. CI runs it; see the pull request for its state.
- **Tried as an attacker, against the code**: a forged source address passes the list and the paired-device lock (as the docs say) and is stopped only by the key; a captured packet with the key is accepted when sent again (a replay, as the docs say); 5000 wrong keys forged as the tablet were compared 20 times and then refused unchecked, with nothing let in; three seconds after the flood the tablet's right key was let in again, and at one wrong key a second the tablet still got through part of the time (the limit can also be reset by a spray of forged addresses, so it is the 80 bits that stop guessing); a guest and a presenter get 403 from the key and the messages routes and no list in `GET /api/osc`.
- **Master's own code** loads a settings file with the new keys and keeps them on a save; it does not enforce them and its export would carry the key.
- **The first CI run of the browser test failed and was right**: two saves close together (a choice of hours, then a switch) each reopened UDP 9876 and the second got "Address already in use". Fixed in the code, not in the test: one save at a time, and the socket stays open when only the locks change; two tests, each seen to fail without its code. See the pull request for the state of the run after that.
- **Never on a box, never with a real TouchOSC.** Rows O1 to O12 in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`.
- Open: an independent security review before the merge; the panel has no log out; the receiver is IPv4 only.
