## 2026-10-10, HTTPS with the owner's own root (`https-own-ca`, #119, D79)

Branch `https-own-ca`, draft pull request #119, stacked on `pin-and-logout` (#117 merged meanwhile and master merged in, not rebased). The owner asked for a secure connection and chose the "own certificate authority on your Mac" route; he was away, so every choice is written down in D79 with its reason, and `docs/HTTPS.md` has the facts the design rests on with their sources (Apple's 825-day rule applies to all TLS server certificates, its 398-day rule explicitly not to user-added roots; iPhone and iPad need the second trust step under Certificate Trust Settings; Chrome on Android trusts a user root, most other Android apps do not; name constraints are enforced by Apple since iOS 9 and macOS 10.12, by Chrome on user roots since 112). Unconfirmed items are marked as such there.

Built, in this order, each part committed and pushed as it was finished:

- `tools/boxcert.py`: `make-root` (an encrypted ECDSA P-384 key and a public root with name constraints: `.local` names plus any domains given, the private IPv4 ranges plus any given; refuses to overwrite), `sign` (reads the names out of the box's request, leaves out what the constraints would refuse, 397 days by default and 825 at most, a random serial, `serverAuth`, SHA-256; writes the box's certificate followed by the root as one file and keeps `signed.json`), `renew`, `show`, `verify`, `list`. Every extension goes through a configuration file because LibreSSL has no `-addext`; the passphrase goes in on stdin, never on the command line. Tested end to end against OpenSSL 3.6 and the Mac's LibreSSL 3.3 in `tests/test_boxcert.py` (the chain with `openssl verify -purpose sslserver`, the names, the validity, the key usage, a wrong passphrase, no overwrite, the list, a name outside the constraints).
- `pvj/httpsbox.py` and the server: the box makes its key and request with the system's `openssl` (ECDSA P-256; `/var/lib/pvj/tls` 0700 and `key.pem` 0600, pvj-web's); a small DER reader says what an uploaded certificate is for and `load_cert_chain` refuses one that does not match the key; a run-out certificate is refused only when the box's clock is set from the network, and one in use is served whatever the clock says; the one before is kept for Undo; Remove and a factory reset return the box to HTTP only. `TlsServer` is the same handler on `PVJ_HTTPS_PORT` (443 in the unit; no new privilege, the capability for port 80 covers it), the handshake in the connection's own thread, every connection closed at once while no certificate is loaded. Over TLS the cookie is `__Host-pvj_token; Secure` (a second name: a cookie's identity is name, domain and path, not scheme, and browsers leave a Secure cookie alone when plain http tries to replace it). The switch `https.owner_only` (no schema change; master's own `settings.py` was made to load a file with the key and a device record with `secure: true`, in a test) can be changed over TLS only, by a device whose token was issued over TLS; while on and a certificate is loaded, the PIN is refused over plain http, a full-access device gets 403 with the https address on every route but hello, logout and the status itself, a full-access token issued over plain http is refused over TLS too, and guests go on as before. `tests/test_https.py` runs all of it over a real TLS socket on loopback with a certificate the tool signed from the box's own request.
- The panel: System > Secure connection (`httpsCards` and the functions beside it), the row's state line, a notice when a 403 points at https, and the reset's words. Browser steps at 320 px and the words per device with six injected user agents; the page is in the Signal list, so the width sweep covers it.

**Not built:** a box without `openssl`; revocation; automatic renewal (the root is not on the box, by design); a certificate for a public address (the constraints refuse it; `--no-constraints` exists); a root helper that would keep the key from the panel process (rejected in D79, with the reason). **Nothing ran on the box or met a phone.** CI's state is in the pull request; the independent security review is next.

What was tried as an attacker, in the tests and by hand: a cookie from one origin replayed on the other (the secure name is ignored over plain http; the plain cookie works over TLS by design, and with the switch on a full-access token issued over plain http is refused there); a certificate for another name, one whose key is not the box's, the root uploaded as a certificate, the request uploaded, garbage, a run-out one, and one too large (each refused with its message, nothing taken into use); a guest, a presenter and nobody on every certificate route (403 and 401); the switch toggled without the request header (403) and from plain http (403); the request file read for anything about the key (it holds the public key only); every GET's body, an export with passwords and the diagnostics file searched for the key and the certificate; a factory reset over TLS. Not tried: a downgrade by a device on the network answering for the box's name (the design's answer is in D79: a device with the root refuses it over https, and over http the switch keeps the PIN useless there); a key file read by another account (the modes are asserted, no second account exists in the tests).

## 2026-10-09, late evening (the tenth read of #111: the rule against one strike had one strike in it)

A re-check of the ninth read's answers by the second model found one high finding, in a rule the coordinator had asked for: "give up at once when a still took longer than the transition asked for". The box's default duration is one second and the limit for a slow still is one second, so at the default and at half a second (two of the panel's four choices) every slow still was again the last one for five minutes. The tests hid it: their helper raised the duration to two seconds. The Pi trial ran at two seconds, where the rule held.

- **Now:** the duration is out of the rule. One slow still is used at every duration; two in a row and the box dips; one still over `HOPELESS` (3 seconds) and it dips at once. A test runs one slow still at each of 0.5, 1, 2 and 5 seconds, and the helper no longer changes the duration.
- **Left as they are, from the same read (all low):** the give-up state is written without a lock from three places (a lost reset or a doubled "trying again" line at worst); the retry after five minutes is passive and a box that is truly too slow will freeze twice every five minutes while plays happen (a back-off would be kinder); a player restart does not start the count of slow stills again; Vibes forgets its own dip when it steps aside for a tapped clip, so if that clip then fails to load the old picture stays half dark until the next play; a second tap during the rise from black takes a still of a half-lit picture.
- **On the test Pi afterwards** (the owner said "deploy the transitions to the pi"; `c436f5f` installed at 00:31 UTC on 2026-10-10 the same way as before, three files equal by hash, five services active, `pvj-selftest` with no failed check; the panel and the player restarted). Screen 2560 x 1440, the same two clips, nobody at the monitor:
  - Crossfade at 1 s (the default), 0.5 s and 2 s, a wipe at 1 s and a slide at 2 s, three plays each: every play answered in 0.91 to 1.06 s, `mix.fallback` stayed empty, and the journal has no "the still took" line, so no still alone went over a second in fifteen plays. What a still alone takes there is still not known as a figure: `last.still_ms` is not in `GET /api/status`.
  - Fade out, then a tap with Crossfade chosen: the play answered in 0.19 s (no still is taken from a dark screen); that the picture then rises from black was not seen.
  - Blackout 0.25 s after a tap, so during the screenshot itself: answered in 0.25 s (X12's open number). Stop at the same moment: answered in 0.26 s and nothing played after (X11).
  - Afterwards `/run/pvj/web` held only its three own files; the player did not restart.

## 2026-10-09, the evening (#113 after its independent review: the queue of wishes, a floor under the guard)

Branch `effect-over-shader`, pull request #113, still a draft. The read found nothing above medium; D74 has "After the independent review". `transitions` was merged in first (4c1ee53): it holds the cure for the stress test that failed here in one unit job of four on three pushes. That test ran its clock ten times fast and still held the one-second limit for a still, so a still had a tenth of a second of real time; the limit is out of that test's way now, and the second stress test (an effect beside everything) holds every give-up again. **Nothing was run on hardware.**

- **M2, a controller's wish dropped at a Vibes step.** A wish carried the player's epoch; a rotation's step moves it; the worker's put came back empty and nothing was said. Now it carries `Player.clears`, which only a clearing of the screen moves. And the queue had the fault found three times that day in the shaders' queue: Next named its effect from a reading taken at the press, so a second Next made while the worker held the first named the same effect. A step is now a move; steps that wait add up; the worker resolves them when it runs. A wish no longer moves the engine's generation (it did, and the job in flight was taken off again by the wish behind it); Off and an effect by hand do. A wish that comes to nothing says so in `error`, with a `kind` so the card does not say "The GPU refused" for a Stop.
- **M3, no floor under the suspended guard.** `PairGuard` counts the pair's dropped frames across the shaders under the effect; at 2 a second over 20 seconds the effect comes off, the shader stays, nothing is marked. The numbers are guesses and are named at the top of `pvj/effects.py`.
- **Lows:** the refused pair is remembered by the pair (it was remembered by "a shader is under it", which refused the effect over every generator); `about()` claims no unnamed complaint while both are in the player; a generator's arrival asks the effect's worker to look at once; the size is said while the GPU still looks at a new generator; `pvj/MIDI.md` says what the light does (lit while the player runs).
- **Tests.** `QueueTest` (8): 2, 3 and 5 steps, mixed steps, On then a preset, a step then a preset, Off and an effect by hand, in the five windows, made on one thread by hooks that call the real methods; a clip, a generator and a rotation's step in between; a Stop in between; twelve bursts against the real worker thread. `OverShaderTest` has six more (the floor three ways, the size during a look, the unnamed complaint, the look on arrival). Twenty breakages on the committed tree, each caught; two tests did not catch theirs at first (the size test, since an effect already on kept its size anyway, and the threaded test, since the worker was too quick for steps to pile up) and were made to.
- **Chosen differently from the review's words:** `about()` refuses an unnamed stretch only while both shaders are in the player, not always: alone, an unnamed complaint can only be one's own, and a player that printed no text would otherwise never be heard. A browser test of a refused pair's wording was not written: that test's player draws nothing.
- **Open:** rows E1 to E16; the two guessed numbers; whether a start of ambience should take an effect off at once.

**Later the same evening, a second independent read (another model): nothing high, one medium.** A pair's refusal leaked into the whole library: `put` wrote the file's own refusal also when a generator was under the effect, so the row said "refused" over a clip and Next passed the effect by everywhere. Now the file's refusal is written only with no generator under it, the row says a pair's refusal of the pair and only while that generator plays, and the worker's Next leaves it out only then. The two that shaped the code: the player's word about the picture is the carrier's (64 x 36) while the effect meets the generator's drawing, and under a cap below the generator's lines the generator is softened.
- **The places that assumed a generator takes the effect off or refuses it**, found by grep before any was changed and written into the pull request afterwards, with what became of each: `Player.play_source` and `put_effect`; `Effects.available`, `_blocked`, `GENERATOR_HAS_IT`, the reasons in `ENDED`, the module's docstring; `Engine.show`'s sweep of the effect's text; the MIDI lights' "ready"; the status for a carrier; the panel's rule line, its Now playing line and two lines of words; seven tests; `pvj/SHADERS.md`, `docs/MANUAL.md`, `pvj/MIDI.md`, HANDOFF.
- **Built**: D74 has each choice. In short: the effect stays through a generator, Vibes and its steps and dips; Stop and stopping Vibes take it off; a refused pair leaves the generator, says why, and is not sent again; each engine hears only its own refusals in the player's log; Vibes judges no shader while an effect is on; the panel's size line is the generator's drawing.
- **Found on the way**: the effect's text file stayed in the runtime folder after Vibes was stopped (a test of the new behaviour said so; `Engine.off` now sweeps it); the buffers' format would have been set again for every effect put on over a generator (a hitch on a board, by what #92 found for generators); and the two listeners on one log (LESSONS).
- **Tests.** `tests/test_effects.py`: `OverShaderTest` (14) and three in `PlayerLayerTest`, against the stand-in for mpv, which says of a carrier what the real one said in the spike. The first fifteen were run against the code as it was before (all fail), and each mechanism was broken by itself on the committed tree and its test seen to fail (the pull request has the table). A first version of the guard's test replaced `Guard.sample`, which takes a lock, with a function that takes none; an independent read caught it, and the test now drives the real guards of both engines on a clock of its own. `tests/test_pair_gpu.py`: nine tests of the pair through the engines on the real mpv in all three ways of drawing, and one with the player's default scalers; `tests/test_transitions_gpu.py` has a crossfade out of a generator under an effect; the browser test follows the pair on Live and on the card, at 320 px too. The stress test of #111 has a second test, `Stress.test_with_an_effect_beside_everything_an_operator_can_do`: the first test's rounds with one or two actions of an effect added to each (on, off, the next one), and one more thing that must hold: the player is never given the effect before the generator. In a run of its 250 rounds an effect went on about 130 times and the player had a generator and an effect together about 55 times. Its stand-in for mpv (`tests/fakempv.py`) now says what a real one said of the picture. The first test's rounds are as they were, so its mutation table stands; the table has a new row r (the effect before the generator in the list: caught by the second test three times of three). Why a second test and not three more actions in the first: see Slips.
- **What I could run on the Mac**: the whole unit suite (`python3 -m unittest discover -s tests`, 1905 tests; see Slips). Not the GPU tests and not the browser test: CI is the judge for those. The GPU tests of the pair passed in all three `effects-gpu` jobs at their first run (run 37948241791, 40 tests each, about 15 minutes of a job's 30).
- **Slips**: the effect's actions were first put into the stress test's own rounds. All four unit jobs of that push ran them; in one a round with two more threads took a still longer than the box allows (a tenth of a second on that test's fast clock) and failed with "the box gave up on transitions". It had passed about fifteen runs on the Mac. The actions now have rounds of their own, where a give-up is counted and printed and not held (those rounds are about order). On the push after that, one of four unit jobs failed again, this time in the first test's own, unchanged rounds ("the still took 1.2 seconds", seed 20261009 round 1), right after the new test had run before it in the same process; the new test itself printed one give-up in its 250 rounds in that job. Whether that was the new test's leftovers, the runner, or a rate this test always had is not known: the branch underneath shows no such failure in the runs looked at (four green push runs). The new test now runs after the first one and stops its worker when it ends, so the first test runs as it did before this branch. **A give-up in the new test's rounds is not a failure, so a change that made every crossfade give up while an effect goes on would pass it**: a bound on that count is the next thing to add. While looking at that run I took the three unit jobs still running after 23 minutes for a hang, because the same job had ended in 13 on the push before, and cancelled the pull request's run to read their logs: there was no hang. The job goes on to two GPU suites after the unit tests, and it had ended early before only because a unit test failed. The first push of the build failed two checks. The browser test read `working.under` before the worker's look had come (it comes once a second; the test now waits for it). And `tests/test_controllers.py` still expected a controller's effect button and its Next to be refused with 409 over a generator: I had run the suites I thought were touched, not all of them, and CI ran the rest. The whole suite was run on the Mac after that (the only failures left are the Mac's own, in `test_install`, `test_netd`, `test_update` and `test_release`). The task named `pvj/EFFECTS.md`; there is no such file, the effects' notes are the "Effects" part of `pvj/SHADERS.md`, and that is where this went. A first draft of the notes said what a generator costs a Pi 4 from memory; it was taken out before the commit, since nothing of the pair was measured.
- **Open, for a board**: the rows E1 to E14, "An effect over a generator (D74)", in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`: the frame time of pairs at 540 and 720 lines, what the effect's guard says, whether Vibes keeps its hands off, whether a generator chosen under an effect ever shows a flash. **Open, for a decision**: whether a heavy pair should be taken off or stepped down by itself (today it is only said), and whether a controller's wish that meets a Vibes step should be kept instead of dropped.

## 2026-10-09, the afternoon (#111 on the test Pi 4 for the first time: every crossfade was given up, and why)

The owner said "deploy to the Pi" in the session. At about 14:01 UTC the box's schema 15 settings were copied to `/root/pvj-backups/settings-schema15-ndi-2026-10-09.json`, the schema 14 copy of 2026-10-08 was put back, and `transitions` at `fcd6bd0` was installed through `pvj-dev` with `install.sh --offline` (five files compared by hash, the five services active, `pvj-selftest` with no failed check, the panel answering 200). The deploy restarted the panel and the player. A full-access device `claude-transitions-test` was paired with the box's PIN to drive the API from the box (its token is in `/home/pvj-dev/.transitions-test-token`); it is to be revoked when the trial ends. The NDI test's device is gone with the schema 15 settings. **Nobody looked at the monitor**; all of the following is from the API, the journal and timings over SSH.

- **The first crossfade on a board was the last.** Screen 2560 x 1440, two clips ("Lumia 1.mp4", "Lumia 2.mp4"), Crossfade, 2 s. The first play answered after 1.98 s and the journal said "transitions given up: the still took 1.7 seconds"; from then on `mix.fallback` was set and every play was a dip, as designed. No "a cut instead" line: this mpv (0.40) writes the still as the code expects.
- **Where the 1.7 seconds went** (measured beside the panel, three times each): the player's `screenshot-to-file` 0.60 to 0.65 s; `read_still` 1.23 to 1.33 s. Of `read_still`, 0.98 s was the unpacking: this mpv writes the still as 2701 pieces of 4 KB, and the code called the unpacker once per piece with the whole picture as its bound, which makes room for the whole picture each time. One bounded call for all pieces took 0.06 s. The rows' conversion took 0.30 s through views and 0.07 s through plain bytes. One step of the fade (scaling and writing 14.7 MB) took about 0.05 s, so twenty steps a second are within reach.
- **Fixed on the branch, not yet on the box:** `read_still` joins the pieces and unpacks once with one bound, and steps through the rows as plain bytes. Peak memory measured again: 2.25 times the picture, as before. A test writes a still in many small pieces. The second deploy, with this fix, was refused by the session's permission check and was not worked around; the box still runs `fcd6bd0` and dips.
- **What stays:** the player's own 0.6 seconds for a 2560 x 1440 screenshot. That is the freeze before a blend at this size, with about 0.15 s on top. It was 0.5 to 0.6 s whatever the PNG settings (compression 0 or 1, filter on or off), and 3.7 to 4.2 s with mpv's defaults. On a 1920 x 1080 screen it should be about half; not measured.
- **Seen on the dips meanwhile** (so of the fallback, not of the crossfade): Blackout during a change answered in 0.05 s, Stop in 0.1 s, a play followed at once by Stop left nothing playing, and `/run/pvj/web` held no `transition-*` and no `overlay-63.bgra` afterwards. During the one real attempt `overlay-63.bgra` was there for about a second and then gone.
- **With the fix on the box** (the owner said "deploy to the Pi" again; `cfcacc5` installed at 14:28 UTC the same way, the installed `pvj/transitions.py` equal by hash, five services active; the panel and the player restarted). Same screen and clips, nobody at the monitor:
  - Crossfade, 2 s, four times: no "given up", no "a cut instead", `mix.fallback` empty throughout. A play answered after 0.93 to 1.09 s, which is the time the old picture stands still before the blend. That is close to the 1.0 second after which the box gives up (the limit counts the still alone, which is less than the answer; how much less was not measured), so the margin at this screen size is thin.
  - How long a change lasts, watched through the still's file in `/run/pvj/web` every 20 ms: asked 2 s, gone after 3.04 s (crossfade) and 2.99 s (wipe from left); asked 4 s, 5.15 and 5.01 s. So about one second of standing still, then the time asked for.
  - Wipe from left and Slide up, 2 s: ran, same answers. A 5 s wipe ten times in a row: every play answered in 0.89 to 0.97 s, the player did not restart (`NRestarts=0`), no overlay or SIGBUS line in its journal.
  - Blackout 0.7 s into a crossfade answered in 0.08 s; Blackout right after a play (answered 0.91 s) in 0.09 s, and the clip played on under it. Stop 0.7 s into a crossfade answered in 0.11 s and nothing played after; a play followed at once by Stop: Stop answered 0.14 s after the play's 0.97 s, nothing played after.
  - Afterwards `/run/pvj/web` held only `netd.sock`, `pin` and `player.sock`.
  - This covers what a script can see of X2, X3, X4, X10, X11, X12, X14 and X16. Whether the blend is smooth, whether the edge of a wipe moves evenly, whether anything stayed lit under Blackout, and how a second of standing still looks, are for eyes.
- **Open:** every check that needs eyes or hands (X1, X5 to X9, X13, X15, X17 to X23), and the margin under `SLOW` at 2560 x 1440.

## 2026-10-09, the morning (the sixth review of #111: a deadlock, and the order of the locks handed to a machine)

Branch `transitions`, pull request #111, still a draft. The sixth independent read found a deadlock that the fifth round's own fixes had made (Stop against a step of Vibes' own dip), a Blackout that waited behind a load, and a live input's helper that survived the player's restart. All answered; D71 has "After the sixth review". **Nothing was run on hardware.**

- **The method changed.** Every lock of these paths is made in `pvj/locks.py`, which holds the one written order. In the tests each lock knows its place and fails the test at a taking out of order (`tests/lockrank.py`), in one thread, without the interleaving that hangs. Put in before the fix, it failed an old single-threaded test of a Vibes tick. It then found nothing else in the 800 tests of the modules on these paths, apart from one test that took two locks in its own order.
- **The fix is the order**, not a patch: the player's lock stands before the level's; Vibes' step takes them in that order; nothing waits for the player while it holds the level's lock, so a Blackout waits for one write of the level and no more (measured against the stand-in: under half a second behind a load of two seconds, also during a Vibes dip, a ramp and a crossfade).
- **The real player is under test.** `tests/fakempv.py` stands in for mpv on the socket; the stress test and the class `RealPlayer` drive `pvj/player.py` itself. The fakes of the other tests take a lock where the real ones do.
- **Found by the stress test once the real player was in it:** a player that restarts shows at full brightness whatever the mix says, so a clip could be lit under a Blackout (older than the branch). The watcher that puts the overlay and the mapping back puts the level back now.
- **Also:** `Player.quit`, and `pipe_playing` that knows which process it was loaded into; Vibes' dip leaves an operator's Fade out alone; Fade out takes the fader before the lock; Reset's opacity inside the lock; a dip's way down only if nobody took the fader since the tap.
- **A mistake of the session's own:** adding ten kinds of action to the stress test halved what it caught of the old faults (the Blackout switch outside its lock went from three runs of three to none) until the common actions were weighted and the switch was also checked inside each wish's own step. The mutation table is what showed it; it is run again whenever the test changes.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_transitions.py` has 134 (twelve of them on the real player), `tests/test_lockrank.py` 12 for the checker itself. The mutation table has seventeen rows: fourteen are caught by the stress test alone in three runs of three, fifteen by a test written by hand, one hangs the hand-written run, and one is an equivalent mutation and is said to be. Twenty runs of the unbroken code passed, one after the other on a machine that did nothing else; 250 rounds take about twenty seconds now, because the real player and a socket are in them. (Corrected after the seventh review: that was no measure of the code under load. The reviewer saw one failure in nine with other suites beside it, and the same code failed eight of eighteen here once three runs went at once beside two suites: a real fault, the entry above. And the mutation called equivalent was not.)

- **High:** a Vibes rotation could beat the operator's tap. The screen's claim and the ticket are one step under the player's lock now; a rotation after a tap is refused by its epoch and never counts.
- **Medium:** a Stop during a dip puts the picture's level back; a clip that is overtaken no longer stops the live input that overtook it.
- **The dip, older than this branch:** a Blackout, a fade, a Reset or an opacity change during the way down no longer drops the tapped clip; it loads as a cut at the level that was set.
- **Lows:** `abandon` is not an end; a freeze is handed on and always thawed by somebody; Next and Previous are newer wishes; a play that was overtaken says so in its answer; a play that fails stays the newest wish (stated).
- **Tests** (`tests/test_transitions.py`, 102 now): the fake player has the real one's lock, epoch and `claim_screen` and notes any change made without the lock; the generator test drives the real `Engine.show`; a rotation is parked exactly between the tap's ticket and its claim; the five actions during a dip; a Stop during and after the way down; a live input started during a still; an older play that finds a newer one waiting; unique names in the flood; the sleeps that guessed are events. Tried against the old ordering after a commit: six fail there.
- **Still three locks and three counters**, with a table in the class's docstring.

## 2026-10-09, the evening (the second review of #111: ordering)

Branch `transitions`, pull request #111, still a draft. A second independent read found the pictures and the arithmetic sound and the ordering weak: four medium findings and some low. All answered; D71 has "After the second review". **Nothing was run on hardware.**

- **One play generation**, taken by `Api.play` in the caller and looked at for the last time together with the load under the player's own lock, so no window is left between the look and the load for anything that plays or stops through `pvj/player.py`. Every way of playing, Stop (also a sync server's), a generator and the player's restart are newer wishes; a play that is overtaken loads nothing and touches nothing. An `end()` while a play waits its turn takes its blend and leaves the brightness alone. Of a flood of plays the last one wins with one transition.
- **A crossfade step scales the still once** again. `abandon` and the thaw belong to a hold's own token. A player that goes away during the still does not make the box give up. A command the player did not answer is followed by one more removal five seconds on.
- **Tests** (`tests/test_transitions.py`, 85 now): the thread tests no longer count steps in real time or assert on a background thread; a thread's start can be held back (a stand-in for `Thread` in `pvj/api.py`). New: a Stop handled before the controller's thread starts; each other way of playing during a still; five controller plays queued in three orders, one still, the last clip; a flood on real threads; an end while a play waits; a wipe and a slide on 41 x 23 to the last column and row. Tried against the old ordering first: six of them fail there. The main guard is at the end of the file.
- **Settled without a board:** `overlay-add` copies the pixels during the command (mpv's manual, and seen on 0.37 in the spike). **Open:** a command that timed out and is run later (D71; X17), and everything a board has to say.

## 2026-10-09 (crossfade: a spike, a decision, and the first transition, as a draft)

Branch `transitions`, a draft pull request to master, based on `product-leftovers` (#108). The owner asked on 2026-10-08 to put crossfade and other transitions first and was away; the choices were made here and are in D71. **Nothing was run on hardware; the test Pi was not available.**

- **The spike.** Branch `transitions-spike` (no pull request; left for the owner to delete), two rounds through the three `effects-gpu` jobs with the effects tests' module replaced by a script that prints. mpv 0.37.0 on Mesa's software GPU. Round 1 asked the five questions about a still in a shader; round 2 measured the overlay again, because round 1 had looked at a pixel the still did not cover (LESSONS). The numbers are in D71. In one sentence: the texture is cheap, and nothing else about the shader holds (no clock, no still without the mapping, no covering the screen), while a still of the window as the player's overlay is right in every case tried.
- **Built.** `pvj/transitions.py` (new), `Player.still`, the play path and `_settle()` in `pvj/api.py`, the import check in `pvj/boxcare.py`, one call each in `pvj/shaders.py`, `pvj/mapper.py` and `pvj/server.py`, and one option in the panel's picker. Crossfade for a file or a pad played over something that plays. What it does with Blackout, Freeze, Stop, a second play, Vibes, sync, a crash and a rollback is in D71.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_transitions.py` (46, a player that records): the order of the calls, the steps and their pacing on a clock of the test's own, every way it ends, the first frame waited for, a restart and a crash in the middle, each limit and its fallback, the settings' form, the still's reader against files that are not what it expects (a small file that unpacks to 100 MB among them). `tests/test_transitions_gpu.py` (12, a real mpv, in the three `effects-gpu` jobs through `tests/test_effects_gpu.py`): the still is the screen, also with an effect, with a shader at the mapping's stage and over a generator; it stays whole until a late pipe's first frame; the picture goes from old to new through mixes and never back, to a clip and to a still picture, through the panel's own play; Blackout; a frozen clip; the real mapper; an effect put on in the middle; the first play after Stop; a player that dies. Two more in `tests/test_boxcare.py`, a few lines in the browser test. Locally only the touched modules were run (`TMPDIR=/tmp/trans`); the whole suite and the GPU tests are CI's.
- **Found on the way.** The step loop spun without the clock moving (LESSONS). The first-frame sign is `seeking`, not `time-pos`. The outgoing clip has to be frozen before the still, or it jumps back to it. A second read of the work pointed at three things CI cannot show and the tests had modelled wrongly: on a box the player always runs, so "nothing playing" has to be asked of the player (`idle-active`), not of whether it runs; a Blackout must not wait behind a still that is being taken; and whether the player may write into the panel's folder on a box is not proven by a run where both are one account.
- **Open.** Everything a board has to say: the list "Crossfade (D71)" in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md`, above all the time for the still and the steps a second at 1920 x 1080 and 2560 x 1440. The limits in `pvj/transitions.py` are guesses until then. Wipe, slide and a luma dissolve are the same mechanism and not built. A scaled overlay (mpv 0.40) is not built because CI cannot test it. ISF transition files stay out of reach on this output.

Pull request #102, branch `workspace-shell`. Not merged: the owner looks at the shell first. He had looked, and said three things: "in the Workspace layout, on the narrow layouts, the text is overrunning", then "primarily in the main headers", then "on the smaller window size the transport buttons (Freeze, Stop and Blackout) are shoved down a line".

- **How it was looked at.** The dev Mac has no Playwright and no mpv. `PVJ_HARNESS_NO_PLAYER=1 python3 tests/ui/harness.py` served the panel; headless Edge was driven over the DevTools protocol by a scratch script outside the repository (Node's own WebSocket, nothing installed). The box was given long names by its own API (a clip `A_very_long_clip_name_without_any_spaces_2026-10-09_final_v3.mp4`, pads, a projector, walls, scenes, a stream, devices, a mapping), and what a player would say was put into the answers on their way to the page (a clip of a playlist playing an hour in, a long shader and effect name, long presets, a USB drive, the board "Raspberry Pi 4 Model B" with a temperature). Every screen of the owner's menu (29), at 320, 360, 390, 414, 480, 600 and 768 px and 740 by 360, in the default look and in Signal, each also with every text a quarter larger (the font sizes multiplied in the page, since the stylesheet sets them in px and the browser's default font size moves nothing) and, in the default look, in a wider typeface (Verdana, as CI's Linux faces are wider than the Mac's): 1520 looks, each measured by the boxes of every line of text. Before: 1768 faults of 29 kinds. After: 38, every one of them a plain word cut in two with the text a quarter larger (one in the wider face at 320 px), listed below.
- **What overran, and what was done** (all in `pvj/web/app.css`, and one line of `app.js`).
  - *A page's title* (the owner's "main headers"), in Signal, 360 to 414 px: "PROJECTOR" and "S" on two lines beside the switch, "MIDI CONTROLLE" and "R", "PROJECTIO" and "N MAPPING". The stylesheet meant the switch to go under the title first (`min-width: min-content`), but the same look had `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on every heading, and with that the least width of a title is one letter. Now `overflow-wrap: break-word` (which does not change the least width), and the title's first width is its own text: a title that does not fit beside the switch has the line and the switch is under it, at the right. The area's name in the bar likewise.
  - *The title bar under 900 px.* The line that says the screen's job was in one box with the area's name, and the status beside that box squeezed it: three lines in a column of 192 to 234 px at 414, 480 and 600 px, with the status wrapped under it at 600. Now the name and the status share the first line (the status goes under the name when there is no room; the name is never narrowed), and the job has a line of its own, as wide as the bar. At 740 by 360 the bar of the screen with the longest job is 55 px high (58 in Signal) where it was 72 (74).

  - *Names with no space in them widened the page:* a preset of an effect (394 px of page in a 320 px window), a preset of a shader in Signal, the name of a USB drive, a projector's button and a group's name on Setup > Room in larger text. They are cut where their box ends now.

- **The strip is one line from 600 px.** Measured first, as it was: with nine buttons it was two rows of buttons from 600 to 1150 px (1100 in Signal), three rows with the line above them from 600 to 750 (700), and the ones sent down were Stop and Blackout (600 to 750 and 1050 to 1150 px; in Signal 600 to 700 and 1000 to 1100; at the left of a row of their own) or Freeze with them (in between). The owner had seen the panel, not a mock. Now, by the panel's width: from 1200 px everything, as before; from 1000 px what plays, the place in the clip, More, Previous, Next, Freeze, Stop, Blackout; from 800 px without the place in the clip; from 600 px what plays, More, Freeze, Stop, Blackout. More is the phone's More and opens what is folded in a second line under the first. At every width from 600 to 1366 px (step 50), closed, in both looks, in the wider face and with larger text: one line, Blackout ending 16 px from the right edge, the name at least 180 px wide. Under 600 px nothing changed (Previous, Next, Stop, Blackout; Freeze behind More): that is what D65 decided and the owner has a larger change to the strip planned. No control was added, removed or renamed.
- **A wide window.** Every box of every screen at 1280 by 800 in both looks was written down before and after: 58 of 58 screens the same, but for the strip's line of what plays (the name over the count and the time, where it had been a letter beside them).
- **The check that stays** (`tests/ui/shell.js`, called from `panel.test.js` in the shell's step). `narrow()`: every screen at 320, 390 (each also with More open), 600 and 768 px and 740 by 360, in both looks, with long names put into the box's answers by `page.route` (nothing is stored, so no later step meets them): the page does not scroll sideways; no text leaves its own box, a box that holds it or the window, or is clipped; a line cut with dots has its whole text in a title; no two texts lie on one another; a title is not cut inside a plain word; and from 600 px the strip is one line with Freeze, Stop and Blackout on it, in Signal too. A failure names the look, the screen, the size, the element and its text. `strip()` now holds the one line at 600, 667 by 375, 768, 799, 800, 999, 1000, 1199, 1200 and 1366 px: which buttons are shown, Freeze, Stop and Blackout at the right end, More opening the rest at 768 px with those three where they were. `reach()` asks for the buttons a width keeps. One existing step followed (the fill of the place in the clip at 768 px opens More first).
- **Run where.** Here: `reach`, `tabWalk`, `menus`, `strip` and `narrow` through the DevTools shim (which was given `route` for this), all passing (narrow: 406 looks in 20 s), and both failing against the stylesheet before the change (strip: two lines at 600; narrow: 352 faults); the width sweep's own `measure` on every screen in both looks, with nothing new. When this was first written Playwright had run none of it. **CI then ran it twice.** The first run (1394e5d) failed before the new steps, in the width sweep: Signal at 320 px, Room > Walls, "a word is broken: Main wall: Console (102 px in 101)". The cause was this change: to hold a long preset name, every button of a row that wraps had been allowed to be cut anywhere, and a button that may be cut anywhere has no least width, so the row of sources squeezed where it had wrapped. The rule is now for the buttons that carry a typed name alone (an effect's presets, a group's projectors). The same line had been on the Mac's screen, with the long wall name of the test room in it, and was put aside as "a long name"; it was not. The second run (2d052d3) passed: `text at narrow widths: 406 looks at every screen with long names, in both looks, nothing overran (21.9 s)`, and the shell's step with the strip's one line held, in Playwright's Chromium with CI's typefaces and a real player. The unit tests: 1621 run, 30 fail on this Mac (`test_install`, `test_update` and `test_release` on `tar --sort` and `mv -T`, which are GNU's; four socket tests of `test_netd`; one DMX test of `test_control`, whose port the harness held). They were not run without the change. None of them reads the panel's files, and the six test files that do read them pass.

## 2026-10-08, the night (the fourth CI run of the shell: nine controls "lost" that were not)

Pull request #102, branch `workspace-shell`. Master was merged in first (b13b858, #104's change of mirror; the journal keeps both sides).

**The failure (2230d59, job `panel-ui`).** The slider step passed and the test reached the control inventory for the first time anywhere under Playwright. It reported nine controls of the owner as lost: `#dmxproto`, `#dmxuni`, `#dmxstart`, `#dmxallow`, `#dmxsave`, `#oscport`, `#oscallow`, `#oscsave` and the button "Remove local". The same line listed as new `#sysswitchon` and "Remove inventory", and that was the clue.

**The cause: the check's own set-up, not the shell. Nothing was unreachable.**
- *DMX and OSC (8 keys).* The inventory starts a second box of the harness (no player) beside the first, which by then listens for DMX on UDP 6454 and for OSC on its port. `signal.setUp` asks the second box to switch both on; the box cannot open the ports, puts its settings back and answers 409 ("cannot listen on UDP 6454: Address already in use"). `post` in `tests/ui/signal-pages.js` does not look at the answer. A Setup page whose flag is off shows "Switch on ..." (`#sysswitchon`) in place of its card, so the cards' fields were "not in the page". In CI's log the last `POST /api/dmx` and `POST /api/osc` of the run are the only two that answered 409. The fixture was made with one box running.
- *"Remove local" (1 key).* The browser test paired the owner's device as "inventory" (the run lists "Remove inventory" as new), so the fixture's owner was called "local": inferred from that swap, the scratch driver that made the fixture is not in the repository. The same row of People and codes under another name.
- It is not a matter of Linux. **Reproduced on the dev Mac** without a browser: two `tests/ui/harness.py` side by side, each paired over HTTP; the first answered 200 to both, the second 409 to both with the words above, and `GET` gave `enabled: false`. With DMX and OSC switched off on the first, the second answered 200 and listened; after the second was stopped the first took them back (200, listening).

**What changed (tests only; `pvj/` is untouched).**
- `tests/ui/panel.test.js`, the inventory step: DMX and OSC are switched off on the first box if they are on, for the time of the inventory, and put back when the second box has exited (the test fails if the first box does not take them back). The owner pairs with `inventory.OWNER`.
- `tests/ui/inventory.js`: `OWNER` ("local") and `LISTENERS`; `prepare()` asks for MIDI, DMX and OSC once more after `setUp`, reads each back, and throws with the box's own reason if one is not on, and likewise if the owner's device is not called `OWNER`. So this fault says "DMX is not on (cannot listen on UDP 6454 ...)" at the start and not "controls lost" three minutes later. The state both lists are taken in is written out in the file's head.
- `tests/ui/shell.js` `inventory()`: the three roles are all compared before it fails. The presenter's and the guest's lists have never been compared in CI (the owner's failed first), so one run now names everything.
- The assertion and the fixture are as they were; no key was excused.

**Run here.** `node --check` on the three files. The two-box sequence above against the real harness (`TMPDIR=/tmp/ws102`, removed after). No unit test module was touched, none was run.

**Not run here, so not known.** The browser test itself: the Mac has no Playwright and no mpv. Whether the inventory now passes in CI for the owner is expected and not seen; the presenter's and guest's comparisons under Playwright are a first run and may name differences of their own (they held in Edge on the Mac, 237 of 238 and 68 of 68, the one being `#nav-room`, which is excused with its reason).

**On the way.** The repository's volume (a disk image on the external drive) was unmounted for some minutes in the middle of this and came back intact; the merge commit was already made. Each step after that was pushed as soon as it was committed.

## 2026-10-08, the night (the review of #108: eight findings, all fixed on `product-leftovers`)

Branch `product-leftovers`, pull request #108, pushed, not merged. An independent read-only review of D70 found no high finding, one medium to low and seven low; all eight are answered. For 1 to 6 there is a fix and a test that was run against the code before the fix and failed there; 7 is about the tests and was tried against a deliberately broken `main()`; 8 is a correction of text. D70 is rewritten where behaviour changed. **Run on the development Mac only** (`tests.test_leftovers`, `tests.test_update_button`, `tests.test_update`; never the whole suite, the disk is short). The ten tests of `tests.test_update.UpdaterTest` that run `install.sh` cannot run on the Mac (`install.sh` uses `mv -T`, as it did before this branch); they were seen failing so after this work, and CI runs them. **Nothing was tried on a box.**

- **1, `pvj-update check` made a work folder without the lock.** `main()` now takes the lock for `check` too, and it sweeps like the others. Test: `CommandLine.test_a_check_takes_the_lock_too_because_it_makes_a_work_folder`.
- **2 and 6, the lock was in `/run/lock`, where any account can make a name, and was used unseen; a missing `/run/lock` was made with mode 755.** The lock is now `/run/pvj-update/update.lock` (`update.DEFAULT_LOCK`, in `paths.UPDATE_DIR`); `take_lock` refuses a folder its group or others can write and anything that is not a plain file with one name; the old name (`update.OLD_LOCK`) is taken as well by `_old_lock`, best effort, only where the lock is the default one, and its folder is never made. `take_lock` now returns a small holder (`_Held`) whose `close()` lets go of both. Tests: eight in `Lock`, from `test_the_lock_is_in_the_update_units_folder_which_they_make_for_root_alone` down, and `test_the_default_lock_takes_the_old_name_without_being_asked` for what `main()` gets without naming anything. `test_a_lock_that_cannot_be_opened_is_an_error_not_another_file` now asserts "nothing but the lock was opened" instead of "exactly the lock was opened", because a folder that cannot be used is refused before any open. "Somebody else's file at the old name" is made by saying we are somebody else (`os.geteuid` patched); a file that really is another account's needs root.
- **3, an unusable lock was never said to the panel.** `main()` writes it to the result file as failed. Test: `CommandLine.test_without_a_usable_lock_there_is_no_update_and_the_panel_is_told_why` (the old test asserted the opposite), and `test_with_the_lock_held_elsewhere_the_result_is_the_running_updates`.
- **4, the age test trusted the clock.** `paths.remove_leftovers` counts a time more than the age ahead of the clock as old. Tests: `RemoveLeftovers.test_a_file_from_the_future_is_a_leftover_too`, and a line in `SettingsLeftovers.test_loading_removes_the_temp_file_of_a_save_that_a_power_cut_ended`. What it cannot do is in D70.
- **5, a linked install was swept by nobody.** The session that coordinates this work decided not to refuse the update there (a box that could update before must still), so `Updater.sweep_scratch` resolves the install folder, opens it, and sweeps only if it is a real folder of root's that group and others cannot write; the same check now holds for an install that is not a link. Tests: three in `ScratchSweep`, from `test_an_install_folder_that_is_a_link_is_swept_where_it_really_is`. A folder that really is somebody else's needs root to make; that case is the default owner seen by somebody who is not root.
- **7, the tests.** `test_it_is_the_same_for_root_and_for_anybody_else` is gone (it patched a function `take_lock` did not call). New class `EndToEnd`: the real `main()` with a real `Updater` in a folder of its own and the default owner. One test shows a folder that is not root's is left; the other tells `lstat` and `fstat` that everything is root's and sees it removed. Only a run as root removes a folder that really is root's. Tried against `main()` passing the caller as owner: four tests fail.
- **8, D70's reason for the hour was wrong.** Only `pvj/server.py` makes a `Settings`. Corrected in D70, in the comment in `Settings.load` and in a test's message.
- **CI on the head before this (b87f348):** `test (3.12)` and `effects-gpu (desktop OpenGL)` both ended in the install step with "ci-apt: gave up after 3 attempts"; no test ran. The mirror change of #104 came in with the merge of master.
- **Open, noted by the review outside the diff, not fixed here:** `previous` in the install is written with a plain `open("w")` (`_restore_previous` and `_switch_to` in `pvj/update.py`), so a power cut can leave it empty and a rollback then finds no previous release; and `install.sh` can leave `releases/<v>.old.<pid>`, which D70's list of leftovers does not include and nothing removes.
- **Also open:** the lock's new place, `fs.protected_regular` and the read-only root mode were not looked at on the test Pi; the first update from a release with the old lock to one with the new is the case the old name is held for, and it has only been run in tests. The volume the repository is on went away once during this session for a reason nobody found; commits were pushed one finding at a time after that.

**Later the same night: a second independent read, of the fixes only.** No high and no medium finding, six low, all answered on the same branch. Run on the development Mac only, the same three modules; nothing on a box.

- **1 and 5, the sweep removed by path.** `Updater.sweep_scratch` opens each work folder through the opened install without following a link, looks at what it opened, empties it with `os.fwalk(dir_fd=)` and removes it by name in the opened install; `rmtree`, the path re-check and `samestat` are gone. Tests in `ScratchSweep`: `test_an_install_whose_path_leads_elsewhere_after_the_look_is_emptied_where_it_was_looked_at` (failed before: the tree put at the old path was removed), `test_a_link_inside_a_work_folder_goes_as_a_link_and_what_it_points_to_stays`, `test_a_work_folder_swapped_for_a_link_after_the_listing_is_not_followed` (these two passed before as well: `rmtree` did not follow either), `test_a_work_folder_that_cannot_be_emptied_is_left_and_is_no_error`.
- **2, a held lock and a stale result.** `main()` writes failed, "another update or check is running", unless the result says "running". Test: `CommandLine.test_with_the_lock_held_by_something_that_writes_no_result_the_panel_is_told_why` (failed before). The message on the terminal changed with it.
- **3, whose the lock is.** `take_lock` requires the folder and the lock to be the effective user's. Tests: `Lock.test_a_lock_in_somebody_elses_folder_is_refused`, `Lock.test_a_lock_that_is_somebody_elses_file_is_refused` (both failed before). Somebody else's file needs root to make, so `stat` and `fstat` are made to say so for the one file or folder. The tests used to say "root" to everybody by patching `os.geteuid`; that would make every file of the test somebody else's, so `tests.test_update.root_for_main` now says it to `main()` only (it looks at the caller's name, which is a test's trick and no more).
- **4, a test that proved nothing on Linux.** `Lock.test_a_lock_that_cannot_be_opened_is_an_error_not_another_file` works in a folder of its own and asserts again that exactly the lock was opened. Tried against a `take_lock` without `O_NOFOLLOW`: it fails.
- **6, a comment.** The comment in `main()` about where the result is written, and the one in `tests/test_update_button.py`, now say what happens on a box; `CommandLineTest.test_a_rollback_from_a_terminal_replaces_the_last_update_line` also shows the lock's folder being made and the result written in it (this passed before; it tests the claim, not a fix).
- The two open items above stand. CI was still running on the branch when the work was handed back; it is not known to be green.

## 2026-10-08, the evening (what a power cut leaves on the box: five places in `pvj/`, D70)

Branch `product-leftovers`, a pull request to master, not merged: it touches the updater, which runs as root, and waits for its own review. Found while mending the tests' temp folders (#106) and confirmed by that pull request's independent review. D70 has the decisions. **Nothing here was tried on a box.** Run on the dev Mac: `tests.test_leftovers` (27 tests, 1.9 s, all pass) and the touched modules (`test_update`, `test_update_button`, `test_settings`, `test_themes`, `test_modules_themes`, `test_paths`, `test_theme_api`: 154 tests with the new ones, the same 10 errors of `test_update` as on master, all "mv: illegal option -- T", the installer on macOS). The whole suite was not run (the disk). Each repair was undone in turn and a test failed each time, except one equivalent change (see P5).

- **P1, `pvj/update.py`: an update's work folder after a power cut.** `Updater.sweep_scratch()`, called by `main()` under the lock. Tests: `ScratchSweep` (a planted folder goes and nothing else in the install; the name is the one `check()` really makes; a link named so is not followed and not removed; an install that is a link is not swept; a folder that is not root's stays; no install and a folder that cannot be removed are no error) and `CommandLine` (the sweep comes before the update and under the lock; with the lock held elsewhere nothing is removed; `status` does not sweep; a sweep that fails does not stop the update).
- **P2, `pvj/settings.py`: `.settings-XXXXXXXX`.** Removed by `Settings.load` when older than an hour. Tests: `SettingsLeftovers` (the old one goes, a fresh one stays, the settings and the backup are untouched; the pattern matches the name a save really uses; a first start with only a leftover starts).
- **P3, `pvj/shaders.py`, `pvj/effects.py`, `pvj/themes.py`: `.upload-<pid>-<thread>` and `.adding-<pid>-<thread>`.** Removed when the library is built. A leftover never counted toward `MAX_UPLOADS` or the sixteen themes: `_names()` only lists names that begin with a letter or a digit, and a test says so. The effects folder has the same upload and was not on the list; it is tidied too. Tests: `LibraryLeftovers`.
- **P4, `pvj/update.py`: the lock.** No fallback; `UpdateError` for root and for anybody else, a missing lock folder is made. Tests: `Lock` (a folder, a link or a file in the way is an error, and only the lock itself was tried; the same for root and others; a made folder still excludes; the default is the fixed file and the environment may name another) and `CommandLine.test_without_a_usable_lock_there_is_no_update_and_the_result_is_left_alone`.
- **P5, `pvj/update.py`: where the work folder is.** In the install, which is made if missing; never the system temp folder. Tests: `ScratchPlace`. Undoing only the `dir=` half (back to "the temp folder if the install is missing") changes nothing any more, because the install is made first; undoing the making fails two tests.
- **For the reviewer, the lines that carry the risk:** `shutil.rmtree(path)` in `sweep_scratch` (root deletes a tree: the checks before it are `islink(parent)`, the whole-name pattern, `lstat`, a real folder, owned by root); `os.makedirs(parent, mode=0o755, exist_ok=True)` in `check()` (root makes the install folder, also for the read-only `check` command from a terminal); `os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), ...)` in `take_lock` (root makes `/run/lock` where it is missing, mode 755 where Debian's is 1777); the `raise UpdateError` there (an update that used to run now does not: on a box where `/run/lock` cannot be used, which was not looked for on the test Pi); and `os.unlink(name, dir_fd=fd)` in `paths.remove_leftovers` (the panel's account removes files in `/var/lib/pvj`, by a pattern).
- **Not done:** moving the lock out of the world-writable `/run/lock` (D70 says why); a sweep at boot; anything in the installer.

The product is right. The test now lets each request ask again for two seconds; with the fix it tests taken out of `pvj/server.py` it still fails, which was run. ## 2026-10-08, later (the tests filled the Mac's disk; a run now removes what it makes)

Branch `tests-clean-tmp`, a pull request to master, not merged. D69 has the decision and the rule for new tests; LESSONS has how it was found. **Measured on the dev Mac (Python 3.14, no mpv, so the tests that play are skipped there). Linux is CI's to show.**

- **What a run left, measured.** One run of `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` on master with TMPDIR set to an empty folder, and `tempfile.mkdtemp`/`mkstemp` wrapped to note which line called: 1,502 folders were made and 1,447 were still there at the end, 24,171 files and folders in all, 215.7 MB. By bytes: `test_install.py` 94.6 MB in 39 folders and `test_update.py` 69.9 MB in 57 (copies of `pvj`, `bin` and `install`, 3 MB each), `test_server.py` 23.8 MB in 678 (the panel folder of every `ServerBase` test, which 28 other test files build on), `test_usb_media.py` 12.6 MB, `test_mapper.py` 8.6 MB, `test_netd.py` 2.1 MB in 134, and 33 more modules, 4 MB together (`test_support.py` 106 folders, `test_figma_theme.py` 40, `test_qr.py` 38). About 120 lines in 46 files make a temp folder. Fifty runs a day by several agents is the 72,000 folders of 2026-10-08.
- **What was built.** `tests/tmpguard.py`, called from `tests/__init__.py`: the process gets `<temp>/pvj-t-XXXXXX` (at first `pvj-test-XXXXXXXX`, see the review below), `TMPDIR` and `tempfile.tempdir` point at it (so the programs the tests start use it too), and it is removed at exit and on SIGTERM and SIGHUP; a Python started by a test finds `PVJ_TEST_TMP` and uses its parent's folder. At the start it removes folders of killed runs (two hours old and the process gone, or two days old). `tests/test_000_tmp.py` is the first module the plain `discover` loads and imports the package; it also holds the tests that keep this (17 then, 26 after the review). And the largest sites remove their own folders (`test_install`, `test_update`, `test_server`, `test_usb_media`, `test_mapper`), so a killed run leaves little; `test_run_folders` removes the one folder it made in `/tmp`, which no guard could see.
- **The same run afterwards.** With `PVJ_KEEP_TMP=1`, so that what the tests still leave inside the run's folder could be counted: the temp folder held one entry, the run's own folder, with 691 folders and 19.6 MB in it (13.3 MB of that the bundles of `test_update.py`, 2.1 MB `test_netd.py`); removing it with the guard's own function took 0.22 s and left the temp folder empty. So one run leaves: before 1,447 entries and 215.7 MB, after 0 and 0; a run killed at its very end: before the same, after one folder of at most 19.6 MB, which a later run removes. The full run was not repeated without `PVJ_KEEP_TMP` (the disk allowed two runs in all); that the folder goes at the end by itself is what the 17 new tests show on smaller runs. Run time: 614 s before (1,565 tests), 625 s after (1,582 tests; the 17 new ones take 11 s). The same 29 tests fail on the Mac before and after, by name (`test_install` 12, `test_update` 10, `test_netd` 4, `test_release` 3: tool differences of macOS), and 91 are skipped both times. The longest socket path a test bound went from 72 to 90 bytes in a temp folder as long as the Mac's own (49); the limit is 103.
- **One folder of the measuring is still in `/tmp`:** `/tmp/tmpw08jj73z` (empty folders and a link, from `test_run_folders.py` on master, the line that is now mended). It was left alone because the owner is clearing the temp folders himself.
- **The independent review, the same day, and what was mended.** It found no way to make the guard delete anything but a marked folder of the same user, and eight other things; each has a test that fails without the repair (checked by undoing each repair in turn), in `tests/test_000_tmp.py`, 26 tests now, 13.7 s.
  - High: two runs starting together both found the same old folder, and the second got `FileNotFoundError` out of `import tests` when the first had removed it halfway (a red run in CI, an unguarded one on a desk). `remove()` now catches everything the file system does, and `sweep()` carries on whatever one folder does.
  - Medium: the handler for SIGTERM did not end the process if the removal or its message failed (with `PVJ_KEEP_TMP=1` and nobody reading the errors, "kept: ..." raised and the run lived on). It now dies of the signal in a `finally`. And a `PVJ_TEST_TMP` left in the environment by a dead run was adopted as the folder, and a later run swept it from under the tests: it is used only while its maker is alive.
  - Low: a marker with a process number that is none (huge, text) raised; a relative `TMPDIR` on Python 3.9 made a relative folder that was not removed after a `chdir`; `chmod` in the unlocking followed a link; a folder whose process was alive went after two days (a paused run), now after fourteen (D69 says why not the start time); and the rule about `dir=` read lines, and now reads the syntax (`dir=` on a second line, `NamedTemporaryFile(dir=..., delete=False)`, `addClassCleanup`, and the cleanup has to name the folder). Run over the tests of `ndi-input`, `workspace-shell` and `panel-file-not-delivered`, it names one line in each: `test_run_folders.py`'s folder in `/tmp`, the one this branch mends.
  - Notes: the marker is read through a handle that does not follow a link; something mounted inside a run's folder is left; the folder is now `pvj-t-` and six characters, which takes the longest socket path from 94 to 89 bytes of 103 on the Mac; and a test says "the temp folder is N bytes too long for the sockets the tests make" where a long `TMPDIR` used to fail a dozen socket tests obscurely. `pvj/server.py`'s `main()` sets its own SIGTERM handler and would replace the guard's: no test calls it in the test process (looked for; `harness.py` calls `build`), so there was nothing to restore.
  - Not run again: the whole suite (the disk). The 26 tests and the modules they start ran in a temp folder of 11 bytes; the earlier measurement stands for the rest.
- **Not changed.** `.github/workflows/pvj.yml`: a runner is thrown away after the job, and the check that nothing is left runs inside the suite, where it also runs on a desk. `tests/ui/harness.py`: it imports `tests.test_server`, so it gets a folder like any run and the SIGTERM that ends it removes the folder; **that was not run here** (no mpv and no Playwright on the Mac), only the same mechanism in a small program. The shell tests: `security_test.sh` already removes its folder in a trap; `real_install_test.sh` leaves one empty file from `mktemp` on a runner that is thrown away.
- **`pvj/` itself was read for the same fault and has none in normal running.** Every temp file it makes is in the folder of the thing it will become (settings, an upload, an update's scratch folder beside the install, a shader text in the run folder) and is removed in a `finally`; uploads and shader texts left by a crash are swept at the next upload or start. The services run with `PrivateTmp=yes`. Left by a power cut and never swept: an update's `.update-XXXXXXXX` folder beside the install (the bundle and its unpacked tree, on the card, not in `/run`), an empty or half-written `.settings-XXXXXXXX` file in `/var/lib/pvj`, and a `.upload-<pid>-<thread>` file in the shader folder. Small and rare. Two more of the same kind: `update.take_lock` falls back to a `pvj-update.lock` in the temp folder when `/run/lock` cannot be used, one empty file that is never removed and does not grow; and `Updater.check` makes its scratch folder in the system temp folder when the install's parent folder does not exist (`dir=parent if os.path.isdir(parent) else None`), which on a box would put a bundle and its unpacked tree into the small `/tmp` and measure the free space there; on an installed box the parent exists. All written down here, none mended. **Read in the code, not tried on a box.**

## 2026-10-08 (the file that was not delivered: the cause, a longer queue, and a page that asks again)

Branch `panel-file-not-delivered`, D68. The question came from the shell's branch (#102), whose check of the roles found `room.js` with no answer about once in ten runs on the dev Mac and guessed at the server's queue.

- **How the server is built** (`pvj/server.py`): `PvjServer` is a `ThreadingHTTPServer`, one thread a connection, HTTP/1.0 (so no connection is ever used twice), a cap of 64 connections that answers 503 without starting a thread, 10 s for each read, a timer that ends any connection after 60 s (not an upload of a paired owner), and `request_queue_size` never set: socketserver's 5. A file of the page is read whole and sent; no lockout or rate limit is in its way.
- **The load script** (scratch, standard library only): N browsers, each asks for the page and then its nine files over six connections at once, 40 rounds, against `tests/ui/harness.py` started with a counting server class and no player. Requests that failed, of all asked, by listen queue:

  | browsers | 5 | 8 | 16 | 32 | 64 | 128 |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 | 0 of 400 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | 2 | 247 of 800 | 50 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | 3 | 493 of 1200 | 283 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | 6 | 1545 of 2400 | 1372 | 965 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

  Every failure was a reset, at connect or at the first read, within milliseconds. In every run the server counted 0 refusals at the cap and 0 handler errors; the slowest request took 71 ms and at most 22 threads ran at once. At 128: 8, 10, 12, 16 and 24 browsers (7200 requests, 144 connections asked for at once) lost nothing. With a pause of 0.3 s between a browser's rounds, three browsers at a queue of 5 still lost 158 of 1200. Asking for keep-alive: the same numbers, the server closes every connection.
- **The real browser.** Edge, headless, three separate contexts (each with its own six connections) paired as owner and loading the panel together. Queue 5: 90 page loads, 1640 requests, 85 failed (`ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`, a few `ERR_SOCKET_NOT_CONNECTED`): `app.js` 31, `room.js` 26, `shaders.js` 6, `effects.js` 5, the style sheets 5, `load.js` 4, API calls 7, the icon 1. Queue 128: 120 page loads, 1923 requests, none failed. (More often than the one in ten first seen, because here the three always start in the same instant.)
- **Silent connections.** Twelve that send nothing beside six browsers: no harm, 48 threads at most. Seventy-two (more than the cap): the cap refused 2278 connections as it should; the clients read 760 of them as 503 and 1510 as a reset. See the lesson.
- **What macOS does and what that says about Linux.** A listener that never accepts, backlog 5: 40 clients all connected; of 400, 111 were reset. `netstat -s` showed no overflow at any time. So macOS is loose about the number and resets what does not fit. Linux by default drops the handshake and the client tries again after a second; with that the overrun would be a wait on the box, not a missing file. Not measured: there is no Linux on this Mac, and the Pi was not touched.
- **The change to the server**: `request_queue_size = 128` on `PvjServer`, and the reason beside it. Nothing else.
- **The change to the panel**: `pvj/web/load.js` (new, first script in the head), `data-gives` on three script tags of `index.html`, one line in `app.js`. A file that reports `error` is asked for again after 0.4 s and after 1.5 s more; then the page says which file is missing and offers Reload. A script that arrives and leaves nothing is named as a fault of the file. With the queue at 5 in Edge and `load.js` in the page: 27 of the 90 page loads were made whole by asking again, 4 were not (`load.js` itself was among the files reset), and none of the others was left in part.
- **Tests.** `ManyAtOnceTest` in `tests/test_server.py`: six browsers at once all get every file; forty connections wait while the server is not taking any and are all answered; thirty connections past a cap of 3 are all refused at once and none is served; the queue is 128 and the cap 64. With the queue put back to 5 on the Mac: `{'200': 196, 'no connection (ConnectionResetError)': 588, ...} != {'200': 810}` and five to twelve of the forty refused. `tests/ui/delivery.js`, called from `panel.test.js`: the harness withholds a file on request (`drop`, `busy`, `broken`, written by the test into the file the harness names in its first line), and the checks cover one miss, two, three with the notice and Reload, a broken script, two files at once, both style sheets, and the redraw. Run on the Mac with Edge over the DevTools protocol, five times, about 10 s each, all passed; in CI it runs with Playwright as the last step of the browser test.
- **For the merge with #102** (the shell): `load.js`, `delivery.js`, `index.html` and the end of `harness.py` are not touched by that branch. `app.js` gets one line at the end of `boot()`, after `setInterval(fillRanges, 250)`, which that branch leaves alone; `panel.test.js` gets one block before the final `const csp`, thirty lines after that branch's last change. A trial merge with that branch as it stood (`b81259e`) merged all the code by itself, and `delivery.js` passed on the merged tree with Edge on the Mac, the shell's `render()` included. The three log files conflict, as two branches that both add at the same end do: keep both sides. That branch's own check of the roles loads the page once more when a file was not delivered, and its lesson leaves the question "for the owner to decide"; D68 is the answer, and taking that second load out again is for that branch.
- **Not done, and why.** Nothing was run on the Pi or on Linux. The 503 that arrives as a reset is left as it is. Keep-alive is not introduced (D68 says why). The harness still leaves its temporary folder behind (lesson).
- **The Mac's disk** was full twice during this session (see the lesson); nothing of another session was removed.
- **To check on the Pi**, by whoever is next there: `ss -ltn` shows the panel's listener with a queue of 128 after the deploy (the second number of the Send-Q column); `nstat -az TcpExtListenOverflows TcpExtListenDrops` before and after a few phones open the panel together, on the old build and the new (on the old build the counters should move and the page should take a second longer; on the new they should stay); and one phone on the venue's Wi-Fi, to see that the page starts no later with `load.js` in its head.

## 2026-10-08, the evening (a unit test that failed by chance)

- `tests.test_boxcare.ExportTest.test_default_export_holds_no_secret` failed once in CI on #102: it looks for the box's PIN anywhere in the text of a settings export, the PIN is four random digits, and that run's PIN was "0002", which stands in the test's own projector id "aaaa0002". Nothing leaked. The test now sets a PIN of its own choosing that is nowhere in its fixtures, and looks for a secret made of digits (the PIN, a join code) standing by itself, not inside a longer number or word. A real leak of either would be the value by itself.

## 2026-10-08, the evening (CI's install retry met its first slow mirror, and lost)

- On #102's second run the `effects-gpu` job for the Pi 4's kind of OpenGL failed in its install step with "ci-apt: gave up after 3 attempts": the lists came at once each time and the 48 MB of packages did not arrive in 90 seconds, three times, from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`. So the retry of #101 failed sooner (under five minutes instead of ten) but cured nothing: the same runner asked the same slow mirror three times.
- `tools/ci-apt.sh` now changes the mirror after a failed attempt: `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` becomes `archive.ubuntu.com` in the files apt reads its mirrors from. What an attempt downloaded stays in apt's cache, so the next one carries on. Not proven: no run has needed the second attempt since; the first slow mirror after this merges is its test. If Ubuntu's own archive is slow from that runner too, the step still fails, and a rerun is still the cure.

The one that is gone is the row "Room" of a presenter's System index, on purpose (D65). Taking the three lists took 166 s here.
