Cause: the four area tabs could not shrink below word plus padding, and CI's Linux face is wider than the Mac's (LESSONS). Mended in the panel: the tabs have no least width and 2 px of side padding, and under 380 px a 14 px word and less air round the row; the height is unchanged (48 px in the default look, 56 in Signal). Shown on the Mac with a wider face forced on the menus' and the strip's buttons (Verdana, 0.06em), at 320, 360, 390, 600 and 768 px on five screens: before, the same fault as CI's (347 px in 320, #tab-setup at 263 to 346) and "Blackout" broken on the strip (75 px of word in 73); after, 0 faults. The strip got room to spare as well (13 px words under 380 px in both looks; in Signal also less padding, for a theme that asks for the device's own face: with 0.1em forced on Archivo "Blackout" was 68 px in 61, now it fits). The row of an area's screens and the side menu passed the forced sweep as they were: the row goes to a second line, the menu's words wrap. Then, with nothing forced: the 29 screens at 13 sizes 0 faults in both looks, the states 7 of 7 in both, Signal's checks 116 of 116, the shell's checks all passed in both looks. The control inventory was not taken again after this change of the stylesheet: the Mac's disk was full (LESSONS).

**The third CI run (b20094d).** The width sweep passed on CI's typefaces. The test went on through the first Signal round and failed in the round at 1366 px with one line: "Signal, projectors-turn-off at 1366: slider fill 83.7% for a value at 4.1%: #seek Position in the clip". The bar was shown (the "hidden or empty" in the page's notes is from later, when the window was 390 px again and the strip closed; no rule about hidden sliders was needed or added). Cause: the fill had its own clock and trailed the value by up to 250 ms after every poll; the check's second look 300 ms later met the next write (LESSONS). `max` never changes (1000 steps), and the strip being kept across screens is not part of it. Mended in the panel: `patchLive` writes the fill with the value, an observer fills whatever is drawn again before it is painted, the clock stays as a net. The other sliders the box moves in place (the effect and shader controls) write the number beside them in the same turn, which the observer sees; that was read in the code, not seen moving (no player, no controller here). The check takes no second look now, and the browser test has a new part after the round at 1366: with a clip playing, 30 looks over three seconds at 1366, 768 and 390 px with More open, each of which must find the fill where the value is, and the place must have moved. Shown on the Mac in headless Edge, with status answers given a clip of 7.3 s that plays and answers up to 0.9 s late (this Mac has no mpv, so the bar stands still otherwise): before, 20 of 190 looks found the fill behind, and with late answers the check's own fault came back once in 20 s ("slider fill 92.6% for a value at 6.4%"); the new part failed at all three widths. After: 0 of 818 looks at the three widths, the new part passes at all three, Signal's checks without the second look 116 of 116, the shell's checks pass in Signal. Not run here: the Playwright test itself, the unit tests and the inventory (the disk; the change is the panel's script and the test). master was merged in first (D66 and its entries kept beside D65).

**Open.** The first CI run of this branch: the Playwright steps were not run here. The fixture's comparison in CI (Linux) has not run; if a control exists on the Mac's harness and not on CI's, it will say so by name. The owner's look, and his judgement on the places D65 lists as a judgement. `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` still names the old tabs. New pictures for `docs/images/ui`.

## 2026-10-08 (the ISF parser: comments before the header, and an input called `time`)

Branch `isf-leading-comments`, not merged. D66 has the decision. **Run on the dev Mac only, where there is no mpv: the translator's text was checked, nothing was drawn. The GPU tests added here run in CI. Nothing ran on a box.**

**Why.** ISF generators as people publish them often begin with credit lines in comments, and the parser wanted the JSON comment first, so such a file was refused at its first line. The owner asked for three leniencies. Nothing from anybody's files is in the repository: the test files are written here, with a made-up credit.

**What changed in `pvj/shaders.py`.**

- `find_header`: blank space, `//` comments and `/* */` comments may stand before the header, at most 4 KB (`MAX_LEADING`). The header is the first `/*` comment that begins with `{`; no later comment is tried. One pass with `str.find`, no pattern. The comments are in no text the player gets; the line the code starts on counts them, so `#line` is still the file's line. The stored upload is the upload's bytes.
- `renamed`: one rule for an input whose name is a reserved word in some letter case. GLSL's and ISF's words in another letter case (`time`, `Date`, `Float`) are taken and written as `pvj_in_<name>`. Letter for letter they are refused, and the player's own words are refused in any letter case, both as before. `color` as before.
- A `bool` DEFAULT: the check is spelled out (true, false, the number 0 or 1) and its message says so. Its behaviour did not change: see the first surprise below.
- `tools/isf-survey.py` reads the header where the translator finds it, and counts the header's size as the header comment's own.

**Two things the brief had wrong, found by looking** (both in LESSONS):

- The numbers 0 and 1 were already taken as a switch's DEFAULT. What published files do, and what is refused, is the text `"1"` and `"0"`, in quotes. The brief said to take exactly 0 and 1 and refuse everything else, so text stays refused, the tests pin both sides, and the question goes back to the owner (one line to change).
- `time` clashed with nothing. It was refused because the reserved list is compared in lower case and ISF's `TIME` is on it. The cause is fixed for the language's and ISF's words; the player's own words keep the lower-case comparison.

**Counted against real files.** The change was counted before and after over a folder of published generators outside the repository (read only, never executed, nothing copied); the counts went to the owner with the report of this work and are not kept here. What still refuses such files after this: a switch whose DEFAULT is text in quotes, and code that declares its own `uniform` (which stays refused). "Loads" there means the translator took the file; nothing was drawn.

**What a reviewer should look at first:** `find_header` (the loop's exits and its two limits, in characters while scanning and in bytes at the header); `renamed` and its use in `_input` (the order of the refusals around it); the test that walks every reserved word in four spellings and holds that each is refused or renamed. Two refusals changed their words and stayed refusals: `/* not json */` and `/*[1, 2]*/` before code are now "no header" (read as a plain comment), where they were "cannot be read" and "must be an object". Two names changed sides: `Time` and `rendersize` are taken (renamed). A no-break space before the header is refused now.

**Tests.** `tests/test_shaders.py`: `LenientTest` (10 tests) and `LenientUploadTest` (1). Against the parser of `origin/master` 10 of the 11 fail (the one for the switch passes there, as it must). Worst cases for the header search at 32 KB (54 texts, 20 parses each) run in well under the 1 s the test allows; measured singly, 0.06 to 1.5 ms a parse. `tests/test_isf_survey.py`: one test. `tests/test_shaders_gpu.py`: one test, **not run here**. `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` on the Mac: 1579 tests, 14 failures and 15 errors, all in `test_install`, `test_update`, `test_release` and `test_netd` (the four files that need Linux, as was expected of a Mac; not compared with a run of master here), 93 skipped (no mpv, no GPU).

**Open.** Whether a switch's DEFAULT may be the text `"0"` or `"1"` (the owner). The GPU test's first run in CI. Nothing here was seen on a display.

**After the independent review, the same day** (pull request #103; no high or medium finding, four low ones, all taken):

- The header's 8 KB was counted in characters, on master too: a header of 8,100 euro signs is 24 KB and passed. It is counted in bytes now, which is tighter than master. Test: `test_the_headers_limit_is_counted_in_bytes`.
- D66 and SHADERS.md named one thing that is refused now and was not (a no-break space before the header). There are more, and both say so now: every other kind of Unicode blank space there, and more than 4 KB of plain blank space.
- The 4 KB is counted after the byte order mark is dropped and Windows line ends are made `\n`, and SHADERS.md said "as the file's 32 KB is". The words were wrong, not the code; a test pins what is counted.
- `tools/isf-survey.py`: where the translator refuses to look for the header, the survey fell back to the first comment of any kind, so it took a long credit for the header and reported no problem. It gives the translator's own words now, and for the description looks only for a comment that begins with `{`.
- Which comment is the header is pinned character by character (`test_which_comment_is_the_header_is_decided_by_its_first_character_after_plain_blank_space`): `/* {` is the header, `/**{` and a no-break space before the brace make a plain comment. The docstring of `find_header` said the search stops at 4 KB; it reads a run of blank space or a comment that starts before that to its end, linear all the same, and says so now.
- Master was merged in (#101).

**The owner's answer on the switch, the same day.** He chose the recommended form: a `bool` input's DEFAULT may also be one of four texts in quotes, `"0"`, `"1"`, `"true"`, `"false"`, letter for letter, and no other text (D66, the addition). Done in `_input` with a closed table (`_BOOL_TEXT`); the refusal names everything that is taken. `clean_value` is untouched: a value that is set stays exactly true or false. Test: `test_a_switch_also_takes_four_texts_in_quotes_as_its_default_and_no_other_text` (the four and what `translate` writes for each, 34 near misses, the places that do not follow); it fails against the parser of the commit before. The earlier test of the switch lost the four texts from its list of refusals. Counted again over the same folder of published files outside the repository; the count went to the owner with the report. That closes the open question above; the others stand.
## 2026-10-08, late: the independent review of the sound (2 high, 4 medium, 8 low), all answered

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94, still a draft. Nothing of this has been on the box. Nobody has heard the sound. The dev Mac has no mpv, so the two new tests that run the real mpv were written blind and **their first run is CI's, after this push**.

**CI on the push before this one (`010d363`).** 26 of 28 checks passed and 2 failed: one of the two unit test jobs in each of the two runs (Python 3.12 in one, 3.9 in the other; the other job of each run passed). Both failures were the same test, `ReceiverSoundTest.test_when_the_player_stops_reading_sound_is_dropped_and_never_piles_up`, at its last line. A fault of the test, not of the helper: it waited for one more block of a flood to be written after the reader woke, which only happens if the pipe was full when the flood ended (always on the Mac, whose pipes hold 64 kB; luck on Linux, 1 MiB). It now gives a block after the reader woke and waits for that one. LESSONS has it.

**High.**
- **H1, the helper decided whether mpv parses Matroska.** Fixed in three places. The panel's handle on the helper reads "matroska" as "raw" unless the owner's switch is on (`Input.open`), and `play_ndi` asks the switch again at the call to the player. For the Matroska load the player sets `Player.MKV_ONLY` as properties first (`vd=rawvideo,-`, `ad=pcm_f32le,-`, `sid=no`, `ordered-chapters=no`, `cover-art-auto=no`, `embeddedfonts=no`), refuses the load if mpv refuses one, and takes them back before the next load (`_undo_pipe_globals`, which now keeps what it could not take back and tries again). Read for it, at v0.35.0, v0.37.0 and v0.40.0: `common/codecs.c` (`mp_select_decoders`: an entry `-` stops the fallback; a decoder is added only if its codec is the track's), `demux/demux_mkv.c` and `demux/codec_tags.c` (the names "rawvideo" and "pcm_f32le"), `filters/f_decoder_wrapper.c` (the options `vd` and `ad`, and the words "Failed to initialize a decoder for codec"), `options/options.c` (that `sid`, `ordered-chapters`, `cover-art-auto`, `embeddedfonts` exist at all three and their defaults), `player/loadfile.c` (when files beside the one played are opened by themselves: only with `sub-auto`, `audio-file-auto` or `cover-art-auto`, and cover art only for a file with sound and no picture). The two stale sentences ("raw video only") are corrected and `pvj/NDI.md` says what a taken-over helper can now reach: the Matroska reader. Tests: `PanelSoundTest.test_with_sound_switched_off_the_pipe_is_read_as_bare_frames_whatever_the_helper_answers`, `ApiSoundTest.test_a_helper_that_answers_matroska_while_sound_is_off_is_loaded_as_bare_frames`, those of `PlayerSoundTest`, and for the real mpv `RealMpvTest.test_a_stream_that_names_other_codecs_is_not_decoded_once_the_player_is_narrowed` (and the first real-mpv test now runs with the narrowing on).
- **H2, the sound's thread span flat out while the connection was down.** `recv_audio` now answers `AUDIO_LOST` for the library's error and the thread waits a quarter of a second, as the picture's does; and both threads wait out the rest of a tenth of a second when a call comes back with nothing sooner than that. The fakes now wait as the real library does; new fakes that answer at once are what the tests use. Tests: `ReviewTest.test_while_the_connection_is_down_the_sounds_thread_waits_and_does_not_spin`, `..._a_library_that_answers_at_once_with_nothing_is_not_asked_flat_out` (sound, and the same name in `test_ndi.LifetimeTest` for the picture), `..._the_library_saying_the_connection_is_down_is_told_apart_from_no_sound_in_time`.

**Medium.**
- **M1, late for good after a hiccup.** The count is now brought back to the clock (D62 has the rule and why). Test: `ReviewTest.test_after_a_hiccup_the_sound_comes_back_to_the_clock` (the reviewer's case: a 0.4 s stall, then the held blocks at once; the sound is within 0.05 s of the clock again three seconds later, and stays there), and `..._sound_a_little_behind_the_clock_for_a_whole_second_gets_that_much_silence_and_one_late_block_does_not`.
- **M2, long blocks played as silence for ever.** Both halves of the suggestion: the silence for missing sound now waits two blocks' lengths and stops a block's length before the picture, and blocks longer than 0.25 s are refused in words. Test: `ReviewTest.test_blocks_of_every_length_a_sender_may_use_are_played_and_longer_ones_are_refused_in_words` (12 s each at 0.0167, 0.1, 0.2 and 0.25 s: every block written, no fill, lead within 0.05 s; 0.4, 0.6 and 1.0 s: refused, the words on the page, the picture goes on).
- **M3, a second close freed the connection under a thread in the library.** `close` keeps the threads that did not come out and gives the connection back only when the count of threads that may be inside is zero. Tests: `ReviewTest.test_a_second_close_does_not_free_the_connection_under_a_sound_thread_still_in_the_library`, and the same added to `test_ndi.LifetimeTest.test_a_capture_that_will_not_come_back_leaves_its_connection_open_and_says_so` for the picture's thread.
- **M4, the player's queue.** Read in `demux/demux.c` at the three versions: `demuxer-max-bytes` is one total over all streams; at the limit, with one stream empty, mpv warns once and marks the empty stream as ended for now. So the queue is sized from the picture and the longest block (`Player.pipe_queue`), and step N10 (k) names the line. Tests: `PlayerSoundTest.test_the_players_queue_is_sized_from_the_picture_and_the_longest_block_of_sound`, and for the real mpv `RealMpvTest.test_at_a_real_size_and_in_real_time_the_players_queue_takes_picture_and_sound_together`.

**Low.** L1: one lock around looking at the hold and setting the speed (`LiveSpeedTest.test_a_speed_request_and_a_play_at_the_same_moment_cannot_leave_a_live_source_at_the_mix_speed`). L2: Stop ends the hold, and so does the status when nothing live plays (`..._stop_ends_the_hold_and_the_mix_speed_is_the_players_again`, `..._a_live_source_that_ended_by_itself_ends_the_hold_at_the_next_look`). L3: the hold ends only when the player took the speed (`..._a_mix_speed_the_player_did_not_take_keeps_the_hold_until_it_does`). L4: the helper's default is no sound (`ServiceSoundTest.test_the_helper_plays_sound_only_when_the_panel_asks_for_it_in_so_many_words`). L5: the sentence about a rollback is in `pvj/NDI.md`. L6: a block is converted only after it is known to be wanted (`ReviewTest.test_a_block_that_will_not_be_written_is_not_converted`). L7: the silence's place is taken under the lock, with the queue, before it is written (`ReviewTest.test_silence_for_missing_sound_never_lies_over_a_block_queued_while_it_was_being_written`). L8: the manual. **Notes:** the documents say each cluster has its exact length; they say what the queue's bound is and that the writer may hold one more batch; the test that read source text to see that the three live sources hold the speed is now three real plays (`LiveSpeedTest`, `ApiSoundTest`, `CaptureApiTest`); OSC, MIDI and the lighting desk set the speed through `control` only (looked: `pvj/osc.py`, `pvj/midi.py`, `pvj/dmx.py`), the multi-box follower sets it itself for clips and was left.

**Each new test was run against the code as it was before the fix**: 16 failures and 9 errors in the touched modules, the test of every item that has one among them (L5 and L8 are sentences), then none.

**A mistake of mine in a device step, corrected.** N12 sent the owner to `journalctl -u pvj-player` for mpv's words. They are not there: the player starts mpv with no terminal output. N10 (j) now has a line that asks mpv over its socket.

**Not verified.** Everything on a box. The two new real-mpv tests anywhere, until CI has run them. mpv 0.35 and 0.40 with the narrowing (read, not run). What a correction of the sound's count sounds like.

## 2026-10-08, NDI sound (built, measured on the dev Mac, never heard)

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94, still a draft; master (#101) merged in first. **Nobody has heard any of this.** Nothing here ran on the Pi.

- **The owner, 2026-10-08.** After the first run he looked at the Scan Converter picture on the Pi's monitor: "The pi looked good". That is the first look by eye, at a moving source only; the still source has not been looked at. Then: "go ahead with sound". He will send sound from NDI Tools on the Mac (Test Patterns' tone, and a film's sound through Scan Converter). The repeat of a frame that nothing follows is accepted for now and gets tried on the box with the next deploy.
- **The design** is in D62's dated paragraph: one pipe that carries picture and sound with a time on each, as Matroska written by the helper (uncompressed UYVY, float PCM), read by mpv's own reader; a source without sound, or with sound switched off, keeps the bare frames that ran on the Pi. Weighed and not chosen: a second pipe of raw samples (two streams timed by counting, in a path that drops and repeats frames), NUT (read through libavformat, with checksums to write), leaving sound to the room.
- **What the real senders sent (dev Mac, library 6.3.2.0, nothing played aloud).** Test Patterns: 48000 Hz, 2 channels, blocks of 4800 samples, a 1 kHz tone peaking at exactly 1.0 (RMS 0.70709). macOS AV Output: 48000 Hz, 6 channels, blocks of 800, sound in the first two only. Scan Converter: no sound in 8 seconds. The library's time on a block is its sending time (steps of 35 to 165 ms between 100 ms blocks), and Test Patterns' still frame carried a time 8061 seconds older than its sound: the sender's times are not used.
- **Through the helper into a pipe, ten seconds of Test Patterns:** the stream was Matroska, decided 0.06 s after the first picture; 100 blocks received, 100 written, 0 dropped, each following the last without a gap; the sound in the pipe peaks at 0.10000, -20.00 dBFS (RMS -23.01), which is where an SMPTE alignment tone belongs. Converting ten seconds of stereo takes 77 ms of one core on the Mac.
- **Built.** `pvj/ndi.py`: `check_audio`, `audio_block`, `mkv_header`, `mkv_block_head`, the receiver's third thread, the choice of what the pipe carries (`decide`), silence where sound is missing, the bound on what waits, `_leave` (the connection goes back to the library only when both the picture's and the sound's threads are out of it), the switch in `configure`, `_audio` (the panel's re-check). `pvj/player.py`: `play_pipe(..., "matroska")`. `pvj/api.py`: `POST /api/ndi {"action": "sound"}`. The page: a Sound card with the switch and a line that says what arrives. Settings: `ndi.sound`, no new schema. **The unit's sandbox lines are unchanged.**
- **Tests.** New `tests/test_ndi_sound.py`: the checks on a block (20 hostile shapes, nothing read before they pass), the level (the reference tone at -20 dBFS, clipping, NaN and infinity as silence), the container read back by a reader of the test's own, the receiver with sound (one stream with times, no copy of a frame, a source without sound, late and changed sound, a refused block, a stalled reader, sound ahead of and behind the clock, silence filled in, the still frame, the connection's release), the service, the settings, the panel's re-check, the routes, the player's options. Two tests run the real mpv on the real stream through a real pipe; they skip where there is no mpv, which is the dev Mac.
- **What CI shows and what it does not** is said in `pvj/NDI.md`, "Sound".
- **Docs.** `pvj/NDI.md` (a "Sound" section), the manual, the roadmap, HANDOFF, D62, three lessons, device step N10 rewritten as the sound's test (ten parts).
- **Not verified.** Everything a person would hear. The delay, lip sync, drift. What mpv does when a block is left out or silence is filled in. The level of a film against a clip. The cost on a Pi 4. mpv 0.35 and 0.40 with this stream.

## 2026-10-08, the first run of the NDI input on a Pi 4, and three things it found

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94, still a draft; master (#99, #100) merged in first, both sides kept in the log files. **The run was done by the coordinator over SSH on the owner's test Pi 4 (Debian 13, mpv 0.40); nobody has looked at the monitor.** This entry writes down what he measured and no more. What was changed afterwards (below) has NOT been on the box.

**Deployed:** a local merge of master into `ae4d690`, by `git archive` and `install.sh --offline`; four files equal by hash; five services active; the settings went from schema 14 to 15 (a schema 14 copy is kept root-only on the box).

**Measured.**
- N0 held after the plain install: no account, group, unit, drop-ins, `/opt/pvj-ndi`, `/run/pvj-ndi`, no process; `pvj-ndi-runtime status` said not set up and named the command. `avahi-daemon` was already installed and active on the image before the opt-in.
- The x86_64 library by its real file name (`libndi.so.6.3.2`) was refused: "the NDI runtime file is for x86_64, this box is aarch64", exit 1, nothing left behind.
- `sudo pvj-ndi-runtime install "<the folder holding lib/aarch64-rpi4-linux-gnueabi>"` worked first time: library installed, group and user created (uid 996, gid 984, no other groups), unit enabled, "was running 3 seconds after its start", exit 0. `systemctl show pvj-web`: SupplementaryGroups "audio video pvj-ndi" and `PVJ_NDI_DIR`. NRestarts 0.
- Module switched on through the API: the helper logged "loaded the NDI runtime (NDI SDK LINUX 13:10:53 Apr 13 2026 6.3.2.0)"; discovery from inside the sandbox listed "FIRESPRAY-31.LOCAL (Test Patterns)" at 192.168.0.159:5962 within about 3 seconds (the first poll), later also "(Scan Converter)" at :5963. Sender: NDI Tools on a Mac on Wi-Fi, the Pi wired, same /24.
- Test Patterns (a still source): play answered 1920 x 1080 at 29.97; helper state "still", counts received 0, shown 1; mpv: path `/run/pvj-ndi/ndi.fifo`, file-format rawvideo, 1920x1080 uyvy422, container-fps 29.97, vo-configured true, frame-drop-count 0, time-pos 0.0, estimated-frame-number 0, core-idle true. `GET /api/preview.jpg` answered 503 `{"error": "mpv: error running command"}` while it was up. **Whether the still picture was on the monitor is not known** (N12 stays open, now with this evidence).
- Scan Converter capturing a film in VLC (moving): play answered 1764 x 992 at 30.0. Over 61 seconds: received 1769, shown 1770, dropped 0, dropped_by_runtime 0 (about 29 a second on average, uneven between 10 second samples: 333, 300, 251, 238, 251, 273; the sender is on Wi-Fi, so this may be the link or the sender). mpv after 59 s: estimated-vf-fps 30.0, frame-drop-count 1, decoder-frame-drop-count 0, vo-delayed-frame-count 0, display 75 Hz. Preview picture 200 OK, 156 kB: right way up, no shear, colours plausible (skin tones and blues as expected, no green or purple cast), judged from the preview JPEG by the coordinator, not from the monitor.
- CPU, one 3 second `top -H` sample: mpv threads vo 21, three workers 21 each, main 20, demux 12 (about 117 percent of one core); helper two python threads 15 and 12, four "shqd" 7 each, two "ndir:re" 4 to 5 (about 68 percent). Load 2.0, 46.7 C, helper RSS 50 MB, 25 tasks of 64, MemoryCurrent "[not set]" (memory accounting is not on in this unit's cgroup on this box).
- Network rules: `systemd-run` as pvj-ndi with `IPAddressDeny=any` and the unit's allow list: `curl -m 6 https://example.com` timed out (exit 28; the name lookup may be what failed, not only the connection), a TCP connect to the sender's port succeeded, and the same curl without the rules gave 200.
- The helper holds UDP *:5960 sockets (four lines in `ss`) while the module is on.

**Three things it found, and what was done.**
1. **The SDK's own `libndi.so.6` is a link, and the command refused it** ("cannot be read as a file (it is a link)"), while device step N2 told the owner to pass exactly that name. `ndi.same_folder_target`: a link given as the file is followed only to a plain name in the same folder, at most two links deep; a target with any folder in it, a dangling link and a longer chain are refused, and the file is still opened without following a link. Tests: `RuntimeInstallHardeningTest.test_a_link_given_as_the_file_is_followed_only_to_a_plain_name_beside_it`, `test_a_name_swapped_for_a_link_after_the_look_is_refused_at_the_open`. N2 reworded.
2. **Two device steps could not be run as written.** N9: systemd 257 says "Failed to parse IP address prefix: localhost" for a list on one `-p`; the step now has one `-p IPAddressAllow=` per range and no localhost, and says how to tell a failed name lookup from a stopped connection. N6: `socat` is not on the image; a python3 line over the player's socket replaces it. N14: how to read the helper's memory when `MemoryCurrent` is not set.
3. **The snapshot of a still NDI source was a 503.** Why, as far as it can be told without the box: mpv had the one frame and stood at frame 0, idle; from memory of mpv's source (`player/video.c`, NOT read again for this) it shows a frame only once it also holds the next one, so a source that sends one frame never gets it shown, and there is nothing to copy. **That is a reading, not a finding.** Two changes: the helper writes a frame that nothing follows once more after 0.3 seconds, once (`REPEAT_AFTER`, the count `repeated`; tests `ReceiverTest.test_a_frame_that_nothing_follows_is_written_once_more_and_only_once` and `test_frames_that_follow_each_other_are_never_written_twice_and_no_buffer_runs_out`), and a snapshot that the player cannot make of an NDI source answers 409 with plain words that the page shows (`SnapshotOfAStillSourceTest`, three tests). N12 says what to look at on the monitor and what to try if the picture is still not there. If it is not, the repeat comes out again (D62).

**Docs.** `pvj/NDI.md` has "First run on a Pi 4" with the figures, marked as one short run from a Wi-Fi sender; HANDOFF no longer says NDI never ran on Linux or a Pi; D62 has a dated paragraph (what is shown, what is not); LESSONS has four entries; the device steps carry what the run showed beside each step it touched.

**Not verified.** Everything changed after the run: the link form on the box, the repeat with a real mpv, the snapshot's new answer on the box, the rewritten N9 line beyond what the coordinator typed. And all of what the run did not cover: sound, latency, the monitor by eye, 60 frames a second, 1080p60, HX, interlaced, a five minute run, N8, N11, N13 to N18.

## 2026-10-08, after the second read of the opt-in's fixes (2 medium, 2 low and notes, all fixed)

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94, still a draft. The second independent read (head `3397583`) found nothing high, 2 medium, 2 low and notes. **Nothing here ran on a Pi.** The two earlier pushes (`ad7df06`, `3397583`) passed every check on GitHub's runners, the real install under the runner's systemd among them; that is a Linux runner without a display and without NDI's library, not a box.

- **M1, the undo was around the wrong lines.** `write_units` is inside the part of `enable()` that is undone, and any `OSError` is undone too; `check()` refuses a link or a folder in the place of one of the three files before anything is changed; the message no longer says "nothing was written" when something was. Tests: `EnableTest.test_something_in_the_place_of_a_file_is_refused_before_anything_is_changed`, `test_a_file_that_cannot_be_written_undoes_the_ones_that_were`, `test_a_disk_that_is_full_or_read_only_undoes_the_setup_too`.
- **M2, the group.** `check_account` refuses a group `pvj-ndi` with gid 0, a number another group has, members, or that is somebody else's own group, whether or not the account exists. Members are refused (D62 says why). Test: `AccountTest.test_a_group_that_is_roots_shares_its_number_or_has_members_is_refused`.
- **L1, a fault my own fix brought in.** The installer's removal of a helper nobody set up was a bare `rm -f` under `set -e`. It now says WARNING and goes on, in the uninstall as well, and looks for the mark once more right before it removes. Tests: `InstallTest.test_a_folder_in_the_place_of_the_unit_does_not_stop_the_install` (CI only: the installer's tests need GNU tools), `NdiUnitTest.test_the_installer_removes_a_helper_that_nobody_set_up` (static, runs on the Mac).
- **L2, the look after `useradd`.** It has a test now, and its refusal says the account was made a moment ago and how to remove it. Test: `AccountTest.test_an_account_that_comes_out_of_useradd_wrong_is_refused_and_the_words_are_true`.
- **Notes.** N2: a link where the mark goes is removed by the installer (the link, not its target) with a WARNING; `InstallTest.test_a_mark_that_is_a_link_is_not_an_opt_in`, `test_a_link_at_the_mark_with_a_unit_present_leaves_nothing_systemd_would_read`, `NdiUnitTest.test_a_link_where_the_mark_goes_is_removed_and_said_loudly`. N3: the link that enabled the unit is removed with it (same tests). N4: a `.` as a folder name is refused in the installer and in `check_prefix`; `NdiUnitTest.test_a_dot_is_not_a_folder_name`, and three more spellings in the installer's prefix test. N5: device step N0 and `pvj/NDI.md` say that a box from an earlier build of this branch loses its helper at the next install. The updater's two calls: `RollbackTest` and `UpdateRestartTest`.
- **N6, declined with a reason.** The helper does not end when the library is missing or fails to load: it stays up and says so (`HelperIdlesTest`, new). It ends when the module is switched off with the library loaded (exit 0, on purpose, and the quick clean start is the point) and on a crash inside the library. `RestartPreventExitStatus` or a longer `RestartSec` would either slow the wanted case or not catch the unwanted one, since a crash in closed code has no exit status of its own. The unit is unchanged; `NdiUnitTest.test_a_helper_that_ends_is_started_again_as_written_and_why_that_was_left` pins it, and `pvj/NDI.md` says what it costs and what to do on the Pi if the library crashes on load.
- **Each fix was undone in a copy of the tree, one at a time (18 lines), and each time a named test failed.** For the installer's shell lines the test that fails on the Mac is the static one in `tests/test_units.py`; the tests that run the installer run in CI only.
- **Not verified.** The installer's new lines and its six new or changed stage tests, until CI has run them. Nothing on a Pi.

## 2026-10-08, after the review of the opt-in (2 medium and 6 low, all fixed)

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94, still a draft. An independent read-only review of the four opt-in commits (`ad7df06`) found nothing high, 2 medium and 6 low. Each is fixed with a test. **Nothing here ran on Linux or on a Pi**; the installer tests and the real-install steps run in CI only, and the real command has never succeeded anywhere, because that needs NDI's library.

- **M1, a refused library left `/opt/pvj-ndi`.** `install_runtime` now removes the folder it made when no library was put in place. Tests: `RuntimeFileTest.test_install_refuses_the_wrong_file_and_leaves_nothing_behind`, `CommandTest.test_a_refused_library_through_the_real_command_leaves_no_folder`, and the real-install step after the refused file.
- **M2, a rollback on an opted-in box.** The unit has `ConditionPathExists=@PVJ_DIR@/bin/pvj-ndi`, and the updater's restart try-restarts `pvj-ndi.service` with the network helper. Tests: `RollbackTest` (both), `NdiUnitTest.test_a_release_without_the_helpers_program_skips_the_unit_and_does_not_fail_it_for_ever`, and a real-install step that moves the helper's program away and looks at `NRestarts`, `is-failed` and `ConditionResult`. `pvj/NDI.md` says what a rollback does and how to take the helper off by hand when `pvj-ndi-runtime` is a link to nothing.
- **L1, the checks came after the changes.** `ndisetup.check` (the install folder, the template, an existing account) runs first in `enable` and, in the command, before the library is copied. `install.sh` drops slashes at the end of `--prefix` and refuses `//`. Tests: `EnableTest.test_a_prefix_or_a_template_that_is_refused_stops_it_before_anything_is_changed`, `test_an_account_that_is_refused_stops_it_before_a_package_is_installed`, `CommandTest.test_a_setup_that_would_be_refused_does_not_even_copy_the_library`, `InstallTest.test_a_slash_at_the_end_of_the_install_folder_is_dropped_and_a_doubled_one_refused`, `NdiUnitTest.test_the_install_folder_is_written_one_way`.
- **L2, a failed setup left the mark.** A failing reload, enable or start undoes the files on a box that was not set up before. Tests: `EnableTest.test_a_step_that_fails_after_the_files_were_written_undoes_them_on_a_box_that_was_not_set_up` and `..._on_a_box_that_was_already_set_up_leaves_it_set_up`. `EnableTest` now writes the real files into a fake root; only the commands and the accounts are stand-ins.
- **L3, "installed and running" was not looked at.** The command asks `systemctl is-active` three times a second apart and says "was running 3 seconds after its start" or "NOT running"; the second leaves the setup in place (it is what one wants to look at on the Pi) and ends with an error. Test: `EnableTest.test_a_helper_that_does_not_stay_up_is_said_and_is_not_called_running`, `CommandTest.test_a_helper_that_did_not_stay_up_is_an_error_for_the_command`.
- **L4, a helper without the mark.** Chosen: the installer disables and removes it (D62 says why). Tests: `InstallTest.test_a_helper_without_the_mark_is_removed_by_the_next_install`, `NdiUnitTest.test_the_installer_removes_a_helper_that_nobody_set_up`, and a real-install step.
- **L5, the account.** Root under another name, a shared number and a login shell are refused, before anything is made. Test: `AccountTest.test_an_account_that_is_root_shares_a_number_or_can_log_in_is_refused`.
- **L6, a folder where the mark should be.** `remove_units` goes on and says what it left; `remove` still takes the library and ends with an error. Tests: `DisableTest.test_something_that_cannot_be_removed_does_not_stop_the_rest`, `CommandTest.test_remove_takes_the_library_also_when_something_of_the_helper_could_not_be_removed`.
- **The notes.** `pvj/NDI.md`: where not to unpack the SDK; the read-only root (the command also warns when it sees it, `EnableTest.test_on_the_read_only_root_it_says_that_the_setup_will_not_last`); that avahi keeps running after an opt-out if the opt-in installed it; that root could also set `PVJ_NDI_DIR` in `pvj.env` (the test that claimed "from nowhere else" is renamed and says so). `refresh --root` says in its help and docstring that the folder is trusted. `tests/real_install_test.sh` says that the real command's success needs the library and is device step N2, and now uninstalls an opted-in box under real systemd.
- **Declined: nothing.** One thing done differently from the suggestion: a helper that does not stay up is not undone (L2 covers the steps that fail outright); undoing it would take away the unit that device step N3 needs.
- **Not verified.** Everything in shell (the installer's new lines, the real-install steps) until CI has run; that `ConditionPathExists=` reads as "skipped, not failed" on the runner's systemd is first seen there.

## 2026-10-08, later still (the NDI input: the owner's four answers; the helper is now opt-in per box)

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94, still a draft and not merged. **Nothing here ran on Linux or on a Pi.** The NDI input has only ever received frames on a Mac; the installer's tests cannot run on the dev Mac (they need GNU tools) and run in CI.

- **The owner's answers (2026-10-08).** (1) The helper is opt-in: "install the helper only when NDI is wanted; one extra command at the box, alongside the runtime install". (2) A full-access support session may change NDI settings exactly as it may change Streams. (3) Sound later, after the picture has been measured on the Pi. (4) He accepts the NDI SDK licence himself, for testing on his own test Pi only; the venue comes later and stays his question to settle with NDI.
- **What a plain install does now.** Nothing about NDI: no `pvj-ndi` unit, account, group or folder, no avahi package, and the units of the panel and the player are byte for byte master's again (the extra group `pvj-ndi` is out of them). The image build enables what it enabled before. What every box does get: the program files and the command `/usr/local/bin/pvj-ndi-runtime`.
- **The opt-in command** is `sudo pvj-ndi-runtime install <the NDI SDK>`: the library first (a refused file changes nothing), then `pvj/ndisetup.py`: avahi packages if missing, the group and account, the unit (its template moved from `install/` to `pvj/systemd/` so that it travels with the program; its sandbox lines are unchanged), two drop-ins giving the panel and the player the extra group, reload, enable, start, and a restart of the panel and the player. An installer flag was the other candidate and was dropped: a box made from the image or updated from the panel has no installer on it. The panel's drop-in is the mark of the opt-in; `install.sh` finds it and has the new program write the unit and drop-ins again (`pvj-ndi-runtime refresh`, one writer) and keeps the helper enabled. `pvj-ndi-runtime remove` opts out and leaves the account, the group and avahi, saying so. D62 has a dated paragraph; LESSONS has why the group had to leave the core units.
- **The panel on a box that did not opt in.** It takes `PVJ_NDI_DIR` in its environment (set only by its drop-in) as the mark. Without it the helper's socket is never tried, `GET /api/ndi` answers `"setup": false` with the command, playing an NDI source answers 409 in plain words, the addresses can still be filled in, and the page says "NDI is not set up on this box" with the licence note and the one command (it used to say the helper was not running and that a restart usually helps, which would be wrong there).
- **The tunnel.** Already the same as Streams: neither route is in `support.REMOTE_DENY`, neither handler looks at the tunnel, both ask the same roles. No behaviour changed. `TunnelSameAsStreamsTest` pins it, through a real support session in the test harness.
- **Docs.** `pvj/NDI.md` (the opt-in, what it changes, opting out, the owner's licence decision; what NDI's documentation pages say, which was quoted, is now given in this project's words throughout, keeping only the trademark sentence NDI asks to be shown; the agreement itself was and is only paraphrased), the manual, `install/README.md`, the roadmap line, HANDOFF, and the device steps: N0 and N1 now check a box that did not opt in, N2 is the opt-in command, N17 ("a box that did not opt in shows nothing of NDI on the network") and N18 (an update over an opted-in box, and the opt-out) are new.
- **Tests.** New `tests/test_ndi_setup.py` (36 tests: the three files and their order, the refresh, the account's flags and the refusal of an account in another group, every command of the opt-in and the opt-out in order with fake commands, the command itself, the panel and the routes on a box that is not set up, the tunnel). `tests/test_units.py`: the sandbox pins are untouched; the tests that said "the installer and the image enable it" now say the opposite, and the group is pinned to the drop-ins. `tests/test_install.py`: six new stage-mode tests (a plain install leaves nothing of NDI and says nothing of it or of avahi; an opted-in box keeps and refreshes its unit; a link is not a mark; uninstall). `tests/real_install_test.sh`: new steps under a real systemd, without a library (nothing after plain installs; the real command with a wrong file; opt-in; a plain install and an update over it; opt-out). **Run and passing on the dev Mac: the Python tests other than the installer's. The six new installer tests were run there and fail there like the twelve older ones, at the installer's first GNU-only command (`mv -T`), so they say nothing yet. Not run anywhere when this was written: the real-install steps, shellcheck and the browser test's changed NDI step. All of these first run in CI on the push.**
- **Not verified, and for the reviewer.** That systemd adds a drop-in's `SupplementaryGroups=` to the unit's own list as its documentation says (the real-install job checks it on the runner; step N2 on the Pi); `apt-get` from the opt-in on a Pi; the time the screen is dark; that the helper starts under its sandbox on the CI runner at all (it never has started under systemd anywhere). The root and installer parts want an independent read before this leaves draft: `pvj/ndisetup.py` whole, the NDI lines of `install/install.sh`, `runtime_main` in `pvj/ndi.py`.

## 2026-10-08 (the resizing study, step 1: a width sweep, and the twelve faults it found)

Pull request #100, branch `resize-step1`, not merged. The owner said yes to step 1 the same day. D64 has the decision; this is what was measured. **Measured in headless Chromium (CI, Linux) and in headless Edge on the dev Mac against the harness with no player. Nothing was seen on a real phone, and nothing ran on a box.**

**The sweep.** `tests/ui/sweep.js`, called from `tests/ui/panel.test.js`: the 45 screens and states of `tests/ui/signal-pages.js`, in Signal and in the default look, each at 13 windows (320, 360, 390, 600, 768, 899, 900, 1200, 1366, 1600 px wide; 667x375, 844x390, 932x430). The first run in CI: 90 screens, resizing and measuring 47.8 s for both looks together, the default look's own round 56.0 s with the opening of its screens; so a little over a minute more (half of the measuring rides on a round that ran anyway). That run failed at the sweep, as it should, so its step time is not a passing run's: three runs of master took 5 min 39 s to 6 min 2 s, and the pull request has the time of a run that passed.

**The faults, and what mends each.** The first five were found on the Mac before the first push and their repairs were in the first run in CI, which did not name them again. That run named the rest (100 lines; the tab bar's fault is on every screen); whether the repairs for 6 to 10 hold is what the pull request's last run says.

1. Schedule, the form for a new entry, both looks: the seven day buttons were 34 to 37 px wide at 320, 40 to 43 at 360, 40 to 42 at 900 and 42 at 932x430 (the laptop's two columns make the card narrow again). Now a grid: seven across where each can be 44 px, four and three where not.
2. Live in the default look at 320 and 360: the page scrolled sideways (367 px in a window of 320). Blackout, the last of the four big buttons, was at 241 to 333, and the pill beside the title reached 366. Under 380 the four buttons are two and two (at 390 they fit in a line, as before); under 600 the title row wraps and the pill may break.
3. The default look at 320: "Previous" was cut in two on Live's effects strip and on Mix (66 px of word in 64), and on the Shaders page (66 in 56). Under 380 those buttons have 2 px of padding and 14 px type.
4. System > MIDI controller in the default look, on the Mac only: "Medium" on the brightness of a controller's lights was cut at every width (54 px in 38). CI's typeface did not show it. A button there is now as wide as its word.
5. Room in Signal at 320: the scene "WORKSHOP" was cut (137 px in 117). The name's size is `clamp(16px, 5.2vw, 20px)`: 20 px from 385 px of window up, as before.
6. The default look at 320, every screen with five tabs: "System" was cut and ran out of its tab (58 px in 52). Under 380 a tab is as wide as its word plus an even share of the rest, 44 px at least.
7. The default look, a row of choices: "Exponential" (a shader's wave on Live) was cut at 320, 360, 390, 900 and 932 (107 px in 70 to 94), "Round" on Mix at 320. Below 1366 a choice is as wide as its word and the row wraps (first written for under 1200; see "Every bundled row of choices" below).
8. The Shaders page in the default look at 390: "Previous" cut (78 px in 76). Under 600 the three buttons are even columns.
9. Live with a shader on, the laptop's columns, both looks: the Vibes button had no floor. Signal: 63 px wide at 1200 with its words outside it, 40x126 at 932x430; the default look: a word cut at 900. From 900 to 1365 it keeps 10rem now and the buttons beside it go to a second line. The sweep found no fault there at 1366 or 1600, so the rule stops at 1365 and those sizes are as they were.
10. The same screen at 932x430, both looks: the lower controls of the shader's strip could not be brought into view, or lay under the tab bar. The strip is held in place beside the pads and is taller than that window. In a window under 480 px high it now scrolls with the page.
11. Projectors, the form for a new one, Signal at 932x430: the port field lay under the message line. Not mended: the line goes by itself, and the sweep no longer counts it as a cover.
12. The Shaders page in Signal at 320, found by the third push's run and only by one of its two: a choice with one figure on it (the petals of `nxlx-prism`: 3, 4, 5, 6, 8) was 40 px wide. Signal had replaced the 44 px floor of a row of choices with `min-content`; the floor is back. Nine of the bundled shaders and effects have such a row.

**What the sweep did not find.** Nothing at 600, 768 or 899, and nothing at 667x375 or 844x390. The medium widths show a phone's column stretched wide; a short window shows Signal's title block (106 px) and the tab bar (57 px) around 212 px of page. Both are whole by the sweep's rules. Nothing was changed there, apart from rule 7's, which also applies at those widths.

**Not fixed, on purpose.** The transport's "Prev" and "– 10 s" go to two lines at 320 in the default look (between words, so no word is cut). The left and right safe areas of a notched phone held sideways are not padded; a headless browser has none to measure.

**The second run in CI** (commit `e41bec1`, the repairs for all ten in): the browser test passed with the sweep in it. The sweep's own line: 90 screens at 13 sizes, 48.6 s of resizing and measuring, 56.3 s for the default look's round. The step took 7 min 0 s, against 5 min 39 s to 6 min 2 s on three runs of master: 60 to 80 seconds more. The unit tests on Python 3.9: 1565 run, OK, 70 skipped. ## 2026-10-08 (Automatic on a Pi 4: edge glow and corner colour tint step down to 540 lines)

Branch `auto-540-two`. The owner answered the open question of D58: yes, `fx-edge-glow.fs` and `isf-corner-color-tint.fs` work at 540 lines in Automatic on a Pi 4.

- **Why.** The run of 2026-10-05 (below, "effect detail on the Pi 4"): at 720 lines both dropped nothing but kept the GPU 94 to 96 percent busy, so the mapping on top would tip them; at 540 lines they dropped nothing with the GPU 77 to 78 percent busy, and are softer.
- **How.** `PI4_720` in `pvj/effects.py` is untouched: it is what the board measured, and it says both held. A new set beside it, `PI4_HEADROOM`, names the filters that held at 720 and are stepped down for room, with the reason, the dates and that it is the owner's choice; `_pi4_lines` reads it, so `AUTO["pi4"]["lower"]` now holds three filters. Only Automatic reads it: 720 lines or Full chosen for the box are what the effect works at. A Pi 3's row and the boards that are not measured are as they were.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_effects.py`: the two and isf-edge-blowout get 540 on a Pi 4 in Automatic, fx-grade 720, 34 filters stay at 720; a chosen 720 or Full is not overridden; every name in the set is in `PI4_720` and held there; the two measured rows are what they were.
- **Text.** `pvj/SHADERS.md` (the Effect detail paragraph, the findings of the run, the example of the state), HANDOFF.md (the question is off the owner's list; the Effect detail notes), a dated paragraph under D58. The panel's line "540 for the heaviest effects and for ones you add" stays: the three at 540 are the three with the dearest pass at 720 lines. `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` does not name the filters and was not changed.
- **Not run on hardware.** Nothing here was run on the Pi. That the two hold at 540 lines rests on the measurements of 2026-10-05; all 37 at Automatic have not been run again with the new table.

## 2026-10-08 ("no reply from mpv" in the GPU tests: two different failures, one shown and fixed, one not explained)

Pull request #96, a draft, not merged. The task: stop rerunning the "no reply from mpv" that came now and then in CI's GPU jobs, add a diagnostic, make it happen, fix the cause where it lives. Everything here is CI's software GPU (Mesa under xvfb on GitHub's runners, mpv 0.37). Nothing ran on a Pi and nothing here says what a board does.

**The evidence that was still there.** All 879 kept runs of "pvj platform layer" (2026-09-29 to 2026-10-08) were searched, earlier attempts of rerun runs included: 56 failed jobs whose failed step was a GPU step, and 169 whose failed step was the unit tests or the browser test. Two logs hold the message, both of 2026-10-05, and they are not the same failure:

- Run 37339188136 attempt 3, job 111886082758, `test (3.9)`, the GLSL 1.40 step: `DesktopGlLiveTest.test_time_goes_on_across_a_change_of_a_value_and_of_the_speed`, `screenshot-to-file` at line 167, right after the speed was set to 0 and a quarter second of waiting. The seventh test of the step, two minutes in. At that commit `Player.swap_source` set `fbo-format` at every change of a value (taken out in #92), so the screenshot came after a change of shaders and a new set-up of the renderer.
- Run 37377643589, job 111990914734, `effects-gpu (OpenGL ES)`: `GlesEffectTest.test_a_capped_effect_draws_the_right_picture...`. **Not a screenshot**, as the hand-off had it: `set_property keep-open` in `Player._play`, in the test's set-up, three seconds into the first test of the job, so at the first start of a player on that runner. The first question (`get_property pid`) had been answered.

**What the code rules out.** `Ipc.request` opens a connection for each request and closes it when it returns. A late answer goes to a closed socket; it cannot be taken for the answer to a later request, no request waits behind another on a socket, and there is no reader thread and no reconnect. The one-shot connections watch no property, so a flood of events is not likely either. What is left is "slow" or "stuck".

**The diagnostic** (`tests/mpv_watch.py`, tests only, in both GPU set-ups). It times every request and prints a table per kind when the process ends. When a request gets no reply it prints the request and its number, the ten requests before it, what came on its connection, each thread of the player and of Xvfb with its state and its share of a processor, the load, timed cheap questions on new connections, when the answer to the original request did come (the connection is kept open and read on for 20 s), the same request once more with 30 s of patience, and the end of the player's own log with `PVJ_MPV_LOG=1`. The request fails as before. The product's client is not changed for it: while a watch is on, the `socket` that `pvj.player` uses is a stand-in whose sockets note what they receive and can be kept open after the client closed them.

**A workflow file could not be pushed** with this login (GitHub: "refusing to allow an OAuth App to create or update workflow ... without `workflow` scope"). The login was left alone. The loop ran inside the three existing `effects-gpu` jobs instead: while a marker file was in `tests`, a push made `python -m tests.test_effects_gpu` run the loop in place of the tests, and the pull request's run of the same commit ran the tests as usual. The coordinator agreed to that afterwards. The marker, the loop and the hook are gone again.

**Round 1** (push run 37752093365; jobs 113227538068, 113227538101, 113227538246; about 61 runner-minutes). Per job a third quiet, a third with one spinning process per processor (4), a third with the disk cache emptied before each start of a player. 82 batches, 166 tests: the generators' "time" test 26 times, the capped effect test 12 times, and five more. Two batches were marked failed, both for the same thing, and no test in them failed:

- In two of the three jobs the **first start of a player on the runner** got no reply to `get_property pid` in 2 s. The answer came 3.307 s and 2.809 s after the request, "success", together with mpv's `video-reconfig` and `idle` events. Nothing else had come on the connection. The player's threads were asleep (once the `vo` thread was in state D, waiting for the disk, at 4 percent of a processor; once S at 10 percent), Xvfb at 0, the load 0.7 on 4 processors. A cheap question on a new connection was answered at the same moment as the original one and not before: the player's core answers nobody while this lasts. Asked again, 0.000 s. This `pid` is the question `_spawn` asks in a loop, and a miss there is forgiven, which is why it never showed.
- Busy: nothing lost, the longest answer of any kind 0.42 s. Cold cache: nothing lost, first answers at most 0.47 s. So neither a busy machine nor an emptied disk cache makes the long first start; a machine that has never started the player does.
- Screenshots: 8,839, the longest 0.211 s (quiet 0.083, busy 0.172, cold 0.211).

**The same in the ordinary runs** of the branch (runs 37750527948, 37750543853, 37752099514, and round 2 below): the watch's table names the first answer of the first GPU player of each job. Nineteen fresh runners in all: 0.22, 0.23, 0.31, 0.93, 0.95, 1.10, 1.15, 1.27, 1.29, 1.39, 1.59, 1.76, 1.95, 2.78, 2.81, 2.81, 2.81, 3.31 and 5.04 s. Six of nineteen were over the client's 2 s. Every later start in the same job answered within half a second. The times bunch at 2.81 and 3.31; what mpv waits for there is not known (no start that hit it had the player's log on).

**So the second failure is explained.** A player that this process has just started answers nothing for up to several seconds while it makes its window, at the first start on a machine. Usually that moment swallows the first `pid` question, where a miss is forgiven. On 2026-10-05 the first question was answered before it began and the next request, a plain property set, was lost. That last step is read from that log (its place: three seconds into the first test of the job), not seen again. The deaf moment itself was seen over 2 s on six runners, five times with its report.

**The fix** (`pvj/player.py`, the IPC client that everything uses; small). `Ipc` has `patient_until`; `Player._spawn` sets it to 15 s from the start, and until then a request waits up to 10 s for its answer (`START_GRACE`, `START_WAIT`, with the numbers above in a comment). At any other time, and for a player this process did not start (the box, where systemd starts it), the wait is the 2 s it was. Why not a longer wait everywhere: a player that says nothing for 2 s in the middle of a show is a fault the panel should hear of at once, and the measurements say that outside the start no answer took a second (the longest 0.87 s, under twice the load). Test: `tests/test_player.py` `DeafAtTheStartTest`, a stand-in mpv that answers the first question and is then deaf for a second; no mpv and no GPU. With the old wait it fails with "no reply from mpv", the message CI printed.

**Round 2** (push run 37754821097; jobs 113236668967, 113236669046, 113236669144; about 59 runner-minutes), with the fix, for the screenshot. The generators' tests only, with a test-only switch that put back the `fbo-format` set at every value change, as the code was on 2026-10-05: quiet, then with two spinning processes per processor (8). 92 batches, 451 tests, none failed, no report. On that path 2,502 screenshots quiet (longest 0.066 s) and 1,836 under the load (longest 0.195 s). And the fix on three more fresh runners: first answers of 1.39, 1.95 and 2.78 s, the last of which the client now waited for.

**The first failure is not explained.** About 34,000 screenshots in all the runs of this branch, on three ways of drawing, quiet, busy, twice as busy, cold, and on the old code path: the longest 0.211 s, a tenth of the wait. So it was not "a screenshot is slow on a software GPU", and it was not made to happen. Not ruled out: a rare stall of Mesa or xvfb, or of the runner itself for two seconds. The code path it ran on is gone since #92. If it comes again, the report is in the job's log.

**Not done, and what I am unsure of.**

- What mpv does in the deaf moment at its first start. One run with `PVJ_MPV_LOG=1` on the first start of a fresh runner would say; the report prints the end of that log.
- Whether a box has such a moment at boot (the panel's first requests to a player that systemd has just started) is not known and not claimed. The fix does not reach that case: the panel did not start that player.
- The new tests in `tests/test_player.py` take about 8 s on the Mac (its `_alive` cannot see a finished process without `/proc`). On Linux they should be quick; not measured.
- A failed `effects-gpu (OpenGL ES)` in the pull request's run 37752099514 (job 113227559444) was the slow package mirror again ("Install mpv, a virtual display and Mesa" timed out after 10 minutes); nothing of ours had run.
- The watch stands in for a module inside `pvj.player` while a GPU test runs. It is undone at the end of each test and a test checks that, but it is a hook into the product from the tests, and a change to how `Ipc.request` makes its socket has to keep it in mind.
