## 2026-10-08 (a third, narrow read of the fixes, and the first run of the browser test on `controller-code`)

Four small things in the two-thread reader and the device count, each with a test written first and seen to fail (`tests/test_midi_threads.py`: `ThirdPass`, `AfterALoss`, `Reserve`).

1. **Medium: an error swallowed on a release left the hold armed**, so a later tap could make a code. A real message that fails is now followed by "lost", and the hub forgets what is held and which pads are down.
2. **Low to medium: the worker's test-then-clear of the lost flag could wipe out a newer loss.** Only the reader touches the flag now; when nothing else comes it puts the marker itself as soon as there is room.
3. **Low: a full access code could pair device 201.** A device paired from a controller also needs the list as a whole to have room.
4. **Low: a scan could open a device again before the old reader had closed it.** A path whose input was halted in a pass gets its new one in the next pass.

A side effect of the second pass is undone: on "lost" the hub forgets which controls are down only for what sends notes. A knob or fader mapped to a trigger rests at a value, and forgetting that fired the trigger again at its next step.

**The browser test ran in CI for the first time with this branch and failed**, rightly: on a laptop People and codes is a grid of two columns, and the new card pushed the devices card under the first one. The page now puts "Let someone in" and "A code from a controller" in the left column and the devices beside both (one rule, for every look; a phone keeps the order access, controller code, devices). The test's own assertion is unchanged and has new ones beside it. Not run here: only `node --check`.

**Seen and left.** Guests and presenters can pass 200 devices in all: their rule counts only the 180 shared places, not the total, so 160 of them beside 40 PIN devices still admit one more. That is so on master with this feature off, and changing it is a decision of its own.

- The second run of the browser test passed the layout step and failed at its last check, "console problems": the new step asks the box to end a code when none is showing, the box answered 404, and a browser writes every 404 to its console, which the test watches for missing files. Ending a code that is no longer there now answers 409, as the API does elsewhere when the state has moved on; the 404 watch is as it was. (The coordinator, from the CI log.)

## 2026-10-07, later still (a second review, of the fixes: two medium, three low, all fixed on `controller-code`)

The two-thread reader from the first review brought faults of its own. The tests are in `tests/test_midi_threads.py` and run the real threads on a pipe; each was written first and seen to fail before its fix.

1. **Medium, every box with MIDI on: a worker that died left the controller deaf.** An exception from a handler (the API catches only its own errors, so a failed save can come through) ended the worker; the reader lived on, `alive` looked only at the reader, and the scan never replaced the input. Now one bad message ends only itself (`MidiInput._hand`, one log line in ten seconds with the kind of error), and `alive` needs both threads. Tests: `WorkerTest`, `HubAndWorker`.
2. **Medium, with the feature on: "messages were lost" was handled before the older messages still queued**, so a tap after an overflow could be timed from an old press and make a code. The marker now travels in the queue, at the place of the loss, and on it the hub forgets which controls are down as well as what is held. Test: `LostInOrder`, with a queue of four that really fills; before the fix it made a code.
3. **Low: the PIN's 20 places could be taken in one order** (20 full access codes first, then 180 guests). A full-access device paired from a controller now counts with the guests and presenters for all three. Tests: `Reserve`, both orders.
4. **Low: a slow fader flush on a tick forgot a real hold.** Ticks from an input that stamps carry a time, and count as stamped. Tests: `TickFlush`.
5. **Low: stopping waited up to 6 seconds a controller, one after another.** All inputs are told first, then waited for against one deadline (`stop_inputs`, 3 seconds in all). Test: `StopTest`.

Also from the reviewer's list: a fast sweep of 1500 messages arrives whole and in order through the two threads, and stop, unplug and start again leave no thread behind (`OrderTest`). Not run: the browser test, the Pi, a real controller.

## 2026-10-07, later (the independent review of the controller code: two medium, eight low, all fixed on `controller-code`)

Nothing high. Each finding and what was done; the tests are in `tests/test_controller_code_review.py`, one class per finding. They were written with the fixes, from the reviewer's sequences; they were not each run against the code before its fix.

1. **Medium: a tap could count as a hold when the thread that handles MIDI was waiting for the player.** `MidiInput` now has a thread that only reads and stamps each message with the clock, and a second that hands them on; the hold is timed from the stamps (`plan(..., at)`). For a caller with no stamps, calls slower than half a second end what is held. A full queue ends it too. Tests: `ReadTime`.
2. **Medium/low: the full access switch could come back by itself** from a hand-edited `{"enabled": false, "owner": true}`. One function, `auth.controller_setting`, now says what the section means; `validate`, the diagnostics file and the start of the box (which rewrites the section) all use it. Tests: `OwnerSwitch`.
3. **Low: the look at the display and the screenshot were two steps.** Decided under the preview lock now, looked at again after the picture, retaken without text for a lesser device if something appeared; and a kept picture with text is not given to a lesser device unless nothing secret was up around it. Tests: `Snapshot`.
4. **Low: a release swallowed by Learn left the control held.** What is held is forgotten when Learn starts, ends or captures, when the map is saved, and when a MIDI switch changes. Tests: `LearnAndTheMap`.
5. **Low: the lockout denies the code to the person at the box.** The lockout is untouched. The display says "Pairing is locked for N minutes after wrong guesses": instead of a code when the lock outlasts one, under the code otherwise. In `pvj/MIDI.md`. Tests: `Lockout`.
6. **Low: the press that hides a code runs before the USB check and without the setting.** Left so, and said in the docstring of `MidiHub._local`; `pvj/MIDI.md` now says that a USB id proves a USB MIDI interface, not a person.
7. **Low: a fader could carry the action through a personal mapping.** A controller number counts only as 127 down and 0 up with nothing between. Not "notes only": that would shut out every button of a nanoKONTROL2. Tests: `OnlyAButton`.
8. **Low: devices paired this way were never pruned.** A presenter paired so goes after 7 unused days (a presenter from a join code does not expire, so there was nothing to match; the guest rule was used). A full-access device paired so cannot take the PIN's 20 places. Tests: `Devices`.
9. **Low: the code was used up before its device was added.** Now after. A failed save no longer leaves a device in memory only. Tests: `Devices` (a race of two requests, a list that filled, a device that slipped in, a full disk).
10. **Low leftovers.** `Player.overlay_remove` removes the QR code's file, also when the player is silent; `clear_qr` remembers a failure and each tick tries again; the diagnostics file's scrub list has the active code. Tests: `Leftovers`, `Diagnostics`.

Also added from the reviewer's list: MIDI switched off in the middle of a hold, two controllers holding at once (one code at a time, the later replaces the earlier, both counted), the QR code drawn again after 15 seconds for a restarted player, and the `with_text` a lesser device's screenshot is really taken with. One word changed on the display: the comma in "Scan the QR code, or type it" is gone, since a comma is not in the display's set of characters. Still not run: the browser test, anything on the Pi, any real controller.

## 2026-10-07 (a pairing code on the display from a MIDI controller, D61; branch `controller-code`, not pushed, not merged)

- The owner asked for "a way to pull up a join code or owner code from a controller if i dont have a paired device with me". Built: two MIDI actions (`code_join`, `code_owner`) that make the box draw a one-time code on its own display for 2 minutes; a box setting with two switches, off by default, on System > People and codes ("A code from a controller"), where a paired owner also sees that a code is showing and can end it. D61 has every choice and its cost; `pvj/MIDI.md` ("A pairing code on the display") has the rules as a person meets them.
- The gesture is a hold of 3 to 10 seconds that ends with letting go. A stuck note, a tap and something left on a pad ask for nothing. The Launchpad Mini's one spare pad has the presenter code; the other two layouts had no button to give.
- Where the code is: one slot in `pvj/auth.py`, apart from the join codes; `pvj/controllercode.py` holds the rules; `pvj/pinscreen.py` draws it while `auth` says it is active; `pvj/midi.py` has the hold and hands the request to the manager without the API (`MidiHub._local`). Settings schema 14.
- Tests: `tests/test_controller_code.py`, 44 tests on a fake clock with a fake player. The unit tests were run on a Mac: 1491 tests, 15 failures, 15 errors, 89 skipped. One failure is this branch's and is open: `tests/test_effects.py`, `test_the_three_shipped_layouts_have_effect_controls_on_what_was_spare`, asserts that every layout still has a spare control, and the Launchpad Mini now has none (its last spare pad shows the presenter code). The other 29 are the Mac's (`mv -T`, `tar --sort=name`, no `SO_PEERCRED`) in `test_update`, `test_install`, `test_release` and `test_netd`, files this branch does not touch. **Not run: the browser test** (no Playwright here); a step for the new card was added to `tests/ui/panel.test.js` and one line to `tests/ui/signal-pages.js`, both only syntax-checked. **Nothing ran on the Pi or with a real controller**; `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` has a list, C1 to C10.
- Open: an independent review before merging, since this is a new way to get access (files: `pvj/auth.py`, `pvj/controllercode.py`, `pvj/pinscreen.py`, `pvj/midi.py` from `HOLD_MIN` to `MidiHub._local`, `pvj/api.py` `set_controller_code` and `_access_state`, `pvj/boxcare.py` `NEVER`, the tests). The state "a code is on the display" of the new card is in no picture, because only a controller can make it. Seen in passing and left alone: `Auth._new_pin` replaces the whole `auth` section of the settings (LESSONS).

## 2026-10-07: six known small faults read again; four fixed, two were already fixed (#92)

Pull request #92, branch `small-faults`. **Not merged** (the brief said so). **Nothing here ran on hardware**, in a browser or on a GPU: this Mac has no mpv, so every test of these changes is with a fake player, and the two GPU checks ran only in CI. Each fault was confirmed from the code before it was touched.

- **A fade takes the seconds asked for.** `Fader.ramp` slept `seconds / steps` after each step on top of what the step cost (2 seconds became 2.6 on the Pi 4 on 2026-10-05). It now sleeps what is left of each step by the clock, as Vibes' own fade has since its review, and takes a clock and a sleep so the test needs no real time (`FaderTest` in `tests/test_transport.py`). A fade whose steps cost more than their share runs as fast as the player allows and still sets every level.
- **A generator's value change leaves `fbo-format` alone.** `Player.swap_source` set it at every exchange of the text. It is set only when a source comes or goes: the bare carrier, a source again, a player that was restarted (`tests/test_player_layers.py`). `Player.play_source`, which Vibes uses for its next shader, still sets it each time; not touched.
- **With nothing playing, a panel's Next and effect button say so** (D55 amended). The answer depends on who calls: a paired device's request for Next, Previous, the one button's On or a preset of another effect asks the player first and gets 409 with the reason; a call the box makes for a controller (the devices `midi`, `osc`, `dmx`, `room`) asks nothing and hears "ok" as before. The test that holds the player's lock and counts a controller's questions is unchanged; a new one in `tests/test_effects.py` (`RolesTest`) covers both sides. The panel already greyed these buttons out with nothing playing, so this is for a state a few seconds old and for a script. A preset of another effect was not in the fault as written; it had the same answer and got the same cure.
- **A refused generator names the right line on GLSL 1.40.** The rule was read in the specifications before anything was changed: GLSL 1.40, section 3.3, "as if it is compiling at line number line+1"; GLSL 3.30, 3.3, "line number line"; GLSL ES 1.00, 3.4, "the following line has line number line". So `#if defined(GL_ES) || __VERSION__ >= 330`, which an effect's text already had, is the specification's own rule, and the generator's text has the same five lines now. Tests: the text (`tests/test_shaders.py`), and a refused generator's line in `tests/test_shaderlive_gpu.py`, the file CI also runs with Mesa as a GLSL 1.40 driver. The older check of that line (`tests/test_shaders_gpu.py`) never ran on GLSL 1.40, which is how the fault lasted.
- **Already fixed, nothing changed:** the Updates card after `pvj-update rollback` from a terminal (fixed in #62: the terminal command writes the result file and the API drops a "done" line for a version that is not running; `test_a_rollback_from_a_terminal_replaces_the_last_update_line`; the journal line that reports it is older than that entry). And the guard over a clip shorter than its six seconds (fixed in #87: `Guard.sample` adds the rises up across a loop; `test_a_clip_shorter_than_the_guards_window_can_be_called_heavy`). HANDOFF still listed the second as open; it no longer does.
- **Seen and not touched:** the full-screen fade of a Mix transition set to Dip gives each half `duration / 2` to the same `Fader`, so those were a third long too and are right now by the same change, untested by themselves. A clip of under about nine seconds that does not loop can still never be called heavy (three seconds to settle, six to judge), which is the rule and not a fault. The update result file is only written where its folder exists, so a terminal rollback on a box whose update units never ran relies on the API's version comparison alone (tested).

## 2026-10-07, later (the NDI input: the real library on a Mac, the licence read, and the review's thirteen findings)

Branch `ndi-input`, draft pull request #94 (pushed by the coordinator; this session commits locally only). **Not merged; a draft until the owner decides about the licence.** Nothing ran on the Pi, on Linux or with mpv.

- **The code met the real library, on macOS only** (run by the coordinator against the owner's NDI Tools; not the Pi, not Linux, not mpv). It loaded `libndi.dylib` 6.3.2, found two senders on the same machine, and received from both. Scan Converter: 3360 x 1890 UYVY, stride 6720, 30000/1000, progressive; 155 frames in 6 s straight from the library (middle gap 39.1 ms, worst 55.0 ms, none dropped by it); through `Receiver` into a pipe with a Python reader, 23.6 whole frames a second over 10 s (300 MB/s) at about 56 percent of one core, closed cleanly. Test Patterns: 1920 x 1080 UYVY, 30000/1001, **one frame and then none** (a still pattern with a tone, the owner confirms). So the structure layouts and constants are right for 64-bit macOS; nothing is known for Linux or aarch64. Figures are in `pvj/NDI.md`.
- **The licence was read** (the agreement the Linux SDK's installer shows; not accepted, installer not run, nothing of NDI's on the Pi). Recorded as a summary by section in `pvj/NDI.md`, without the text, which the agreement treats as confidential. The point that matters: its free licence covers general purpose computers and names, among what it does not cover, embedded devices running Linux with a fixed function. **Whether a Pi running this image may use the library is an open legal question for the owner.** The NDI page, `pvj-ndi-runtime` and the manual now say so where the install steps are.
- **The independent review found nothing high and thirteen things, each fixed in its own commit with tests** (`tests/test_ndi.py`, `tests/test_units.py`):
  1. A taken-over helper could hold the panel: the timeout in `netd.exchange` was per read, so a reply sent a byte at a time lasted 65536 times as long. One deadline for the whole exchange now (shared with the other helpers' clients, whose tests pass). A reply of 60000 "[" raises RecursionError on Python 3.9 to 3.12, which is not a ValueError: refused by a nesting bound before it is read, and caught. `server.build()` no longer calls `sync()` bare, and `sync()` itself never raises.
  2. A frame of two woven fields (format 0) was accepted and would have shown combed, while the document said interlaced was refused. From the SDK's NDI-RECEIVE page: the "fastest" format always allows fields, so the library does not de-interlace in it. All three interlaced types are refused alike now; the flag and each value are pinned. An existing test had written the bug down (it asserted that format 0 passes).
  3. `"ndi": []` in a hand-edited settings file raised out of `sync()` and stopped the panel. `Wanted` reads the section without ever raising, leaves bad entries out, logs once, and the page says what was left out. A refusal by the helper is no longer shown as "not running".
  4. Sixty-four fake announcers could hide the real sender (cut first, sorted after). Sorted before the cut now, with the source on screen and senders at added addresses kept, 2048 rows looked at, and a line on the page when the list was cut. The id is made from the name and the announcing machine.
  5. `pvj-ndi-runtime install`: O_NOFOLLOW and O_NONBLOCK, fstat on the descriptor, a read bounded at the limit plus one byte, the copy 0600 until every check has passed.
  6. A status over 64 KB made the helper look dead (64 long emoji names: 96,799 bytes). A name is also bounded as the answer writes it (768 bytes) and an address at 100; the worst status is built in a test and fits.
  7. The capture thread span on repeated "lost". It waits a quarter second and logs the drop and the return once each.
  8. A clip could still fall between "nothing else was played" and loading the pipe. The check and the load are one step under one lock, and `_player_call` cancels a connecting source before every `play`, `play_pipe` and `clear`.
  9. The watch tried a gone source every 3 s for ever and logged each time (28,800 lines a day). It backs off to once a minute, logs a reason once, and lets go of a source that ended by itself (the page keeps why). With it: a still picture is "still", not "waiting", and is never reloaded.
  10. The unit: MemoryMax 512M, TasksMax 64, CPUQuota 300% (three of a Pi 4's cores; reasoned, not measured), a bounded private `/tmp` in place of PrivateTmp, HOME there, no localhost. The system bus stays, with the reason in the unit (avahi's client library uses it). Switched off after being on, the helper exits and comes back without the library. Uninstall says what stays; `--purge` removes `/opt/pvj-ndi`.
  11. The panel checks the path is a pipe owned by the helper's account before the player is told to read it (a look that can be raced; said in the comment). mpv's source opens a file for reading without blocking and its wait can be interrupted, so a silent helper does not hold a current mpv; mpv 0.35 was not determined: step N11.
  12. Only the Service removes the pipe (and wakes a reader waiting on it); the receiver is read under its own lock in `status()`; a connection is never freed while the capture thread may be inside the library (left open and logged if it will not return); a failed `mkfifo` is an answer.
  13. Names are written one way (NFC, every space a plain one, no pile of accents), and the helper's words are scrubbed of control and direction characters before they are cut.
- **A fix that was itself wrong, caught by a second read before the report.** The bound on nesting in fix 1 counted brackets inside strings, and "[" is allowed in a name: one sender named with 65 of them, or 64 named "Cam [", would have made every answer of the helper a bad answer and the input look dead. The count skips strings now, and a real status with brackets in every name goes through the real client in a test. The earlier tests had never put a status through `Client.request`. The same read tightened the cancel in `_player_call` to the player's own methods, and checked that the fader's cancel cannot deadlock with it (it joins nothing and calls its callback outside its lock).
- **The real-library run was made at 8d4797e**, before these changes; it should be run again at the head of the branch, with the senders' address strings written down.
- **Found on the way.** Hidden characters meant as escapes had landed in `tests/test_ndi.py` as the characters themselves (a bidirectional override among them); they are escapes again, and a test keeps both NDI source files ASCII.
- **Not done, on purpose.** `/api/ndi` through the support tunnel is as it was (the owner is being asked). The settings schema stayed 14 and the decision D61 until #93 had merged; see the entry of 2026-10-08 above for the move to 15 and D62.
- **Still not run.** The installer (its tests need GNU `mv`), every browser step, anything on Linux, anything with mpv.

## 2026-10-07 (the NDI input, built against a fake library; nothing of it has received a frame)

Branch `ndi-input`, commits local only (a push from the session was refused by the permission system). **Not merged, not reviewed.** It takes input from the network through a closed-source library, so it needs an independent read-only review before it merges. Nothing ran on hardware; the dev Mac has no NDI runtime, no mpv, no Playwright and no sender, and the test Pi was not touched.

- **Why.** The owner asked that "the NDI playback module" run smoothly. It did not exist: only `pvj/modules.d/inputs-ndi.json`, marked planned. This session built the receive path as far as it can be built and tested without the runtime, and wrote down what is left. "Smooth" is unproven.
- **The design (D62, written as D61 at the time; `pvj/NDI.md`).** A new service `pvj-ndi` under its own account and group (not group `pvj`), sandboxed, with its network held to private, link-local and multicast addresses. It loads the owner's `libndi.so.6` with `ctypes`, lists senders, and writes whole UYVY frames into a pipe in `/run/pvj-ndi` that the one mpv plays with the raw video demuxer, as the capture input does. The panel asks it over a socket (the small server from `netd.py`), plays by an id made from the checked name, and loads the pipe again when a sender changes size or rate.
- **The licence.** Read from NDI's documentation pages, quoted in `pvj/NDI.md`. No NDI file is in the repository or the image. NDI publishes no separate Linux runtime (the header's download address for Linux is empty), so the runtime comes from the SDK, which a person must download and accept. `pvj-ndi-runtime` (root) copies it to `/opt/pvj-ndi` after checking the file's processor type. There is no upload route and no automatic fetch. The page, the manual and About carry NDI®, the trademark sentence and the link to ndi.video. **Not read: the SDK's License Agreement and its list of exclusions**, which are only inside the download; whether this box may use the royalty-free SDK at a venue is for the owner to check.
- **Built.** `pvj/ndi.py` (the checks on names, addresses and frames; the ctypes layer behind a seam; `Receiver`, `Service`; the panel's `Client` and `Input`; the runtime command), `install/pvj-ndi.service`, `bin/pvj-ndi`, `bin/pvj-ndi-runtime`, the installer and image lines, `GET`/`POST /api/ndi`, `{"ndi": id}` on play, settings schema 15 (`ndi.addresses`; written as 14 at the time) with its migration and its check in the settings file, the System page "NDI® input" with its switch (off by default, beta), a format argument on `player.play_pipe`.
- **Tested, all against fakes** (`tests/test_ndi.py`, `tests/test_units.py`): hostile names (controls, a trailing newline, bidirectional overrides, bad UTF-8, 5000 sources), addresses, frame numbers refused before any byte is read, the copy never reading padding or the alpha plane, whole frames only under a slow reader, a size change, a rate change, a sender that goes and returns, a pipe nobody reads, a pipe swapped for a file, stop without a hang or a leftover thread, the helper's answers checked again by the panel, the reload losing a race with a clip, the structure layouts for 64-bit, the unit's sandbox line by line.
- **Found by a second read before the report.** A play of an NDI source that was still connecting (up to 6 seconds) took the screen back from a clip the operator had played meanwhile, and a stop sent in that time was lost after 2 seconds. A ticket taken before the connection and made worthless by every stop decides it now, and a close waits longer than the helper can be busy; the test holds the connection open on an event, plays a clip, and lets go. The page also drew its Play buttons again every 2 seconds; it now draws only when the list changed.
- **Found while building.** A row copy from a ctypes buffer failed ("lvalue and rvalue have different structures") because ctypes calls its byte format "<B"; the fake frames were plain bytes and did not show it, a test over a real ctypes buffer did. Frames counted before the player opened the pipe made "dropped" wrong; counting starts when the screen takes the pipe.
- **Not run.** The installer changes (its tests need GNU `mv`), the browser steps (written, `node --check` only), anything with mpv, anything with the library.
- **Not built.** Sound; the low-bandwidth stream; a source as a pad, in a scene or in the schedule; a way to put the runtime on a box without a keyboard; Health does not list the helper; the Signal look of the page was not looked at.
- **For the owner.** Read the NDI SDK's License Agreement; decide whether "NDI input" as a page name needs NDI's word; run "NDI input" in `tools/DEVICE-TESTING.md` on the Pi with Resolume or NDI Tools; say whether sound matters.
- **For the reviewer.** `pvj/ndi.py` whole; `install/pvj-ndi.service`; the account lines in `install/install.sh`; `play_ndi`, `set_ndi`, `ndi_tick` and `_stop_ndi` in `pvj/api.py`; `play_pipe` in `pvj/player.py`; `ndiCards` in `pvj/web/app.js`; `tests/test_ndi.py` and `NdiUnitTest`.

## 2026-10-05 (the Shaders page drew an answer that said the module was off)

Pull request #91. **Not merged** (the brief said so). Nothing ran on hardware or on a phone; the browser steps ran in CI's Chromium only (the dev Mac has no mpv, so the harness does not start there). The test Pi was not touched.

- **What was seen.** panel-ui failed once on #90 (run 37390838722, job 112035262763) with `pageerror: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'dwell')`, and in the same job the screenshot step said `signal: waited in vain for #roomletin #accesslive`.
- **Cause of the page error.** Not `data.config.dwell` (the box always sends `config`). `GET /api/shaders` answers 200 while the module is off, with `sets: []` and `active: null`, and the box is off from the first moment of `POST /api/modules/shaders`, which then still has work to do before it answers. The open Shaders page asks every few seconds; an answer in that gap was drawn, the set being edited was null, and the settings card read `e.dwell`.
- **Reproduction.** A step in `tests/ui/panel.test.js` holds the switch's answer back until the page has asked again, and then switches the module off from elsewhere. The first commit of the pull request is that step alone: panel-ui failed on it in both of its runs (jobs 112044201850 and 112044187529) with "the answer to the switch held back: the Shaders page drew an answer that had no set: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'dwell')".
- **Fix.** One question, `loaded(d)` in `shaders.js`, asked at every place an answer of `GET /api/shaders` is kept: the page (`draw`, and the set switch in the library), the strip on Live, the ambience control on Room (`room.js`). An answer that cannot be drawn clears the cards, says "Shaders and Vibes was switched off" and has the panel read the modules again (`moduleSaidOff` in `app.js`), so the page becomes the Off page. `countdown`, `toggleVibes`, `applyPreset` and the settings card no longer assume there is a state or a set; the System row's state line no longer assumes a list.
- **The second message was separate.** Another process (`tests/ui/screenshots.js`, its own harness and browser). A presenter lands on Room; the step tapped the Room tab and waited for the first wall, which the screen already had, then opened the fold; the tap's redraw (`goTab`: `loadAll().then(render)`) closed it. The failed log shows it: the fold's `GET /api/access` came before the new screen's `GET /api/room`, and the QR picture was never asked for. `roomUp` now waits for a wall on the redrawn screen. This one was read from the log and not reproduced on demand.
- **Open.** A person who taps a tab and opens a fold within the few tenths of a second before the redraw lands would see it close too. Not changed here.
- **Master moved.** #90 merged while this was open; master was merged in, both sides of the two log files kept, this entry on top as the later one.

## 2026-10-05 (a projector test whose set-up depended on timing)

Pull request #90. **Not merged** (the brief said so). A test change only; the product code is as it was. Nothing ran on hardware and the test Pi was not touched.

- **What failed.** `test_an_input_change_refused_as_the_projector_is_removed_or_switched_off_gets_no_retry` (added in #86) failed once in CI on a docs-only pull request (run 37383189733, test (3.12)), on its own set-up line: "the worker was still there, as the finding says".
- **Cause.** The test takes the projector out of the settings (or switches the module off) with no `apply()` and then asserts the worker still exists. The worker wakes every 50 ms in that test, sees its entry gone and leaves by itself (`_loop` breaks, `_after` takes it out of `_workers`). The assertion was true only until that wake-up.
- **Reproduced on demand.** With the worker woken right after the entry goes, and a wait until it has left, the old test failed with the CI message 20 times out of 20 (counted by exit code and by the message).
- **Fix.** The worker is held in `_after` (it has seen the entry gone and is on its way out) until the refusal has been handled, and the test waits, with a deadline, until it is there. So "ERR3 arrives, the entry is gone, the worker is present and not stopped" is the state every time, for both variants. The test also asserts the command went out once and that the projector, back again, has nothing pending.
- **The other order.** Two new tests: the worker left by itself before the refusal is handled, and `apply()` stopped it before then. `Monitor.set_input` raises the refusal in both; no gap in the product code.
- **Does the test still prove it?** With `cur is None` taken out of `set_input`, the held test failed 20 times out of 20 ("ProjectorError not raised"); the two new ones passed 10 times out of 10, as they should (`w is None` covers them).
- **Runs.** The three tests (both orders, both variants) 300 times in a loop on the dev Mac: 300 passed, none failed. CI is the judge for the rest.
- **Master moved.** #89 (the hand-off) merged while this was open; master was merged in, both journal entries kept, this one on top as the later one.

- HANDOFF.md has a new first section, "Read this first", because the development machine is moving from the Mac to an HP laptop (Windows with WSL). It lists what waits for the owner, the state of the Figma UI kit and what is still open.
- Figma's connector refused all calls on the Starter plan after about 27 calls in the month, writes included, although Figma's page says writes are exempt. The owner chose a local bridge (`figma-console-mcp` and its Desktop Bridge plugin) to go on.
- The Mac's 24 hour keep-awake ran out once during the day and was started again.

## 2026-10-05 (themes the owner can build and share: design tokens, add and save on the Look page, a converter for Figma)

Pull request #88. **Not merged, and it must not be before an independent security review: it adds a route that takes a file (`POST /api/theme/add`) and puts added themes into the settings file.** Nothing here ran on the Pi or on a phone; the browser step ran in CI's Chromium only. The test Pi was not touched. Two other pull requests were open, #86 (flaky tests) and #87 (effects pick their working size). When the first report was written master had not moved, so there was nothing to merge; #87 landed afterwards and master was merged in then (conflicts only in the three log files, both sides kept; D58 is #87's). Then #86 merged and took D59, master was merged in again the same way, and this decision became D60.

**What the owner asked for.** He is building the panel's UI in Figma to experiment: "Please make it so I may build out new 'themes' and layouts to try and maybe even share across devices." Layouts are designed in Figma and built by hand. This is the themes half: a theme he designs becomes a file, goes onto a box through the panel, and can be carried to another.

**Built (D60).**

- *Design tokens.* A theme may set, under `design`: `radius_control` and `radius_panel` (0 to 24), `border_width` (0 to 4), `density` (compact, regular, roomy), `control_height` (44 to 72), `control_height_large` (56 to 72), `title_case` (capitals, sentence), `title_weight` (400 to 900 by hundreds), `text_weight` (400, 500, 600), `font_title` and `font_text` (archivo, system), `font_number` (jetbrains-mono, archivo, system), `tabs` and `primary` (filled, outlined). Each is checked by type and range or against its list. The box writes them into `/theme.css` as `--tk-*` variables from numbers and its own tables.
- *Signal reads them.* About a hundred values in the Signal block now read `var(--tk-..., <the value that was there>)`. With none set the block is the block as it was: checked by putting every fallback back and comparing with master (371 lines, 0 different), and held by a unit test on the fallbacks. The built-in theme files are not changed. Primary actions got a class `pri` (29 buttons) so that "outlined" can be said for them and not for what is chosen.
- *Contrast.* `themes.pairings` computes every pairing a look draws; `validate` refuses under 4.5 to 1 with the pair named, and `warnings` says when a state colour is within 60 of an area colour. All six built-in themes pass.
- *Add, save, remove.* On System > Look: "Add a theme" (one file, 16 KB, read strictly: no key twice, whole numbers only), "Save this look as a file" (a bundled look becomes `my-<id>`, a Signal theme is written with every token), and Remove with the question in place (the look in use falls back to Dark stage first). At most 16; a bundled id cannot be taken; the same id replaces the owner's own. Kept as `<state>/addons/themes/<id>.json`, never through a link, written whole then renamed. `server.build` now gives the Api the add-on folder, which it never had. Full access; add and remove are in the tunnel's refusals and refuse by themselves too.
- *With the settings.* An export carries added themes under `themes` only when there are any; an import checks each with the same code, lets the look it names be one it brings, refuses the file whole if one theme fails, and writes them after the settings are saved; a factory reset removes them.
- *The Look page* shows each look as a small picture from its own tokens (page, surface, the area colours, a title in its font and case, a button, a chip), "yours" on an added one and "in use" on the one in use; two columns on a phone, as many as fit on a laptop; in both styles.
- *`tools/figma-theme.py`.* Nested design-token JSON (`$value` or `value`, collections and modes above the names, aliases) or a flat object of name to value. During the work the coordinator sent the real names of the Figma file "nxlx.mastercontrol Signal UI kit" (`colour/page`, `colour/on colour`, `area/live`, `state/error text`, `shape/corner` for both radii, `shape/rule`, `size/control big`, `font/words` for both text fonts, `font/numbers`, no variable for the case of titles); the converter takes those, the list first agreed and the code names (`bg`, `var(--ar-room)`), without regard to case, colour or color, spaces or hyphens. The case of titles, density, weights and filled or outlined are options. Unknown names are listed; spacing and type sizes are passed over. `tools/theme-samples` has an export shaped like the Figma file's (it converts to Signal exactly, which a test holds), a flat starter, and `soft-room.json`, a rounded, roomy, sentence-case theme with its own colours, to show the range.

**Tests.** `tests/test_themes.py` (tokens and every limit, hostile and malformed files, contrast, the stylesheet's fallbacks, storage and links), `tests/test_theme_api.py` (roles, the request header and Origin, every refusal and its words, the tunnel, save and add back, a damaged theme, settings round trip, a bad theme in a settings file, factory reset), `tests/test_figma_theme.py` (both sample exports, every documented name, value forms, modes, refusals). The browser test has a step that adds a refused theme (the contrast message is on the page), a theme with CSS in it, an oversized file, then a good theme that changes the radius, the case and two colours, sees it on Room and Live, saves it, and removes it; and the earlier steps now assert that no font is fetched before the Look page is opened.

**Decided on the way.** Large controls are 56 to 72, not 44 to 72: D57's floor for what staff press holds whatever a theme says. Title and text fonts are Archivo or the device's own; JetBrains Mono ships in one weight and is for numbers. Contrast is checked for hand-dropped add-on themes too. `state/ready` is not a token: Ready is the text colour. The decision is D60: #87 took D58 and #86 took D59 while this was open (it was written as D59 first).

**The checks, and runners that never came.** On the commit of the first report every job passed in at least one of its two runs (push and pull request), and seven jobs were red in the other with the same annotation: "The job was not acquired by Runner of type hosted even after multiple attempts". No job failed on a test. Two re-runs cleared four of them; three were still red when the report went out, and the report said so. A red check is read before it is believed either way.

**After the independent review (the same day).** No hole for CSS, a header, a path or access; four low findings and some notes, each fixed with a test. (1) An accent chosen under Look bypassed the contrast rule: `set_theme` and the import's `check_theme` only checked that it was a colour, so `dark-stage` with `#121214` was accepted at 1.0 to 1, and the panel's own swatches failed too (five of six on Light). Now `themes.accent_problems` refuses in both paths with the pair named, the page offers only the swatches that pass for the look in use and drops the accent when the next look cannot carry it, and a faint accent already in the settings is not used and is reported on the Look page. A test of mine had asserted 200 for a faint accent; it was wrong and is gone. (2) Two hand-placed files with one id: remove deleted one and the theme came back at the next start. The store tracks every file of an id, prefers the one the panel wrote, and removes all. (3) A skipped theme file was reported nowhere: one log line each and a `skipped` list for full access that the Look page shows. (4) The converter died with a traceback on nesting 5000 deep and on `1e999`, and printed names raw: all three end with return code 2 and no file, and what comes from the export is printed with unprintable characters replaced (not through `themes._show`, which would also remove the `/` of a name). Notes: removal takes the file first and changes the look after, so a failed unlink changes nothing; add and remove hold the lock an import and a reset raise their flag under, and are refused while one runs; the import reads each theme with `themes.checked`; a theme may not take a bundled look's name; guests and presenters no longer receive every look's tokens; eight adds at once at the limit leave sixteen.

**Left out, and weak.** No font family was added (the list is what ships; adding one means fetching and subsetting it, which was not cheap here and needs the network). The design tokens act in Signal only; a default-style theme can still only change colours. Density is one factor. The picture on the Look page is drawn by `app.js` from the tokens, not by the stylesheet, so it is a likeness (it does not show density or the tab bar). Outlined tabs and an outlined primary action were not looked at as pictures by anyone; only filled ones are in the browser step. The sample theme was validated and converted, never seen on a screen. Safari was not tried. A settings file with themes in it cannot be imported by a box older than this. The `tools/panel-playground` knows nothing of any of it. The Signal pictures in the `ui-screenshots` artifact should be compared by eye with the last run's before merging: the claim that they are unchanged rests on the substitution check and on the Signal pass of the browser test, except the Look page, which changed on purpose.

## 2026-10-05 (the test failures that come and go)

Pull request #86. **Not merged. Nothing here ran on the Pi. The browser test ran only in CI (no Playwright on this Mac).** Seven known intermittent failures were looked at against every red or cancelled run from 2026-10-03 20:39 to 2026-10-05 13:00 UTC (about 1300 runs listed; the failed and cancelled jobs of 174 runs and earlier attempts read: a run that was rerun to green shows its failure only in the earlier attempt). For each: how often it was seen, whether it was made to happen, the cause and how sure it is, and what was done.

| Failure | Seen | Made to happen | Cause | Done |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Browser test, mapper step: "Main stage" not in `#mapsets` in 30 s | 2 runs whose log is still there (master after #68, edit-and-guest-codes); more in older entries | Yes, in CI, every time: a new browser step holds the nudge's answer back 700 ms. On a commit with the step and no fix it failed with "cursor in nothing" (run 37317805315); with the fix it passes | Demonstrated. The answer to the nudge rebuilt the card between the moment the name field was chosen and the typing. The letters went nowhere, Save sent an empty name, the box answered 400. Both old logs end the same way: a POST to `/api/mapper` answered 400 about 35 ms after the nudge's answer, then 30 silent seconds | Fixed in the panel: a rebuilt mapping card gives the cursor, the text and the place in it back to the control that had them (`keepCursor`), and its canvas has its height at once. The step waits for what the panel shows, and the new step stays as the regression test |
| 2. `tests.test_netd` `test_disallowed_caller_is_refused_before_anything_is_read`: broken pipe | 6 runs (5 on Python 3.9, 1 on 3.12) | Yes, on the Mac: every time with the order forced (the CI traceback, line for line), and unforced 997 times in 12000 calls with the peer check stood in for | Demonstrated. The helper refuses and closes without reading. When it is quicker than the caller, the caller's write fails while the refusal is waiting to be read, and the client said "is not running" for "not allowed" | Fixed in the product: the clients of pvj-netd, pvj-sysd and pvj-supportd share one exchange that reads the answer before giving up on a failed write (0 wrong in 36000 calls after) |
| 3. `tests.test_projector` `test_an_input_change_asked_before_an_edit_goes_nowhere_after_it`: 'ERR3' != 'stopped' | 3 runs, all Python 3.9 (effects once, signal-style twice) | Yes, on the Mac, every time: a new test holds the old worker's status check open while the edit lands | Demonstrated. For a moment after an edit the projector has no worker (the old one's thread takes it over when its check ends), and `set_input` looked for the worker before it looked at the address | Fixed in the product: the address first. A removed projector still gets the refusal as it came |
| 4. The MIDI hub keeps a button as held when MIDI goes off between press and release (`tests.test_lights` `test_m1_...`) | 3 runs | Yes, on the Mac, every time: two new tests without any timing, and the lights test itself with every controller call made 0.3 s longer | Demonstrated. The release is dropped while MIDI is off, as it must be, and the hub never forgot the press | Fixed in the product: the hub forgets held buttons, pickup and armed guards when it stops listening and when a reader starts for a controller; a message that waited for the lock while MIDI went off is not acted on |
| 5a. Browser test, Shaders page: `#shadernow #detailhigh` not seen in 15 s | 1 run (effects) | Not in the browser. In the engine, every time, with the window forced | Inferred for that run, demonstrated as a defect. The step made three height changes within 160 ms, the last 26 ms after the answer to the one before, and the page then asked the box ten times in 15 s without showing the line. The panel's server answers each request on its own thread and logs a request when it answers: the requests that let the test go on were logged before the changes they waited for, so the changes overlapped. A settings change was two writes, each reading first, and only the second held the lock: a later change could be overwritten and the earlier one kept | The lost update is fixed in the engine (one read and one write under the lock) with a test. If the step times out again it prints the box's height, the chooser, where the cursor is and whether a question is open, which settles it |
| 5b. Browser test: a staff device does not see the painting wall "Warming up" in 15 s | 1 run (room-scenes, 2026-10-04, on a commit whose other run failed at another step) | No | Not found. The fake projector stays in warm-up until it is told otherwise, so it is not a matter of waiting longer | Instrumented only: the step's diagnostic from that day, and the new report of every open page |
| 6. A `test` job that sat in the unit tests for 35 minutes (controller-lights 43f991e) | 1 run. None since: 189 finished unit test steps, the longest 589 s, the median 519 s, no stack dump printed | No. 24 runs of that commit's lights tests on the Mac, four at a time, each 33 s | Not found. The job was cancelled by hand (its annotation: "The run was canceled by @anthonygiglio") and GitHub no longer has its log | Nothing new. The 20 minute limit with faulthandler stays as the trap |
| 7. Both `test` jobs "cancelled" in the GPU shader step after their unit tests had passed (said to be on the Signal branch) | On signal-style no run or earlier attempt has both `test` jobs cancelled. The nearest there: 2 runs with one job cancelled in the GPU step. In all the runs read, 37 have both `test` jobs cancelled, 10 of them in the GPU steps with the unit tests passed, and all 10 are on effects. 23 runs have one `test` job cancelled beside a failed one | Nothing to make happen: each job's annotation says what ended it | Read, not guessed. The two on signal-style: "The strategy configuration was canceled because test._3_9 failed", the matrix's fail-fast, and the job that failed was failure 3 both times. All ten on effects (one job of each read): "The run was canceled by @anthonygiglio", that is by a person or by a session using the owner's `gh`, each time minutes before the next push. No concurrency group, time limit or runner problem in any of them | `fail-fast: false` on the test matrix, so one Python's failure no longer costs the other's answer and log. Job names are unchanged. A cancel by hand stays what it is; this branch's own superseded runs were cancelled the same way and will read the same |

**How the mapper was found.** The server's request log is in every browser job's log, with times. The test's own waits ask the box, and the box knows a change a few milliseconds before its answer is back at the page (a handler changes the settings, saves them, then answers), so the test went on while the page was about to draw. Playwright's `fill` is two steps, choosing the field and then inserting the text, and the rebuild fell between them. A person on a slow link meets the same thing, and anyone nudging with the arrow keys lost the canvas after every step, so the card was fixed and not only the test.

**For the next failure in the browser test.** When a step fails, the test prints for every page it has open (the phone with full access and each staff, guest or presenter device): the screen and tab, the message line, an open question, where the cursor is, each element the failed step named with its text and its card, the whole screen's text, and the box's last 25 answers with what was sent and, for a refusal, what was answered. It printed exactly that on the commit made to fail.

**Seen while reading the red runs, not chased then.** `test_projectors_are_not_all_asked_at_once` once (0.295 s where more than 0.3 was wanted, on ux-proposal; it came again during this branch's reruns and is fixed, below). `#confirmno` detached before the click, and "R 5: Spare" not seen, twice each on development commits of the effects branch whose other run was cancelled; that code changed before it merged. `test_no_secret_is_in_it` (LESSONS, still open). The older `#mapsel "corner 1 of 4"` failure (last on 2026-10-03) has not come back since the mapping card numbers its requests. Everything else red in those two days failed the same way in both runs of its commit: work in progress, not chance.

**CI itself.** Every push to a branch with an open pull request starts each workflow twice, and with three sessions pushing, runs waited half an hour and more for a runner. The runs of this branch's superseded commits were cancelled by hand to make room, and so was the rest of the run made to fail once its browser job had answered. The count of green runs on the last commit is in the pull request, since an entry cannot hold the result of its own commit.

**One more, seen while counting the runs.** The whole of CI ran five times on commit 812ef38, all green (the push and the pull request run, then reruns). Then master moved, it was merged in, and on the merge commit a `panel-ui` job spent more than twenty minutes in "Install mpv and a browser", a step that takes 45 seconds: its log shows that runner fetching packages from the Ubuntu mirror at about 50 kB a second (5.8 MB in two minutes). Nothing of ours was running yet. Only the `test` jobs had a time limit, so such a job could have gone on for six hours. Every job of `pvj.yml` now has a limit, and every step that installs has ten minutes, so it fails with its log and a rerun lands on another runner. I cancelled that run; its log could still be read afterwards, which is not what was found for the job that hung on controller-lights.

**And two more, from the reruns themselves.** After a second merge of master (commit a060a23) the whole of CI ran six times: four green, two red. One red was the slow mirror again, ended this time by the new ten minute limit with "The action 'Install mpv and a browser' has timed out after 10 minutes" in place of a job that goes on. The other was two failures in one run, one on each Python, which is only readable because the two `test` jobs no longer cancel each other:

- `test_projectors_are_not_all_asked_at_once` on Python 3.12: "0.248 not greater than 0.3", seen once before at 0.295. Made to happen on the Mac every time by making the first projector's "who are you" 0.2 s slower. The test measured the gap between the two projectors' first checks, but a projector is asked who it is before its first check, so a slow runner narrowed the gap. It now measures what the claim is, that the second projector's first check comes no sooner than its 0.4 s after the checks were started, and it still fails against a monitor that does not stagger (0.009 s). The product is as it was.
- `tests.test_shaderlive_gpu` `DesktopGlLiveTest.test_time_goes_on_across_a_change_of_a_value_and_of_the_speed` on Python 3.9, in the GLSL 1.40 step: "no reply from mpv" to a screenshot, just after the speed was set to 0. The player's control socket waits 2 seconds for an answer. Seen once, not made to happen, not explained: whether the software GPU was only slow or a screenshot of a frozen picture can wait for a frame that never comes is not known. It is in the hand-off's list.

**After the independent review** (the helper clients' change found sound, nothing blocking, three low findings; each fixed with a test).

- **1. A retry kept for a projector that was gone.** My reordering in `Monitor.set_input` let a projector that was removed, or whose module was switched off, while it answered "unavailable" keep its input change for a retry: it is out of the settings before its worker is stopped, and I looked at the worker alone. The entry is read once under the lock now, and the refusal comes back as it is. The test takes the projector out, and switches the module off, without `apply()`; against the branch as it was it fails with "ProjectorError not raised".
- **2. `keepCursor` put typed text back by the element's id alone.** (a) The name and grid fields of the mapping card belong to the chosen surface: when another surface was chosen while one of them had the cursor, the rebuilt field showed the old text under the new surface, and Rename would have given it to the wrong one. A field now gets its text back only when the card built the new one from the same content as the old (`defaultValue`, or a list's marked option). (b) The set's name was emptied after the card had been drawn again, so a field that still had the cursor (a tapped button does not take it on Safari and iOS) showed the saved name again while a second Save sent an empty one: the very 400 this work set out to remove. It is emptied when the box has taken the Save and before the redraw, and that field's text is the card's own (`data-kept`). Two browser steps: Save with the cursor left in the field and two redraws after it, and a surface's name typed while a held-back answer chooses another surface.
- **3. The stagger test was too weak, and my suggested repair was wrong.** It passed a monitor that gave every projector the same delay, and one that delayed all but the first equally; "the first command of any kind" would have failed on the correct monitor, since a projector is asked who it is at once. It is three projectors that already have their details: the first checked before 0.4 s, the second not before 0.4, the third not before 0.8, each from the start. Run against four monitors on the Mac: no stagger, one delay for all, one delay for all but the first (each fails), the right one (passes).
- **The helper exchange.** After a failed write only a whole line that says `"ok": false` is taken, since nothing was asked and nothing else can be an answer; half a line, something that is no refusal, or an answer that claims success is the failed write's error. A read that fails is an error as before (the branch that pretended otherwise could never be taken).

**A fourth way for a run to be red without a test failing.** While these were pushed, GitHub cancelled jobs that had waited about half an hour in the queue: "The job was not acquired by Runner of type hosted even after multiple attempts". The run then shows as failed with its jobs "cancelled". It is in the job's annotation, like the other two kinds of cancel. The run of the test-only commit for finding 2 (e5088d4) ended that way twice before its browser job had started. So the two browser steps for finding 2 were **not seen failing in CI** without the fix; that they would fail is read from the code (the old `keepCursor` writes the old text into any field with the same id), not shown. With the fix the browser test passes (job 111946964319). The tests for findings 1 and 3 and for the exchange were each run against the code they must catch, on the Mac.

**Tests.** New: two for the held button (`tests/test_midi.py`), two for the helper clients (`tests/test_netd.py`, whose socket tests now also remove their temporary folders), one for the projector (`tests/test_projector.py`), one for the shader settings (`tests/test_shader_engine.py`), one browser step for the mapping card. Each failed before its fix, with the failure CI had printed where there was one. On this Mac only the touched modules were run.

**Not verified, and what I am unsure of.** Nobody has looked at the mapping card's fix in a real browser, and the panel code in it (`keepCursor`) has only the browser test. Failure 5a's cause in that one run is a fit of times, not an observation. Failures 5b and 6 are not explained. The helper clients were looped on macOS, where a write to a closed socket fails in one more way than on Linux (the fix takes any failure of the write). `pvj/netd.py`, `pvj/sysd.py` and `pvj/supportd.py` are the root helpers' clients: the change is in what the panel does with a failed write and nothing a helper does changed, but the rule here is an independent read of anything near them before merging, and that has not been done.
## 2026-10-05 (effect detail on the Pi 4: effects choose their own working size)

Pull request #87, D58. The owner, on reading the first effects run: "If running on a Pi4 or 3, let's auto-scale clips to 720p or 1080, whichever performs better overall." Built, measured on his test Pi 4 over SSH the same afternoon (15:46 to 17:24 UTC), and written down. Judged from the player's counters and six snapshots; **nobody watched the monitor.**

**The answer first.** On a Pi 4, Automatic works at **720 lines**, at **540 for isf-edge-blowout**, and at 540 for an upload. Over the 1080p clip every filter drops frames at full size; at 720 lines 36 of the 37 hold 30 frames a second with nothing dropped and isf-edge-blowout drops 1.6 a second; at 540 lines every one holds. So 720 is the best picture that holds, with one filter a step down. All 37 were then run at Automatic with the code as it is in the pull request: nothing dropped by any of them.

**What was built.**

- The on/off Half resolution is gone from the card. **Effect detail** is one setting for the box (`fx_detail` in the Shaders and Vibes section; `POST /api/effects/config`, full access; applied on the tap, also to the effect that is on; in a settings export and import; absent while the board's own default is in force): Automatic, 540 lines, 720 lines, Full.
- With a number the filter draws a picture whose **shorter side** is at most that many lines, by the hook's own `//!WIDTH` and `//!HEIGHT` in the player's arithmetic, relative to the picture it meets: one text for every clip, a clip at or below the cap left alone, a new clip right from its first frame. The code's `RENDERSIZE` does the same sum.
- **Amount 0** tells the player to leave the filter's pass out (`//!WHEN 0`): the clip at its own size, whatever the cap.
- `"half": true` is still taken (that effect at 540 lines at most); `false` follows the box.
- The card says what is in force ("Automatic: working at 720 lines for this 1080p clip."), that fine patterns look larger at a lower size, and for a heavy effect "lower Effect detail to 540 lines" while there is a lower one that would help.
- Automatic is a table by board (`AUTO`), a Pi 4's row made from the measured `PI4_720` by the rule in D58. Default: Automatic on a Pi 4 (and on a Pi 3 and a small board the box cannot name, at 540 lines, not measured; effects are still not offered on a Pi 3), Full on a Pi 5 and x86, where Automatic scales nothing (not measured).

**The box and the method.** The same Pi 4 Model B Rev 1.5, mpv 0.40.0, 2560 x 1440 at 75 Hz, `CRT.mp4` (H.264, 1920 x 1080, 30 a second) and `testpattern.mkv` (1280 x 720). Before anything, the installed files were compared with `origin/master` (`6f2a9e5`) by hash: the same, 192 files. A temporary full-access device ("claude-measure-temp") was paired with the PIN; a helper in a root-only folder under `/tmp` made every request and asked the player through its socket; each round ran detached and wrote its rows as it went, and a watchdog would have put the box back had the Mac gone silent for 45 minutes. The sound was turned to 0. Per filter: `POST /api/effects`, 4 seconds, a window of 15 seconds with no snapshot in it (45 more where it showed 0.05 to 2.5 dropped frames a second; 60 seconds for nine filters near the top in the last round), then Off. In a window: the rises of the player's two drop counters read every second and added up, the average of every pass read every second, GPU busy from the kernel, and at the end what `GET /api/effects` said (`load`, `working`). Before every window the script checked that its clip was still what played. The branch was installed three times with `install/install.sh --offline` (15:57, 16:30 and 16:57 UTC; each restarts the panel and the player for a few seconds; all five services were active after each).

**1. Before any code went on the box: is mixing at full size affordable?** A reviewer's design was two passes: the filter at the cap into a texture of its own, then a pass at the clip's size that mixes it with the untouched clip, so that the unfiltered share stays sharp. Texts made by the branch's translator on the Mac, the two-pass ones by hand, were given to the player through its socket while master was still installed (`fbo-format rgba8` set by hand, as the engine does):

| Text over the 1080p clip | Filter's pass ms | The mix pass ms | All other passes ms | Dropped a second | GPU busy % |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| the clip alone | | | 13.2 | 0 | 48 |
| isf-flip-h, full size | 8.9 | | 22.1 | 1.16 | 96 |
| isf-flip-h, 720 lines, one pass | 3.7 | | 19.5 | 0 | 77 |
| isf-flip-h, 720 lines, one pass, amount 0.5 | 4.4 | | 19.2 | 0 | 78 |
| isf-flip-h, 720 lines, two passes, amount 0.5 | 4.3 | 7.0 | 22.0 | 2.63 | 96 |
| isf-flip-h, 540 lines, one pass | 2.2 | | 18.2 | 0 | 68 |
| isf-flip-h, 540 lines, two passes, amount 0.5 | 2.7 | 6.9 | 22.1 | 1.75 | 96 |
| fx-vignette, 720 lines, one pass, amount 0.5 | 5.0 | | 19.0 | 0 | 80 |

A second pass at 1080 lines costs 6 to 7 ms and keeps everything after it at full size: with it the cheapest filter drops frames at both caps. **The mix stays where the filter draws.** The same probe showed that mpv 0.40 on V3D takes the size arithmetic, and that a hook left out with `//!WHEN 0` draws no pass but does not give back the player's own two passes (22.6 ms against 13.2).

**4. All 37 at Automatic.** Run twice: after the table was in the code, and again after the text changed (item 6 below), which is the code of the pull request. Both times: `working.lines` 720 for 36 filters and 540 for isf-edge-blowout, `auto` true, `load` "ok", **nothing dropped by any filter** (60 second windows for isf-edge-blowout, isf-corner-color-tint, fx-edge-glow, isf-rgb-halftone, isf-triple-rotate, isf-quad-tile, fx-kaleido, isf-kaleidoscope-tile and isf-kaleidoscope; 15 seconds for the rest). The pass times of the two rounds agree to within 0.1 ms. The GPU was 94 to 96 percent busy under fx-edge-glow and isf-corner-color-tint and 90 to 92 under six more (fx-kaleido, isf-kaleidoscope, isf-kaleidoscope-tile, isf-quad-tile, isf-rgb-halftone, isf-triple-rotate): they hold, with little room. The clip alone, fourteen windows through the afternoon: 13.0 to 13.2 ms for all passes, GPU 47 to 48 percent, nothing dropped. The board was at 37 to 55 degrees.
Also in both rounds: fx-vignette at amount 0 (no pass drawn, all passes 22.4 and 22.6 ms, GPU 75 and 76 percent, nothing dropped), at amount 0.5 (5.0 ms, nothing dropped), and isf-rgb-halftone with the old `"half": true` (`working.lines` 540, `auto` false, 4.9 and 5.0 ms, nothing dropped).

**5. The 720p clip at Automatic** (at the cap, so not scaled; run twice, like item 4): fx-edge-glow 9.7 and 9.8 ms, isf-rgb-halftone 8.6, fx-vignette 4.6 and 4.7, isf-flip-h 3.6, all other passes 14.0 to 14.8 ms, nothing dropped; fx-vignette at Full 4.6 and 4.7 ms with 14.2 and 14.8 for the rest.
